So an edit to any of those fields leaves the collection unpublishable until its diagrams are redrawn, while an edit to a history, a reference or a parameter's description leaves them standing.

### Which way the cones point

Every cone is a future cone, and each row names how its chart is oriented.
`tau` takes a time function, the chart's $t$ unless the row says otherwise.
A static chart's $t$ stops being a time beyond a horizon, and there the chart alone cannot say which way is future, so those rows take a family instead.
`ingoing` makes the ingoing rays future directed toward smaller $r$ everywhere, which agrees with $t$ outside and reads the region inside $r_s$ as the black hole, as the ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein chart does; Schwarzschild's spherical chart, the global monopole's static chart, Reissner-Nordstrom, Taub-NUT, Kerr and Kerr-Newman use it.
`outgoing` does the same with the outgoing rays toward larger $r$, for de Sitter's static chart and the Krasnikov tube, and in Misner's plane of $T$ and $\psi$, where $\partial_T$ is null and no chart time is timelike, with the family that tips over, toward larger $\psi$.
`split` is `ingoing` below the radius the row names in `split` and `outgoing` above it, for a static region between a black hole horizon and a cosmological one, where both agree with $t$: Schwarzschild-de Sitter's static chart and the charged black hole's in de Sitter space use it at the static radius, which reads the region inside $r_h$ as the black hole and the region beyond $r_c$ as the expanding universe.
`vector` takes the chart's time direction, for Godel, whose published $g^{tt}$ is positive and which has no time function at all.

### Declared inputs

An entry that leaves a function free cannot be drawn without a choice, and every such choice is written in its row and printed beside its diagram.
FRW's scale factor and Bianchi I's three are dust, solved from each entry's own published $G^i{}_i = 0$.
Morris-Thorne is drawn as its Ellis-Bronnikov member, $\Phi = 0$ and $b = b_0^2/r$.
Alcubierre is drawn with his own profile, which the entry names, at $v_s = 2$; Natario with the same profile and his zero expansion field, whose divergence the script checks before using it; the Krasnikov tube with a tube built by a ship at the speed of light.
Kasner is drawn at the exponents $(-2/7, 3/7, 6/7)$.
The Aichelburg-Sexl shock is drawn on the plane of $u$ and $v$ at $x = \rho_0/2$, $\rho_0/8$ and $\rho_0/32$, with $8GE/c^4 = \rho_0$, and its delta as a Gaussian pulse of width $0.05$ named in the row's `delta`, which `smoothed` puts in place of every Dirac delta and its derivatives; a pp-wave's profile may be any function of $u$, so the pulse is itself an exact solution, and every ray moving left jumps across it by exactly $\ln 2$, $\ln 8$ and $\ln 32$, which `--verify` checks.
Hotta and Tanaka's shock is drawn at $8GE/c^4 = a$ on two planes of each of its five charts, through the equator of the wave front and $15°$ from a null particle, $\rho = 2a$ and $a/4$ in the null cylindrical chart, with its delta as the same pulse; a ray crossing the shock jumps by $(2GE/c^4)(\cos\theta\ln\frac{1 + \cos\theta}{1 - \cos\theta} - 2)$ in the Kruskal $v$, $-a/2$ on the equator and $0.479\,a$ near the particle, each box is placed so that no cone stands within the pulse, and a view with a declared pulse marks no zero of $g^{rr}$, which inside the pulse takes the pulse's own sign.
Bonnor's beam of light is drawn at $\pi G\epsilon R^2/c^4 = 1/32$, his $m$, in units of the beam's radius $R$, so that $A = \rho^2/8R^2$ inside the beam and $(1 + 2\ln(\rho/R))/8$ outside it: the Cartesian chart's free profile is that uniform beam, on its axis and at $x = 3R$, the null Cartesian chart's is two such beams with their axes at $x = \pm 2R$, his section 6, midway between them and on the axis of one, and the null cylindrical charts are drawn at $\rho = R$, $2R$ and $4R$; every plane is drawn in $z$ and $ct$, its families named with the beam and against it, and `--verify` checks the rays against $u$ and $v + Au$.
Bonnor's stars of charged dust are drawn at $m = 1$: both spheres at $r_0 = 2m$ on the plane of $t$ and $r$, with the line through the centre of the sphere of 1975 and the exterior in the isotropic and the areal radius; the spheroid at $a = m$ and $u_0 = 1$ along its axis of symmetry, $\theta = \pi/2$, inside and out; and Lemos and Weinberg's cloud at $b = m/2$ in its own chart and as the declared $U$ of the harmonic chart, on the line through its centre. `--verify` checks each against $ct \pm \int U^2$ times the flat length, in closed form but on the spheroid's axis, where it is a quadrature.
The black holes of string theory with three and four charges are drawn on the plane of $t$ and the radius of each chart at the parameters `slices.SBC` names: $r_0 = 1$ with $r_i = 1/2$, $1$, $3/2$ and, in four dimensions, $2$; the extreme holes at $(1/2, 1, 2)$ and $(1/2, 1, 3/2, 2)$ in units of $r_2$; and the areal chart at $r_0 = 3r_q/4$; `--verify` holds every ray to $ct \mp r_*$ with `slices.sbc_rstar`, the poles of the tortoise coordinate plus a quadrature of its smooth rest.
Elliptic de Sitter space is drawn at $\ell = 1$: its global and conformal charts along the line through the observer, mirrored, a band whose two edges $\chi = \pi/2$ are one line with the time reversed, which each row marks as a `surface` line; the Kruskal chart on the half $V \ge U$, hatched by `where` beyond $UV = \pm 1$, with both horizons marked; and the static and planar charts as de Sitter's are.
The RP3 geon is drawn at $r_s = 1$: Kruskal's plane on the half $X \ge 0$, hatched by `where` beyond the singularities $T^2 - X^2 = 1$, with both horizons marked and its edge $X = 0$, which is glued to itself by the antipodal map of the sphere, marked as a `surface` line; Schwarzschild's chart of the one exterior; and the isotropic chart on its one sheet $\rho > r_s/4$.
The small universes are drawn as a cubic torus of dust in units of its period $L$, $a = (3ct/2L)^{2/3}$, on one cell with its edges one line and unrolled over six cells, with one galaxy at each of its places $x = (n + 3/10)L$ and our past light cone from $\eta = 3L$, which `marked` traces to the past alone; the closed hyperbolic forms and the horn hold open dust solved from their own $G^i{}_i = 0$ from $a = a_0$ and $a' = 6/5$, the first with the in-radius and out-radius of the Weeks manifold's cell and the second, at $b_2 = b_3 = 2\pi$, with the line beyond which an observer has seen once round the circle of $y$; `--verify` checks the rays against the conformal time of the parametric solution.
The tilted universes are drawn as Farnsworth's dust at $C = W$, in his parameter $\eta$ and, through the declared function `tilted_eta`, in the time $u$ of the surfaces of homogeneity, with the cones oriented by the velocity of the dust, and as the flat model in its own chart and on the inertial plane, where `_tilted_flow` draws the lines of matter and the hyperbolas of constant $u$; `tilted_null_tables` holds the null coordinates that are regular on the Cauchy horizon, which `--verify` and the conformal diagram both use.
Misner space is drawn at Li and Gott's $\psi_0 = 4\pi$, a boost of rapidity $2\pi$, in each of its three charts, every plane drawn unrolled with its periodic edges one line; Misner's own plane marks $g^{TT} = 0$, the chronology horizon.
Gott and Li's self-creating universe is drawn at $r_0 = 1$ and their $\beta = 2\pi r_0$ in each of its four charts, which `print_charts.py` writes: the static chart along a line through the observer and the Kantowski-Sachs plane, each over one period with its periodic edges one line; the steady state plane over one period of $\tau$, whose top edge is its bottom edge shrunk by $e^{-2\pi}$, with the Cauchy horizon $x = \pm r_0e^{-c\tau/r_0}$ marked; and the conformal plane below future infinity, with the horizon $\rho = -\eta$ marked and `where_is_infinity` set so that $\eta = 0$ is not marked as one. Its conformal diagram is drawn at $\beta = r_0$, as Misner's is at a boost of rapidity 1, and `_tools/derivations/self_creating_universe.md` is the derivation.
The universe from nothing is drawn at $\ell = 1$ on the plane of the time and $\chi$ of each of its four Lorentzian charts, from the waist upward: the closed slicing and the quadratic gauge to $ct = 3\ell$, the conformal chart up to future infinity, and the scale factor chart from its join $a = \ell$, where $g_{aa}$ diverges and the cones lie flat, to $a = 4\ell$, with `tau="a"`; the 4-sphere is Riemannian and has no light cones. `--verify` checks the rays against $\chi \pm \arctan\sinh(ct/\ell)$, $\chi \pm \mathrm{arcsec}(a/\ell)$, $\chi \pm \eta$ and $\chi \pm \arctan(ct/\ell)$, and `_tools/derivations/universe_from_nothing.md` is the derivation.
The Khan-Penrose spacetime is drawn where both waves have passed, at $L = 1$: the plane of $u$ and $v$ against $v - u$ and $u + v$, whose curvature singularity $u^2 + v^2 = 1$ `crunch` finds, and the plane of $\tau$ and $\sigma$.
Belinski and Zakharov's wave of two solitons is drawn at $w = 1$ and $\cosh\beta = 5/4$ in both of its charts: the plane of $\tau$ and $\xi$ of the pole chart, and the plane of $t$ and $z$ of the canonical chart, whose free $f$, $P$ and $Q$ the row declares as the wave's, `_bz_wave`, with the root inside a root written out, checked to solve the published Einstein tensor; each marks the rays through the event $t = 0$, $z = 0$, which the two pulses run along, and `--verify` checks the rays against $\tau \pm \xi$ and $ct \pm z$.
The Bell-Szekeres spacetime is drawn where both waves have passed, at $a = b = 1$, in each of its six charts; the regular, global and Kruskal-Szekeres planes are drawn on through the Killing-Cauchy horizon, which each marks as the two rays through the event where its branches cross, and the regular chart's plane is taken at $X = 0.99$, $Y = 0$, since $\eta = 0$ is the rim $X^2 + Y^2 = 1$ of that chart.
The Chandrasekhar-Xanthopoulos waves are drawn where both have passed, at $p = 3/5$ and $q = 4/5$, on the plane $x = y = 0$, in the chart of $\eta$ and $\mu$, the chart of $\psi$ and $\lambda$ and the Boyer-Lindquist chart, the last with $-r$ up and $\theta/\pi$ across, since $r$ is the time between Kerr's horizons and falls toward the future; the ingoing Kerr chart draws Kerr's principal null rays on the equator, through the horizon to the ring singularity. An ingoing principal ray of that chart is $-\partial_r$ with $r$ affine along it, so both terms of its geodesic equation vanish, and `principal_checks` measures a miss against the connection's own size where they do.
Beyond the wave fronts $|\sigma| = \tau$ the cosmological chart's formula describes no event of the spacetime and diverges on $\sigma = \pm\pi/2$, so that row sets `singular_where_claimed`, which judges each singular edge only on its part inside the published domains; every other row judges the whole edge, since Schwarzschild's $r = 0$ is a singularity of the spacetime although its spherical chart claims only $r > r_s$.
Penrose's impulsive wave is drawn for the string with $\beta = 1/2$, $k = 3/16$: the null chart on the planes $\rho = 1$ and $1/2$ against $(U + V)/2$ and $(V - U)/2$, the retarded chart against $u + r$ on the equator and $30°$ from the string, each plane hatched beyond the string, and the flat chart on either side of the front, hatched beyond it.
Visser's thin shell wormhole is drawn at $a = 5r_s/4$, inside the photon sphere, in both of its charts: through the throat in $t$ and $\ell$, where the throat is marked as the least areal radius, and on one side in Schwarzschild's $t$ and $r$ from the throat out, where the shell is marked on the chart's edge $r = a$.
Israel's shell of dust is drawn as the shell that falls from rest at infinity, $\mu = r_s/2$, in units of $r_s$, whose motion `_tools/derivations/israel_shell.py` writes in closed form in $s = \sqrt{1 + 8R}$: the plane of $T$ and $r$ inside the shell and the line through the centre, with the event horizon, the outgoing ray $cT - r = -7/3$; Schwarzschild's plane outside it; and the ingoing chart against $v - r$, where the shell crosses $r_s$ at $v = 0$ and reaches $r = 0$ at $v = 0.52$. Each row declares the shell's radius as a function of its chart's time, `ISRAEL_R_IN`, `israel_r_out` and `israel_r_adv`, and draws it with `curves`, a world line $r = R(x^0)$ drawn and named; `--verify` checks the rays against $cT \pm r$, $ct \pm r_*$, and $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, and `_tools/derivations/israel_shell.md` is the derivation.
The charged shell of dust is drawn at $r_q = 12/25$ and $\mu = 1/5$ in units of $r_s$, the shell that comes in from infinity, crosses $r_+ = 16/25$ and $r_- = 9/25$, turns round at $R = 119/375$ and leaves through the horizons of the next exterior; `_tools/derivations/charged_shell.py` holds its motion, in closed form inside the shell and by one quadrature for the advanced time outside it. Seven views: the plane of $T$ and $r$ inside the shell and the line through the centre, with the event horizon, the outgoing ray $cT - r = -1.167$, marked as `event`, and the Cauchy horizon, the ingoing ray $cT + r = 0.504$, marked as `past`; the static chart outside $r_+$ and, in a view of its own, inside $r_-$, where the shell turns at $t = 0$; the ingoing chart against $v - r$ and the outgoing chart against $u + r$; and the isotropic chart's plane of $t$ and $\rho$, which draws the balanced shell, $b = 0$ and $\mu = a$, at rest at $\epsilon = a$. Each row declares the shell's radius as a function of its chart's time, `charged_r_in`, `charged_r_out`, `charged_r_inside`, `charged_r_adv` and `charged_r_ret`; `--verify` checks the rays against $cT \pm r$, $ct \pm r_*$, $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, $u + 2r_*$ and $u$, and $ct \pm (\rho + 2\ln\rho - 1/\rho)$, and `_tools/derivations/charged_shell.md` is the derivation.
Ehlers and Kundt's A- and B-metrics are drawn at $b = 1$, sixteen views: A II on its static plane, beyond its horizon, in Kruskal's chart with the singularity the pair of hyperbolas $UV = -1$ at the sides, and on the inertial plane of $T$ and $X$ above the hyperbola $\sigma = b$, with $T^2 - X^2$ for its time function; B I on the planes of $\tau$ and $r$ of both its charts, on its static surface, on its de Sitter space with $\phi$ periodic, through the neck, and on the inertial plane outside the hyperbolas $r = b$, with $T/|X|$ for its time function; A III; B II along $r$, on its anti-de Sitter surface in both charts, and through its axis; and B III. `_ab_forms` holds every view to its null coordinates in closed form.
The T-models of Datt and Ruban are drawn in units of $r_s$ as one tube with $\epsilon = 1$ and $\mu = (2 + \tanh(r/r_s))/2\pi$, above $1/2\pi$ on every shell, on the comoving, Ruban and areal planes, the comoving chart's $a$ and $b$ declared from Ruban's solution through `dr_eta` and checked to solve the published $G^r{}_r = 0$ and $G^\theta{}_\theta = 0$; Ruban's T-model on de Sitter space with the same $\mu$ at $\ell = 1$, hatched below the curve $a = 0$, which the row declares in `where` and `singular_zero`; and Kruskal's plane outside a T-sphere, whose surface $V = U$ is the left edge, drawn by a row's `curves` with the word `signed`, since $V$ is negative on half of it.
Vaidya is drawn with a shell of null dust of mass $M$, $m$ jumping from $0$ to $M$ at $v = 0$ in the ingoing chart and its time reverse in the outgoing one, the shell the conformal diagram declares.
The Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff star is the polytrope the conformal diagram declares, solved by the `StarSolver` the two scripts share and checked against the published $G^\theta{}_\theta$, which its construction never uses.
The boson star is the heaviest ground state, of central amplitude $\sigma(0) = 0.271$, in units of the boson's reduced Compton wavelength, in its polar areal chart and its isotropic one: `boson_star.Star` in `_tools/derivations/boson_star.py` solves its two metric functions from the published $G^t{}_t$ and $G^r{}_r$ and the field's wave equation, by a power series about the centre and by shooting on the frequency from there out, and every drawing of it checks the published $G^\theta{}_\theta$, which the construction never uses; the star has no surface, so the sphere that holds 99% of its mass is marked, and `_tools/derivations/boson_star.md` is the derivation.
Kodama's kink is drawn at $f = 1.25$, in units of $1/\mu$, through the throat in the proper radial distance and on one side in Kodama's areal radius: `kodama_kink.Kink` in `_tools/derivations/kodama_kink.py` solves $h$ and $r$ from the published $G^t{}_t$ and $G^\theta{}_\theta$ and the field's wave equation, shooting on the radius of the throat from the throat out and joining Schwarzschild's exterior where the field has reached its vacuum, `kodama_kink.Areal` reads $\eta$ and $\alpha$ off it at the same sphere, and every drawing of it checks the published $G^R{}_R$, which the construction never uses; the areal view declares $r_0$ at the solver's value, and `_tools/derivations/kodama_kink.md` is the derivation.
The universe on a wall moving past a black hole is drawn for a wall with no matter beside its tension, in a flat space, past a black hole of $\mu = \ell^2$, in units of $\ell$: the wall moves as $a^2 = 2c\tau\ell$, its static time is $ct = a + r_* - \arctan a$ in closed form, and `_tools/derivations/brane_wall_universe.py` holds both. The static chart's plane of $t$ and $r$ declares the wall's radius at the static time through `bwu_a_static`, the root of that form, hatches $r > a$ by `where` and draws the wall with `curves`; the Gaussian normal chart's plane of $t$ and $w$ declares $a = \sqrt{2ct\ell}$, where $Q = 1$, and is hatched beyond $w_* = (\ell/2)\ln(1 + 4ct/\ell)$, where the lapse vanishes and the normals to the wall from neighbouring moments meet, which is drawn. `--verify` checks the static rays against $ct \pm r_*$.
Einstein's cluster is drawn in units of its Schwarzschild radius: the areal chart for a declared cluster with no surface, $e^{2\Phi} = 1 - r_s/\sqrt{r^2 + b^2}$ at $b = 2\,r_s$, `EC_CORE`, whose mass function the chart defines from $\Phi$; Einstein's own at $V = 1/2$, where $m = r/6$ and $R = 3\,r_s$, in the areal radius and in his isotropic one, $\sigma = 5 - 2\sqrt{6}$ and $\rho_0 = 2.47\,r_s$; and Florides's of the same mass and radius in the areal radius and in the polar angle of its three sphere, $\sin\chi_0 = 1/\sqrt{3}$; `_tools/derivations/einstein_cluster.md` is the derivation.
Bartnik and McKinnon's soliton is drawn in units of $\ell$ for the soliton whose Yang-Mills amplitude has one zero, `BM_INPUT`, as `_tools/derivations/bartnik_mckinnon.py` solves it: on its plane of the time and the radial coordinate in each of its four charts and on the line through the centre in the areal chart, each marking the sphere on which $w = 0$, $r = 1.5457\,\ell$. The charts leave their functions free, and each row declares them from the solver's as sympy functions that carry their own derivatives, `bm_m`, `bm_delta`, `bm_N`, `bm_k` and `bm_r` among `DECLARED_FUNCTIONS`, each taking the number of zeros for its second argument; the chart of Breitenlohner, Forgács and Maison is drawn with $\tau = \ln(\rho/\ell)$. `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp \xi$ with the tortoise coordinate the solver integrates, which the tracing never uses, `_tools/test_bartnik_mckinnon.py` holds the solver to the tabulated parameters and each chart's published Einstein tensor, with those functions, to the stress of the Yang-Mills field, and `_tools/derivations/bartnik_mckinnon.md` is the derivation.
Tolman-Bondi is drawn for marginally bound dust whose density at $t = 0$ falls as $1 - r^2/r_b^2$, checked to solve the published $G^r{}_r = 0$, and Oppenheimer-Snyder's collapse is released from rest at twice its Schwarzschild radius, as its conformal diagram declares, in both of its charts.
Szekeres's cloud is that cloud, marginally bound, $f = 0$, with the same $R$ and $M$, and with $S'/S = 2r(1 - r^2)/r_b^2$ inside $r_b$ and $S$ constant beyond, which keeps the density positive and the shells from crossing, since $S'/S < M'/3M$; it is drawn in the axisymmetric chart on the two halves of its axis of symmetry, $\theta = 0$ and $\theta = \pi$, which its light rays never leave, checked to solve the published $G^r{}_r = 0$, and the ray marked on each is the last along that half to reach infinity, $-0.81$ and $-0.12\,r_b$ at the centre.
The Malament-Hogarth toy is drawn for every conformal factor at once, since $\Omega^2$ drops out of the null condition, and checked with a declared factor that grows as $1/|ct|$ along the axis.
The Kantowski-Sachs comoving chart is drawn as dust at rest in the chart, $a$ and $b$ solved from the published $G^r{}_r = G^\theta{}_\theta = 0$ from rest at $a = 1$ and $b = b_0$, which `--verify` checks against $1 + \eta\tan\eta$ and $b_0\cos^2\eta$; its dust chart at $\kappa = 0$, and the inside of Schwarzschild's horizon with the future toward smaller $T$.
The cosmic string is drawn at the deficit its conformal diagram uses, $4G\mu/c^2 = 0.1$, Gödel at $\omega = 1$, and Kerr and Kerr-Newman's figures at the spins and charge of their flat views.
Som and Raychaudhuri's universe is drawn in units of $r_c = c/\Omega$: the cylinders of $t$ and $\phi$ at $r_c/2$ and $3r_c/2$ in the cylindrical chart, whose block of $t$ and $\phi$ is van Stockum's with $R = r_c$, so `--verify` checks the rays against the same slopes, $r(1 - r/r_c)$ and $-r(1 + r/r_c)$, and the plane of $t$ and $x$ in the Cartesian chart, where the rays run at 45° and light launched along one is turned into $y$.
Bonnor's rotating dust cloud is drawn in units of $a$, in both charts on its axis, which is flat, so the rays run at 45°, and on its plane $z = 0$ from the null circle $\rho = a$ outward with $\phi$ divided out; the cylindrical chart also draws the circles $\rho = a/2$ and $3a/2$ of that plane through time, as van Stockum's cylinders are drawn, with $\phi$ scaled by $\rho$. `--verify` checks the axis against $ct \pm z$, the plane against $ct \pm \rho_*$ with `_bonnor_plane`, the quadrature of $e^{a^4/16\rho^4}\sqrt{1 - a^4/\rho^4}$ from $a$, and the circles against $c\,dt = (a^2/\rho \pm \rho)\,d\phi$, and `_tools/derivations/bonnor_rotating_dust.md` is the derivation.
The C-metric is drawn at $\alpha m = 1/6$ and $C = 3/4$, on the two halves of its axis, where $\sin\theta$ kills $\Gamma^\theta{}_{tt}$ and $\Gamma^\theta{}_{rr}$; beyond each horizon its time function is $r$, piecewise, taking the future as Griffiths, Krtouš and Podolský's extensions do, the black hole inside $2m$ and the region to the future of the acceleration horizon beyond $1/\alpha$.
The Einstein-Rosen waves are drawn with the pulse of Weber, Wheeler, and Bonnor at $C = a$, checked to solve the published $G^t{}_t$, $G^t{}_\rho$, $G^\phi{}_\phi$ and $G^z{}_z = 0$; on the plane of $t$ and $\rho$ the metric is conformally flat, so the rays are the same for every wave.
The global monopole is drawn at $\Delta = 0.19$, so that $\sqrt{1 - \Delta} = 0.9$ as for the cosmic string, with Letelier's black hole at $r_s = 1$ on its static and Eddington-Finkelstein planes, its horizon at $r_s/(1 - \Delta) = 1.235\,r_s$, and the monopole with no mass at its centre on the Barriola-Vilenkin plane, where every ray runs at 45°.
The dilaton black hole is drawn at $r_d = r_s/2$, the charge $Q = M$, with its singularity $r = r_d$ the left edge of every plane: the Einstein metric on its static and Eddington-Finkelstein planes, and each string metric on its own plane of $t$ and $r$, where a conformal factor leaves the rays $ct \pm r_*$ of Schwarzschild's plane as they are, which `--verify` checks.
Tangherlini's black hole is drawn at $r_h = 1$ on its plane of the time and $r$, every angle held fixed, in each of its three charts of five dimensions and in the static chart of six; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$, $r_* = r + \tfrac{1}{2}\ln|(r - 1)/(r + 1)|$ in five dimensions and $r + \tfrac{1}{3}\ln|r - 1| - \tfrac{1}{6}\ln(r^2 + r + 1) - \arctan((2r + 1)/\sqrt{3})/\sqrt{3}$ in six.
Majumdar and Papapetrou's Cartesian and cylindrical charts are drawn for two holes of mass parameter $m$ at $z = \pm 2m$, on the axis through them and on the midplane $z = 0$ between them, where the symmetry keeps every ray a null geodesic; the isotropic chart is one hole, $U = 1 + m/r$.
Einstein, Infeld and Hoffmann's field is drawn for two bodies of equal mass $m$ on a circular orbit, $d = 20\,m$ apart, turning at the $\Omega^2 = (2Gm/d^3)(1 - 11m/2d)$ their equations of motion set to first order, `EIH_BINARY`, whose six potentials are functions of all four coordinates: both charts on the axis of the orbit, which half a turn about it keeps every ray on, out to $30\,m$, inside the near zone, which ends at $c/2\Omega = 37.1\,m$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm z_*$ with `_eih_axis`, a quadrature.
The Curzon-Chazy particle is drawn at $m = 1$ on its two totally geodesic planes in each of its charts, the axis and the plane $z = 0$: on the axis the cones close toward $R = 0$, which a ray reaches at $t = \pm\infty$, and in the plane they open toward the ring; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm z_*$, $z_* = z\,e^{2m/z} - 2m\,\mathrm{Ei}(2m/z)$, and $ct \pm \rho_*$, $\rho_* = \int_0^\rho e^{2m/s - m^2/2s^2}ds$.
Kasner's universe with a scalar field is drawn at $(p_1, p_2, p_3) = (2/13, 4/13, 7/13)$ and $q = 10/13$, where all three exponents are positive: the planes of $t$ and $x$ and of $t$ and $z$ in the synchronous chart, the plane of $\tau$ and $x$ in the logarithmic time with $-\tau$ upward, and the planes of $T$ and $x$ and of $T$ and $w$ of the vacuum of five dimensions that reduces to it, where the exponent along $x$ is negative; `--verify` checks the rays against $x \pm t^{1-p}/(1-p)$.
Many black holes and wormholes in three dimensions are drawn at $\ell = 1$ as Åminneborg, Bengtsson, Brill, Holst and Peldán's tent at $\alpha = \sqrt{2/3}$, on the plane through its axis and two opposite folds in the sausage, stereographic and free fall charts, each mirrored through the axis: the folds are `curves`, the region beyond them is hatched by `where`, and the event horizon is the ray `marked` from the axis at $ct = -\pi\ell/3$; the exterior chart is drawn outside the wormhole's horizon, $M \approx 0.703$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm 2\ell\arctan(\rho/\ell)$, $c\tau \pm x$, $\mathrm{arsinh}\tan(cT/\ell) \pm 2\,\mathrm{artanh}(\rho/\ell)$ and $ct \pm r_*$.
Shenker and Stanford's shock wave in the BTZ black hole is drawn at $\ell = R = 1$ and $\alpha = 1$ on the plane of its two null coordinates in both Kruskal charts and on the plane of $t$ and $r$ of either outside: the continuous chart hatches $|u(v + \alpha\Theta)| \ge 1$ by `where`, written with $\mathrm{sgn}(u)$, and marks the past horizon of the right outside and the future horizon of the left one as rays `marked` from the shock, $\alpha$ apart; the discontinuous chart draws its delta as the pulse of width $0.05$ in $U$, where a ray moving left follows $dV/dU = \alpha p(U)(1 + UV)^2$, so `--verify` holds it to the $V$ it had before the pulse, `_btz_shock_before`, and the rest of the rays to $u$, $v$, $U$ and $ct \mp r_*$.
Kasner's universe with a magnetic field is drawn at $(p_1, p_2, p_3) = (-2/7, 3/7, 6/7)$ and $b = 1$ on the planes of $t$ and $x$, across the field, and of $t$ and $z$, along it, and the axisymmetric universe on the same two planes in Rosen's time $\eta$, in units of $\ell$; `--verify` checks the rays against $x \pm (7/9)t^{9/7}$, $z \pm (7t^{1/7} + (14/13)t^{13/7} + (7/25)t^{25/7})$, $x \pm \tan(\eta/2)$ and $z \pm (\ln s + s^2 + s^4/4)/4$ with $s = \tan(\eta/2)$.
The quantum BTZ black hole is drawn in units of $\ell_3$: the hole with $M = F = 1/4$ and $\ell = 15\ell_3/16$, horizon $3\ell_3/4$, on the plane $\phi = 0$ of its static and Eddington-Finkelstein charts, a conical singularity dressed with a horizon, $\kappa = +1$, $\mu = 6$ and $\ell = \ell_3/3$, in the chart of the C-metric's brane, and a rotating hole with horizons $\ell_3$ and $\ell_3/2$ with $\phi$ divided out; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$, with $r_*$ summed over the roots of $r\,H$ or $r^2H$.
The exponential metric is drawn at $m = 1$ on its plane of the time and the radial coordinate in each chart: the isotropic radius from $r = 0$ through the throat $r = m$, the line $y = z = 0$ of the Cartesian chart through $r = 0$, the areal radius from the throat $R = e\,m$ out, where the throat is marked on the chart's edge, and the harmonic $u = 1/r$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with the tortoise coordinate of the Curzon-Chazy particle's axis, $r_* = r\,e^{2m/r} - 2m\,\mathrm{Ei}(2m/r)$, whose plane has the same metric.
The moving mirror is drawn at $\kappa = 1$, every window square: Carlitz and Willey's mirror, the mirror of Good, Anderson and Evans and a uniformly accelerating one on the plane of $t$ and $x$, the first two on the plane of $u$ and $v$ and on the plane of $U$ and $v$ that brings a mirror to rest, and each of the three on its own chart, where it stands on the left edge; every view names its mirror as its declared input, hatches what lies behind it by its row's `where`, draws the world line with `signed_curves`, since a mirror's $x$ takes either sign, and marks rays seeded a millionth to the right of the mirror and traced both ways, so that each is drawn arriving and reflected; `--verify` checks the rays against the null coordinates.
Zipoy and Voorhees's metric is drawn at $m = 1$ on its axis and its equatorial plane, both totally geodesic, in each of its charts, for the oblate $q = 1$, $\delta = 2$, and the prolate $q = -1/2$, $\delta = 1/2$: on the oblate axis a ray reaches $r = 2m$ only at $t = \pm\infty$ and the curvature there is finite, and on the other three planes it reaches the singularity in a finite time; the prolate equator's Kretschmann scalar grows only as $(r - 2m)^{-3/2}$, so those rows declare the edge in `singular_zero`, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = r + 4m\ln(r - 2m) - 4m^2/(r - 2m)$ and $\sqrt{r(r - 2m)} + 2m\ln(\sqrt{r} + \sqrt{r - 2m})$ on the axis and the quadratures of $f^{-1-q}h^{-q(2+q)/2}$ in the plane.
McVittie's mass is drawn on the planes of $t$ and $r$ and of $t$ and $R$ at $r_s = 1$ in a universe of dust and a cosmological constant, $a = \sinh^{2/3}(3H_0t/2)$ and $H = H_0\coth(3H_0t/2)$, the expansion Lake and Abdelqader chose, with $H_0 = c/(\sqrt{15}\,r_s)$, which is the $\Lambda r_s^2 = 1/5$ of the Schwarzschild-de Sitter views; its curvature diverges on $R = r_s$ only as $(dH/dt)^2/(1 - r_s/R)$, too slowly for the test at $10^{-5}$ of the drawing, so each row declares that curve in `singular_zero`, an expression whose zero set is drawn as a singular curve after the Kretschmann scalar has been checked at $10^{-20}$ and $10^{-30}$ of the chart's unit from it in 60 digits, and nothing is marked beyond it.
Sultana and Dyer's black hole is drawn at $r_s = 1$ and $\eta_0 = 3\,r_s$, which no ray depends on, one plane for each chart: the plane of $\eta$ and $r$, with the event horizon on $g^{rr} = 0$ and both trapping horizons, $r = \eta/2$ and the curve inside $r_s$, where $|\nabla R|^2$ vanishes for the areal radius $\eta^2r/\eta_0^2$; the plane of Schwarzschild's $t$ and $r$, whose big bang $\eta = ct + r_s\ln(r/r_s - 1) = 0$ the row declares in `where` and `singular_zero` as $(r/r_s - 1)e^{ct/r_s} = 1$, finite on $r_s$, so that the curve is found up the edge of the chart; and the plane of $v$ and $r$ against the chart's own coordinates, with `null_radius` and `other_trapped`, which marks the marginally trapped spheres of the other null family as well, where $|\nabla R|^2$ divided by the derivative of $R$ along the drawn radius vanishes. `--verify` checks the rays against $\eta + r$ and $\eta - r - 2r_s\ln|r/r_s - 1|$, $ct \pm r_*$, and $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, and `_tools/derivations/sultana_dyer.md` is the derivation.
Melvin's universe is drawn on its plane of $t$ and $\rho$ at $B = 1$, conformally flat, and Ernst's black hole on its equator at $B = 1/(2r_s)$, whose plane of $t$ and $r$ is $(1 + B^2r^2/4)^2$ times Schwarzschild's, so its rays are Schwarzschild's; each marks the radius of the widest circle about the axis, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm \rho$ and $ct \pm r_*$.
Ernst and Wild's Kerr hole in Melvin's universe is drawn at $a = 4m/5$ and $B = 1/(4m)$, where $r_+ = 8m/5$, $r_- = 2m/5$ and $k = 401/400$: the axis, whose plane of $t$ and $r$ is $k$ times that of Kerr's axis, and the equator with the circles of $\phi$ divided out, $H$ times Kerr's, with the ergosurface at $1.942\,m$, in the chart at rest and in the chart whose azimuth turns with the horizon, $\Omega = 813/3208\,m$, where $g_{tt}$ is negative from the horizon out on the equator and no ergosurface is marked. The published Kretschmann scalar holds $P$, which vanishes on the axis, below the line, and marks no edge of these views; `_tools/test_kerr_melvin.py` holds the curvature at $r = 0$, and `_tools/derivations/kerr_melvin.md` is the derivation.
The eye of the storm is drawn at $m = 1$, $a = 3m/5$ and $\ell = m/5$, where $r_+ = 1.515\,m$ and $r_- = 0.435\,m$, as Kerr-Newman's is: on its axis, the principal plane of its equator, which starts at $r = 0.1\,m$ since the principal directions of the Weyl tensor are lost to rounding where it vanishes toward the centre, the same plane seen from above, and the axis of its ingoing Kerr chart.
The black hole in a swirling universe is drawn on its equator at $m = 1$ and $j = 1/(16m^2)$, whose plane of $t$ and $r$ is $1 + j^2r^4$ times Schwarzschild's, the dragging term vanishing with $\cos\theta$, so its rays are Schwarzschild's, and the swirling universe on its plane $z = 0$ at $j = 1$, conformally flat; each marks the radius of the widest circle about the axis, $1/\sqrt{j}$, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ and $ct \pm \rho$.
Levi-Civita's cylinder is drawn at $\sigma = 1/4$ and $C = 1$, the point $(2/3, 2/3, -1/3)$ of Kasner's circle, on its plane of $t$ and $\rho$ in Weyl's coordinates and of $t$ and $r$ in the Kasner form, whose rays `--verify` checks against $ct \pm 4\rho^{1/4}$ and $ct \pm 3r^{1/3}$.
Gott's time machine is drawn for two strings with $4G\mu/c^2 = 1/3$, $v = 4c/5$ and $d = \ell/2$, where $\gamma\sin\alpha = 1.44$: on the Rindler and Milne planes of Grant's charts, at the boost $a = 3.64$ and the shift $b = 2.22\,\ell$ those strings have, each plane drawn with its edges $0$ and $a$ one line after the shift along $Y$, the Rindler plane marking the first two polarised hypersurfaces; `--verify` checks the rays against $\ln\xi \pm \eta$ and $\ln|\tau| \mp \chi$, and `_tools/derivations/gott_time_machine.md` derives $a$ and $b$.
The spinning string is drawn at $b = 0.9$, the cosmic string's deficit, and $a = 0.9\,r_c$, with $r_c = a/b$ the unit of length: the cylinders of $t$ and $\phi$ inside and outside $r_c$ in the proper radius and rescaled radius charts, the cylinder $R = a$ of the circumference radius chart, and the cylinders of $\tau$ and $\tilde\phi$ in the helical chart, where the rays run at 45° and one turn of the helix $c\tau = a\tilde\phi/b$ is the circle of constant $t$; `--verify` checks the rays against $c\,dt = (br - a)\,d\phi$ and $-(br + a)\,d\phi$, and `_tools/derivations/spinning_string.md` is the derivation.
Gowdy's torus is drawn for the polarised wave $Q = 0$, $P = -\tfrac{\pi}{4}Y_0(t)\cos\theta$, with the $\lambda$ that solves its constraints, on the plane of $t$ and $\theta$ and on the plane of $\tau = -\ln t$ and $\theta$, whose edges $\theta = 0$ and $2\pi$ are one line; the second is drawn against $-\tau$ so that the future is up, which mirrors the plane, so `Chart._null_dirs` hands back the chart's two families the other way round there and the one named first still moves left. Its sphere chart is drawn for the inside of Schwarzschild's horizon, $r = L(1 - \cos t)$ with $r_s = 2L$, which `print_charts.py` checks exactly, so the row names no `solves`; `--verify` checks all three against $t \pm \theta$ and $e^{-\tau} \pm \theta$, and `_tools/derivations/gowdy.md` derives the wave.
The wormhole time machine is drawn in the throat radius $r_0$ for one round trip of the right mouth, which `WormholeTrip` in `null_rays.py` holds for every diagram: rapidity $\eta = \tfrac{3}{2}\sin^3(2\pi\tau/P)$ over $P = 54\,r_0/c$, greatest speed $0.905\,c$ and greatest acceleration $0.2\,c^2/r_0$, from mouths $D = 10\,r_0$ apart. Morris, Thorne and Yurtsever's chart is drawn on the axis of the acceleration, $\theta = 0$, where the lapse is $1 + glF/c^2$, with $\Phi = 0$, $r = \sqrt{r_0^2 + l^2}$ and $F = e^{-r_0^2/4l^2}$ for $l > 0$, for three units of $r_0/c$ about the greatest acceleration and three about the greatest deceleration; the mouth of the short throat is drawn at $b = 1$, every ray at 45°, which `--verify` checks against $ct \pm l$.
Ori's time machine is drawn for his example $f = a(x^2 - y^2)/2$ at $a = 1/16$, $e = 1/8$ and $L = 2\pi$: the cylinder of the time and $z$ through the central circle $x = y = 0$, which is totally geodesic and Misner's, and the cylinder at $x = 4\,\ell$, $y = 0$, where the circle turns null at $T = \ell^2/2$ and at $t = 2\,\ell^2$, in each of his two charts, and the Brinkmann plane of $u$ and $v$ through the central circle with the edges $u = -2$ and $u = -2e^{-\pi}$ of one copy; `--verify` checks the rays against $z$ and $(T - f)e^{z/2}$, and `_tools/derivations/ori_time_machine.md` is the derivation.
Robinson and Trautman's fronts are drawn in the axisymmetric chart from Macedo and Saa's prolate first front, $f(0,\theta)^2 = f_0^2(1 - \epsilon^2\cos^2\theta)$ at $\epsilon = 4/5$, on the axis and on the equator, where the fronts' symmetry about the equator keeps every ray a null geodesic; `FrontSolver` solves the Robinson-Trautman equation from it in Legendre polynomials, `front_checks` holds the solution to making every published Ricci component vanish and the published Kretschmann scalar equal $48m^2/r^6$, and since the equation runs toward the future only, nothing is drawn before $u = 0$; `--verify` checks each ingoing ray against the Kruskal $V$ it has once the fronts are round.
The black hole threaded by a cosmic string is drawn at $r_s = 1$ and $b = 0.9$, the deficit the cosmic string is drawn at, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in the chart with the wedge removed, and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts; the string enters $g_{\phi\phi}$ alone, so every ray is Schwarzschild's, which `--verify` checks against $ct \pm r_*$.
The Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter black hole is drawn at $r_s = 2L$, where the horizon is $r_h = L$ exactly, the black hole of Hawking and Page's temperature $T_1$, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = \frac{1}{4}\ln|r - 1| - \frac{1}{8}\ln(r^2 + r + 2) + \frac{5}{4\sqrt{7}}\arctan\frac{2r + 1}{\sqrt{7}}$ in units of $L$.
The topological black holes are drawn in units of $L$ as three spacetimes of one line element: the flat hole at $\mu = L$, where $r_h = L$; the hyperbolic hole without mass, whose horizon is $r_h = L$ too; and the hyperbolic hole of negative mass $\mu = -120L/343$, where $r^3 - L^2r - L^2\mu = (r - 5L/7)(r - 3L/7)(r + 8L/7)$. The static chart and both Eddington-Finkelstein charts draw all three on their plane of the time and $r$ at $\rho = 1/2$, Lemos's string and the brane draw the flat hole, the brane on its plane of $t$ and $z$ at $z_h = L$ with the future toward larger $z$ beyond the horizon, and the hyperbolic chart the two hyperbolic holes; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = \frac{1}{3}\ln|r - 1| - \frac{1}{6}\ln(r^2 + r + 1) + \arctan((2r + 1)/\sqrt{3})/\sqrt{3}$, $\frac{1}{2}\ln|(r - 1)/(r + 1)|$ and $\frac{35}{26}\ln|r - 5/7| - \frac{21}{22}\ln|r - 3/7| - \frac{56}{143}\ln(r + 8/7)$, and the brane's against $ct \pm z_*$ with $z_* = \int dz/(1 - z^3)$, and `_tools/derivations/topological_black_hole.md` is the derivation.
The anti-de Sitter soliton is drawn at $r_0 = L$ on its plane of $t$ and the radius in each of its five charts, the tip the left edge of Horowitz and Myers's planes in four, five and three dimensions and the right edge of the Poincaré chart's, and from the tip and along the line through it in the polar chart, drawn against the signed distance $s$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$ with $r_* = B(1/n, 1/2)/n - u\,F(\tfrac12, \tfrac1n; 1 + \tfrac1n; u^n)$, $u = r_0/r$, and `_tools/derivations/ads_soliton.md` is the derivation.
The topological star is drawn at $r_B = 1$ and $r_S = 3/4$, where $R_y = 4r_B$, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in Bah and Heidmann's chart and in the Einstein metric of four dimensions, each with the bubble $r = r_B$ for its left edge, and along the line through the bubble in the chart about it, drawn against the signed $s = \pm\rho$; its black string at $r_S = 1$ and $r_B = 3/4$ in the ingoing chart, against $v - r$, from the bubble out; and the extremal string at $m = 1$ in its isotropic radius. `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$ with `slices.topological_star_rstar`, against $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, and against $ct \mp \rho_*$ with `slices.extremal_string_rstar`, and `_tools/derivations/topological_star.md` is the derivation.
Flat space with supertranslation hair is drawn at $C = \tfrac{1}{2}\ell(3\cos^2\theta - 1)$ on its equator in each of its three charts: the plane of $t$ and $\rho$, hatched inside the supertranslation horizon $\rho = 5\ell/2$, and the planes of $u$ and $r$ and of $v$ and $r$ against their own null times, with $ct = cu + W + \ell$ and $cv - W - \ell$ for the time; every ray is checked against the closed forms $ct \pm \rho$, $cu + 2W$ and $cv - 2W$.
Siklos's waves are drawn in units of $L$. The free charts, Siklos's and the one on the disc, are drawn for Kaigorodov's profile under a pulse, $H = e^{-4u^2/L^2}x^3/L^3$ and $h = LH/x$, on the plane of $u$ and $v$ at $x = L$ and $3L/2$, where a curve moving left is carried along $v$ by $\sqrt{\pi}\,x^3/4L^2$; the time function is $u + v$. Kaigorodov's spacetime is drawn on its slice $y = 0$ with a spacelike Killing direction divided out, `quotient`, as the rotating BTZ hole is: $\partial_u$ in Siklos's coordinates, the horospheric chart, the homogeneous form, where it is $\partial_X$, and the Kundt form, where it is $\partial_U$, and $\partial_x$ in the Poincaré chart; the homogeneous form's plane is drawn against $-U$ and oriented by $v = -Ue^{5Z}$, and the Kundt form's by $Vx^2$. The stationary region, where $\partial_u$ is timelike and dividing it out leaves no light cone, is drawn on its plane of $u$ and $v$ at $\rho = 0$. `--verify` checks the rays against $v - \sqrt{\pi}\,x^3\mathrm{erf}(2u)/8$, $v \pm \tfrac{2}{5}x^{5/2}$ in each chart's coordinates, $ct \pm z_*$ with $z_* = \int\sqrt{1 + z^3/2}\,dz$, and $v + u/2$, and `_tools/derivations/siklos.md` is the derivation.
Schrödinger spacetime is drawn at $L = \beta$, in units of $\beta$, on the plane of the time and the null coordinate at one depth, with the null coordinate across and the time up: at $r = \beta/2$, $\beta$ and $2\beta$ in the Poincaré chart, the same events in the inverse radius, the same depths in the global chart at $\omega = c/\beta$, the exponents $z = 1$, $3/2$ and $3$ at $r = \beta/\sqrt{2}$, and $r = \beta$ in five and six dimensions. No chart has a time function, $g^{tt} = 0$, so every row takes `orient="vector"`, as Gödel's do; `--verify` checks the rays against $t$ and $\xi + \tfrac{1}{2}h\,ct$ with $h$ the coefficient of $-c^2dt^2$ in the bracket, and `_tools/derivations/schrodinger_spacetime.md` is the derivation.
Kundt's waves are drawn in units of a length $\ell$. The free charts are drawn for the simplest wave under a pulse, $G = e^{-4u^2}(x^2 - y^2)/\ell$, on the plane of $u$ and the coordinate along the rays: Podolský and Beláň's at $x = \ell$ and $2\ell$ on $y = 0$, and Kundt's canonical chart at $x = \ell$, $y = 1.1\,\ell$, where $G$ is negative, since where it is positive the chart's $g^{ww}$ changes sign on a curve that is no horizon; its time function is $w + 30u$ and the others' $u + v$. The simplest wave is drawn on the same plane at $x = \ell$, where `--verify` checks the curves moving left against $u - \arctan v$, and on its slice $y = 0$ with $\partial_u$ divided out. The family with a cosmological constant is drawn at $\xi = \ell$, $\eta = 0$ for $\alpha = 1$, $\beta = 0$, $\Lambda = 3/\ell^2$ and for $\alpha = 0$, $\beta = 1/\ell$, $\Lambda = -3/\ell^2$, with the profile $(\xi^2 - \eta^2)(2 + p)/(3p\ell^2)$ under the same pulse; `_tools/derivations/kundt_waves.md` Step 4 gives the reasons.
The charged black hole in anti-de Sitter space is drawn at $r_s = 27L/8$ and $r_q^2 = 11L^2/8$, where $r^2f = (r - L)(r - L/2)(r^2 + 3Lr/2 + 11L^2/4)/L^2$ and the horizons are $r_+ = L$ and $r_- = L/2$, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, whose rows take the time functions $v - h(r)$ and $u + h(r)$ with $h' = r^2/((r^2 + 1)(r^2 + 2))$, since $v - r$ and $u + r$ are spacelike where $f > 2$, near the singularity and far outside; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with `slices.rnads_rstar`, and `_tools/derivations/reissner_nordstrom_ads.md` is the derivation.
Boulware and Deser's black hole is drawn at $r_0 = 13r_h/12$ and $\ell = 5r_h/12$, in units of its horizon radius $r_h = \sqrt{r_0^2 - \ell^2}$, above the ratio $r_0/\ell = 1 + \sqrt{2}$ below which it is unstable, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, and the other branch at $r_0 = \ell$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with `slices.boulware_deser_rstar` and `slices.boulware_deser_plus_rstar`, and `_tools/derivations/boulware_deser.md` is the derivation.
The Kaluza-Klein monopole is drawn at $m = 1$ on its plane of $t$ and the radius on the half axis $\theta = 0$, in each of its three charts, and through the nut in Gross and Perry's; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = \sqrt{r(r + 4)} + 4\,\mathrm{arsinh}(\sqrt{r}/2)$, the proper distance from the nut, and $\rho = r + 2$ with the Taub-NUT radius.
The Kaluza-Klein black holes are drawn in units of $r_s$: the electric hole at $q = 2\,r_s$ in its static chart and against $v - \sqrt{q/r_s}\,r$ in its ingoing one, both with the circle divided out, `quotient`, since between $r_s$ and the ergosurface $r = q$, which each marks, the plane at fixed $y$ has no null direction; the magnetic hole at $p = 2\,r_s$ on the half axis $\theta = 0$; the hole of equal charges at $p = 3\,r_s$ with the circle divided out, Reissner and Nordström's plane with horizons at $2\,r_s$ and $r_s$; and the Einstein metric of four dimensions on its static plane and against $v - r$. `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with `slices.kkbh_rstar`, the integral of $\sqrt{r(r + q - r_s)}/(r - r_s)$, in the ingoing chart of five dimensions against $v - \sqrt{q/r_s}(r + r_s\ln|r/r_s - 1|) \pm r_*$, and the equal charges against Reissner and Nordström's $r_*$; `_tools/derivations/kaluza_klein_black_hole.md` is the derivation.
Myers and Perry's black hole is drawn with one spin in five dimensions at $\mu = 1$ and $a = 3/5$, where $r_+ = 4/5$, in units of $\sqrt{\mu}$: the plane of $t$ and $r$ transverse to the rotation, $\theta = 0$, whose rays are null geodesics, and the plane of rotation, $\theta = \pi/2$, with $\phi$ divided out, in the Boyer-Lindquist chart and against $v - r$ in the ingoing one; with equal spins at $a = 2/5$ on its plane of $t$ and $\rho$ with the Hopf fibre $\psi$ divided out; and with one spin in six dimensions at $a = 3/2$ in units of $\mu^{1/3}$, on both planes. `--verify` checks the transverse planes against $ct \pm r_*$, with $r_* = r + \tfrac{5}{8}\ln|(r - \tfrac45)/(r + \tfrac45)|$ in five dimensions and $r + \mathrm{Re}\sum_i A_i\ln(1 - r/r_i)$ over the roots of $r^3 + a^2r = \mu$ in six; the two-spin chart has no view, and `_tools/derivations/myers_perry.md` says why.
Black Saturn is drawn for a hole with no angular momentum of its own inside a ring in balance, $\kappa_1 = 7/8$, $\kappa_2 = 9/16$, $\kappa_3 = 3/7$ and $\beta = 0$, in units of $L$, on the plane of its ring, $\rho = 0$, with $\psi$ divided out: outside the ring and between the ring and the hole in Weyl's chart, and from the point $z = 0$ out to infinity on $\theta = \pi/2$ in the polar chart. The charts leave $V$, $\Omega$, $W$ and $\nu$ free, and each row declares Elvang and Figueras's on that plane, where they are rational in $z$, `BS_OUTSIDE` and `BS_BETWEEN`, which `_tools/test_black_saturn.py` holds to the functions the page prints; the rows set `kretschmann=False`, since the plane's own functions do not hold the derivatives across it. On the plane the circles of $\phi$ have shrunk and $\Gamma^\phi{}_{\rho\phi}$ is not finite, so `quotient_checks` counts a component that is not finite and multiplies a vanishing part of the tangent as nothing. The ring alone is drawn in ring coordinates at $\nu = 1/2$ and $\lambda = 4/5$, outside the ring, $x = -1$, and on the disc inside it, $x = 1$. `--verify` checks the Saturn's rays against $ct \pm z_*$ with `_bs_star`, the quadrature of $e^{\nu + V - W}$, and `_tools/derivations/black_saturn.md` is the derivation.
Neugebauer and Meinel's disc is drawn at $\mu = 3$ in units of its coordinate radius $\rho_0$, with the functions its charts leave free read from `nm_disc.py`, which evaluates the ultraelliptic theta functions of the solution and tabulates them on the plane of the disc and on the axis; `DECLARED_FUNCTIONS` carries the tables, `nm_g`, `nm_w`, `nm_r`, `nm_e`, `nm_s`, `nm_x` and `nm_y`, each with two derivatives, and the rows set `kretschmann=False`. The axis is drawn in Weyl's form, the plane in Bardeen and Wagoner's with $\phi$ divided out, across the ergoregion, whose two crossings of the plane it marks, the plane at rest in the turning frame out to $1.378\,\rho_0$, where the frame moves at the speed of light, and the axis and the plane beyond the rim in the spheroidal chart; the limit $\mu \to \mu_0$ is drawn at $m = 1$ on its axis and its equator. The plane's box ends at $4.2\,\rho_0$ so that no point of `quotient_checks` falls on the rim, where the second derivatives of the metric have no bound. `--verify` checks the rays against `_nm_star`, a spline through each tortoise coordinate's slope, and `_tools/derivations/neugebauer_meinel.md` is the derivation.
Teo's wormhole is drawn at $b_0 = 1$ in both of its charts: on the axis, where the dragging of frames vanishes and the plane of the time and the radius holds its rays, at his $a = 1/4$, and on the equator with $\phi$ divided out at $a = 1$, where the rays keep $ct \mp l$ for every spin and the ergosurface $r = \sqrt{2a}\,b_0$ is marked; the proper distance chart's $r(l)$ is `teo_rho`, the inverse of Teo's (28) by Newton's method, one of `DECLARED_FUNCTIONS`, the sympy functions a row's `functions` may name that carry their own derivative and their own numbers, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm l$ and, on the axis, $ct \pm l_*$ with $l_* = \sqrt{r(r - 1)} - \ln(\sqrt{r} + \sqrt{r - 1}) + \sqrt{2}\,\mathrm{artanh}\sqrt{(r - 1)/2r}$.
Hartle and Thorne's star is drawn in units of $m$ from its surface at $R = 6\,m$ out, at $a = m/4$ and $q = 4a^2$, on the axis, where the dragging term vanishes, and on the equator with $\phi$ divided out, in their chart and in the Painlevé-Gullstrand one; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with `ht_star`, the quadrature of $\sqrt{(1 - 2j_2P_2)/(1 + 2h_2P_2)}/F$ from `ht_functions`, which writes their (A1) out independently of the file, and against the two quadratures of $dr/(1 \mp \sqrt{2m/r})$, and `_tools/derivations/hartle_thorne.md` says why no figure in three dimensions is drawn.
Kinnersley's photon rocket is drawn in units of the mass $m_0$ it starts with, through a burn from $u = 0$ to $cu = 10\,m_0$ along the first axis with $\alpha = 2\sin^2(\pi cu/10m_0)/(25\,m_0)$, rapidity $w = \int\alpha\,c\,du$ ending at $2/5$, and $m = m_0e^{-3w}$, the least loss of mass that keeps the density of the radiation positive in every direction, on the two halves of the axis of flight in both charts, where $\sin\theta = 0$ keeps every ray a null geodesic; $\alpha m$ stays below $1/16$, so behind the rocket the two zeros of $g^{rr}$ never meet, and the Robinson-Trautman chart's $p$, a name the reader holds as a function, is written out by `prep` before the declared four-velocity goes in.
Hayward's regular black hole is drawn at $\ell = 12m/7\sqrt{7} = 0.648\,m$, where $r^3 - 2mr^2 + 2m\ell^2 = (r - 12m/7)(r - 6m/7)(r + 4m/7)$ and the horizons are $r_+ = 12m/7$ and $r_- = 6m/7$, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, where `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = r + 3\ln|r - 12/7| - \tfrac{6}{5}\ln|r - 6/7| + \tfrac{1}{5}\ln(r + 4/7)$ in units of $m$. Its forming and evaporating chart is drawn with the mass `HAYWARD_MASS`, in units of its greatest value $m_0$: $\sin^2(\pi v/4)$ from $v = 0$ to $2$, $1$ until $v = 4$, and $\cos^2(\pi(v - 4)/8)$ until $v = 8$, with the same $\ell$, so that the curve $g^{rr} = 0$, which the script marks, is closed, opening at $v = 1.479$ and closing at $5.042$, both at $r = \sqrt{3}\,\ell$; `_tools/derivations/hayward.md` is the derivation.
The regular black hole from pure gravity is drawn at $r_0 = 1$ and $\ell = 12r_0/25$, where $r^4 - r_0^2r^2 + \ell^2r_0^2 = (r^2 - 16r_0^2/25)(r^2 - 9r_0^2/25)$ and the horizons are $r_+ = 4r_0/5$ and $r_- = 3r_0/5$, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, each from the centre to $2\,r_0$ with $\psi = \theta = \pi/2$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = r + \tfrac{10}{7}\ln|(r - r_+)/(r + r_+)| - \tfrac{15}{14}\ln|(r - r_-)/(r + r_-)|$ in units of $r_0$, `_rbhpg_rstar`.
Damour and Solodukhin's wormhole is drawn at $r_s = 1$ and $\lambda = 1/5$ on its plane of the time and the radial coordinate in each of its five charts: from the throat out in Damour and Solodukhin's own and in the rescaled time, and through the throat in Bueno and his collaborators' $\rho$, in the isotropic radius, whose throat is $r_s/4$, and in Einstein and Rosen's $u$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$, $ct \mp \sqrt{1 + \lambda^2}\,r_*$, $ct \mp x$ with $x = r_s((2 + \lambda^2)\rho + \lambda^2\sinh\rho)/2(1 + \lambda^2)$, and $ct \mp r_*\,\mathrm{sgn}(u)$, with $r_*$ the closed form `_tools/derivations/damour_solodukhin.md` derives.
The wormhole of Einstein-Dirac-Maxwell theory is drawn at $r_0 = 1$ and $Q_e = 1/2$, so $M = 2/5$, on its plane of the time and the radial coordinate in each of its three charts: from the throat out in the areal radius, and through the throat in Bronnikov and Kim's $u$, $r = r_0 + u^2$, and in the compact $x$, $r = r_0/(1 - x^2)$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$, times the sign of $u$ or $x$ through the throat, with $r_*$ the closed form `_edm_rstar` that `_tools/derivations/einstein_dirac_maxwell_wormhole.md` Step 6 derives.
Interstellar's wormhole is drawn at $a = \rho$ and $M = \rho/2$, the wormhole of James, von Tunzelmann, Franklin and Thorne's figure 3, in units of $\rho$, on its plane of $t$ and $\ell$ in each of its three charts: through the whole wormhole in the proper distance, with the declared $r(\ell)$ of `iw_radius` and the mouths $\ell = \pm a$ marked, along the cylinder, and along the flare from $\ell = a$ out; every plane is $-c^2dt^2 + d\ell^2$, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp \ell$.
Kiselev's black hole is drawn in his example $w = -2/3$ at $r_q = 8\,r_s$, where $f = 1 - r_s/r - r/r_q$ vanishes at $r_\mp = (4 \mp 2\sqrt{2})\,r_s$ and is greatest at $\sqrt{r_sr_q}$, the radius the static chart's `split` takes: on the plane of $t$ and $r$ in the chart that keeps $w$ and in the chart of the linear term, and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, where `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = (4\sqrt{2} - 4)\ln|r - r_-| - (4\sqrt{2} + 4)\ln|r - r_+|$ in units of $r_s$. The matter alone is drawn in Kiselev's two charts of it, each conformal to a flat plane, so its rays keep $\eta \pm \chi$ and $\tau \pm \rho$. The conformally flat chart is published where $\tau + \rho > 0$, and that edge is infinity, where the conformal factor diverges and $g^{\rho\rho}$ vanishes with it: its row sets `where_is_infinity`, so no zero of $g^{\rho\rho}$ is marked there, and `no_throat`, since the area radius is stationary along $\rho$ on $\tau = 0$, which is no throat. Its Kretschmann scalar $2(\tau + \rho)^2/(r_q^4\rho^2)$ diverges on the centre with a coefficient that falls to zero toward $\tau = 0$, so the row takes it at `singular_near = 1e-9` of the drawing from the edge, where every other row takes it at $10^{-5}$, and the singular stretch runs from the origin.
Einstein and Rosen's bridges are drawn as three spacetimes of one page: the neutral bridge at $r_s = 1$ through the bridge in their $u$, on one sheet in Schwarzschild's $r$ and through the bridge in the isotropic radius, where it is $r_s/4$; the charged bridge with a mass at $r_q = \sqrt{3}\,r_s/2$, where $r_+ = 3r_s/2$, on one sheet; and the charged bridge with no mass at $r_q = 1$ in their $u$. The two planes of an areal radius begin below the bridge, with `where` leaving that part hatched and empty, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$, with the sign of $u$ or of $4r - r_s$ through a bridge, for $r_* = r + r_s\ln(r/r_s - 1)$, $r + \tfrac{9}{8}r_s\ln(r/r_+ - 1) - \tfrac{1}{8}r_s\ln(2r/r_s + 1)$ and $r + \tfrac{1}{2}r_q\ln((r - r_q)/(r + r_q))$; `_tools/derivations/einstein_rosen_bridge.md` is the derivation.
Van Den Broeck's drive is drawn with a pocket of Krasnikov's kind, which `_tools/derivations/van_den_broeck.md` derives: in units of the radius $R$ where the wall of the bubble begins, the areal radius is $l + 4$ on a flat floor, $7/4 - (l + 2)^2$ round the rim, $-l$ on a flat lid, $l^2 + 1/4$ through the neck and $l$ outside it, in the proper distance $l$ from the middle of the neck, which makes $B = 380$ in the pocket; the wall falls from $f = 1$ at $R$ to $0$ at $3R/2$, and $v_s = 2$. The Cartesian chart draws the plane of $t$ and $x$ on the axis, as Alcubierre's does, the comoving spherical chart the plane of $t$ and $r$, whose rays `--verify` checks against $ct \pm l(r)$, and Krasnikov's chart the plane of $t$ and $l$, where every ray runs at 45°.
Tippett and Tsang's bubble is drawn as their own, $h = \tfrac12 + \tfrac12\tanh\alpha[R^4 - y^4 - z^4 - (x^2 + c^2t^2 - A^2)^2]$ with their $A = 100$, $R = 70$ and $\alpha = 1/6000000$, in units of $A$, on the plane $y = z = 0$, which holds its light rays: the plane of $t$ and $x$ in the Cartesian chart, the same plane unrolled in the polar chart, where the bubble is a band, and the flat interior alone in its own chart and in Rindler's. The spacetime has no arrow of time, so those rows state one in `tau`, a piecewise time function, the angle $\lambda$ of the plane where $h > 1/2$ and $t$ elsewhere, Tippett and Tsang's choice in their figure 3, and the captions say that the two are opposite across the walls at $x < 0$; a coordinate named `lambda` is written `lambda_` in a row's `tau`, since sympy cannot parse the bare word. The four events $x = 0$, $h = 1/2$ of the plane, where the published metric is degenerate, are marked as removed events; `_tools/derivations/tippett_tsang.md` is the derivation.
The charged black hole in de Sitter space is drawn as the lukewarm hole, $r_q = r_s/2$ and $\Lambda r_s^2 = 27/64$, which is $Hr_s/c = 3/8$, where the horizons are $r_- = (2\sqrt{7} - 4)r_s/3$, $r_+ = 2r_s/3$ and $r_c = 2\,r_s$: its static plane with `split` at the static radius $1.298\,r_s$, both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, whose rows take the time functions $v - h(r)$ and $u + h(r)$ with $h' = r^2/(r^2 + r_q^2)$, since $v - r$ and $u + r$ are spacelike where $f > 2$, inside $r_-$, and the cosmological plane of $\tau$ and $\rho$, where the horizons are the hyperbolas on which $|\nabla r|^2$ vanishes, the singularity $2H\tau\rho + r_s = 0$ is declared in `singular_zero`, and `no_throat` leaves the line $\tau = 0$ unmarked, on which every $\rho$ has the areal radius $r_s/2$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = \sum_i \ln|r - r_i|/f'(r_i)$ over the four roots, and on the cosmological plane against $cT \pm r_*$ with the static time $T$ of `_rnds_cosmic`, and `_tools/derivations/reissner_nordstrom_de_sitter.md` is the derivation.
The travelling wave on a string is drawn for the pulse $A = (\ell/2)e^{-4u^2/\ell^2}$, $B = 0$ on a string with $b = 1/2$, heavy enough for the wave's field to show, on planes of $u$ and $v$ at fixed places across the string: on both sides of it at $r = \ell$ in the null conical chart, whose $F$ is the string's own profile, checked to make the published $G^v{}_u$ vanish, at $x = \ell/4$ in the isotropic chart, and at $X = -\ell/4$ and $3\ell/4$ in the moving string chart; the time function is $3u + v$, since $u + v$ stops being one where $g_{uu} < -1$, and `--verify` checks the curves against $v \pm \tfrac{1}{2}\ell A'$ and, in the moving string chart, against a quadrature of $g_{uu}$, and `_tools/derivations/string_wave.md` is the derivation.
Simpson and Visser's black bounce is drawn at $r_s = 1$ for its three geometries, $a = r_s/2$, $r_s$ and $2\,r_s$, one view for each in each of its four charts: through $r = 0$ in Simpson and Visser's own and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, against $v - r$ and $u + r$, and from the sphere of least area out in Tsukamoto's areal radius; where $a < r_s$ the region between the horizons $r = \pm 0.87\,r_s$ takes its future from the ingoing chart, and `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$, $v - 2r_*$ and $u + 2r_*$ with the three closed forms of $r_*$ that `_tools/derivations/simpson_visser.md` derives.
Born and Infeld's point charge is drawn in units of $r_0$ at $r_q = r_0/2$ for two masses, on its plane of the time and $r$ in each chart: Hoffmann's particle, $r_s = 0.618\,r_0$, whose cones stay open down to the conical singularity at the centre, and the black hole, $r_s = 2\,r_0$, whose cones close at its one horizon, $r_h = 1.867\,r_0$. The particle's $r_s$, $\Gamma(1/4)^2/(12\sqrt{\pi})\,r_0$, has no short form, so its rows state it by the `rounded` field of a `Diagram`, which prints a parameter as `\approx` and the decimals given; `--verify` checks every traced ray against $ct \pm r_*$ with `slices.born_infeld_rstar`.
Bardeen's regular black hole is drawn at $r_s = 1$ and $g = r_s/3$, where $r_+ = 0.775\,r_s$ and $r_- = 0.301\,r_s$, on its plane of $t$ and $r$ in the static chart and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, with no edge marked singular, since $r = 0$ is a regular centre; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$, the tortoise coordinate `slices.bardeen_rstar` sums, and `_tools/derivations/bardeen.md` is the derivation.
Israel, Wilson and Perjés's fields are drawn as three spacetimes of one page, in units of $m$: the spinning source at $a = m$ on its axis, through the ring, and its principal null rays on the equator from $r = -m/2$, outside the circle $r = -0.60\,m$ where the circles of $\phi$ turn timelike; the source of complex mass at $l = m/2$ on its plane of $t$ and $r$ from the horizon $r = m$; and, in the cylindrical chart, Hartle and Hawking's two sources $m \mp il$ at $z = \pm 2m$ with $l = m/2$, `IWP_TWO`, on the midplane with $\phi$ divided out, from $\rho = m$. `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$ with $r_* = r + \ln(r^2 + 1) + \arctan r$ and $r + 2\ln(r - 1) - \tfrac{5}{4}/(r - 1)$, and on the midplane against the quadrature of $\sqrt{W^4\rho^2 - \omega^2}/\rho$, and `_tools/derivations/israel_wilson_perjes.md` is the derivation.
Kastor and Traschen's holes are drawn falling together, $H < 0$, in units of one hole's mass parameter $m$: two holes at $z = \pm 2m$ with $H = -3c/(32m)$ on the axis through them and on the midplane between them, in the Cartesian and comoving charts, and on the midplane in the cylindrical chart, and one hole alone at $H = -3c/(16m)$, the lukewarm hole, in the isotropic chart. Each two-hole view marks the last ray of its plane to reach infinity, the event horizon there, which `slices.kt_last_ray` finds by integrating $dR/dy = R + H(R + xV)^2$ inward from $x = 10^7m$ and which leaves the midpoint of the pair at $c\tau = -6.1995\,m$; `marked` takes `"future"` beside `"past"` so that the midplane marks it from that event on. The Cartesian, cylindrical and isotropic rows declare the singularity $U = 0$ in `singular_zero` and set `ends_on_singular`, which ends a ray on that curve, since the metric is built on $U^2$ and the null condition runs on through it; `weak_singularity` solves for the curve along $x^0$ where it runs level in $r$, and `bound_along` takes a domain's end at the plane's fixed coordinates where it names one, as $-V/H$ does. `--verify` checks one hole's rays against the static time $cT \pm r_*$ of `slices.kt_static_t`, and the two holes' rays, before $\tau = 0$, against where scipy's integrator puts each at $c\tau = -12\,m$; `_tools/derivations/kastor_traschen.md` is the derivation.
Fisher, Janis, Newman and Winicour's scalar field is drawn at $\gamma = 1/2$, the value of Abdolrahimi and Shoom's figures, on its plane of the time and the radial coordinate in each of its four charts: from the singularity out in units of $b$ in the spherical chart, in Janis, Newman and Winicour's $R = r - 3b/4$ and in the isotropic radius, whose singularity is $b/4$ and whose row stops its rays there with `inside`, since at this $\gamma$ the cones stay open on it, and in units of $k$ at $m = k/2$ in the harmonic chart, where the singularity is $u \to \infty$ and the outgoing family moves left; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$ with $r_* = \sqrt{r(r - b)} + b\ln((\sqrt{r} + \sqrt{r - b})/\sqrt{b})$, and `_tools/derivations/fisher_jnw.md` is the derivation.
Roberts's collapsing scalar field is drawn for its three outcomes, $p = 9/10$, $1$ and $2$, in each of its five charts, in units of any length $\ell$: the double null chart against $(v - u)/2$ and $(u + v)/2$, Roberts's $v$ and $r$ against $r$ and $(1 + p)v - r$, the areal chart against $R$ and $(1 + p/2)v - R$, the plane of $t$ and $\rho$ as it stands, and the scaling chart against $x$ and $\tau + x$, a time function where neither coordinate is a time.
The published domains hatch the flat space before the first ray and, for $p < 1$, after the last, the rows declare the singularity $u = (1 - p)v$ in each chart's coordinates with `singular_zero`, and `singular_where_claimed` keeps the chart's formula beyond its domain from marking an edge.
A row with `null_radius=True` has a null coordinate for its drawn radius, so the curve on which the areal radius is stationary along it is marked as the apparent horizon and never as a throat.
The areal chart degenerates on $R = 0$, so its rows take a finer step and the row for $p = 2$ stops its rays at the edge of the domain with `inside`.
`--verify` checks every view's rays against $u$ and $v$, and `_tools/derivations/roberts.md` is the derivation.
Bonnor and Vaidya's charged star is drawn with a thin shell of charged null dust of mass $M$ and charge $q = 0.96\,M$, the ratio of Reissner-Nordström's drawings: falling along $v = 0$ onto flat space in the ingoing chart, against $v - r$, and leaving along $u = 0$ in the outgoing chart, against $u + r$, with $r_+ = 1.28\,M$ and $r_- = 0.72\,M$ on the charged side.
Each row marks the sphere $r = q^2/2M$ on the shell, where its energy density changes sign, orients its cones by the family that keeps the null time, since inside $r = \sqrt{M^2 + q^2} - M$ a slice of constant $v - r$ is timelike, and gives both steps the flat side's value on the shell itself, where sympy's Heaviside function is $1/2$ and $q^2$ would be a quarter while $m$ is a half.
The homothetic chart is drawn at $Q = 0.96\,M$ and $\mu = 1/20$ on its plane of $V$ and $R$, where the zeros of the published $g^{RR}$ are the three outgoing rays that keep their $R$, $0.67\,M$, $1.83\,M$ and $7.50\,M$, and the line $R = Q^2/M$ is marked.
`--verify` checks the shell's rays against $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, and $u$ and $u + 2r_*$, on the charged side, and `_tools/derivations/bonnor_vaidya.md` is the derivation.
Kerr-de Sitter is drawn at $a = 0.45\,r_s$ and $\Lambda = 0.2/r_s^2$, Kerr's spin and Kottler's cosmological constant, and Kerr-anti-de Sitter at $r_s = 2\ell$ and $a = \ell/2$ with $\ell = \sqrt{-3/\Lambda}$: on the axis in Carter's chart, both Kerr charts and the Kerr-Schild chart, the principal null rays on the equator in Carter's chart, and the equator from above in Carter's chart and in the frame that does not rotate at infinity. Carter's chart is published between $r_+$ and $r_c$, a domain `parse_domains` reads at the two outermost zeros of $g^{rr}$, and its row is oriented by `split` at $r = 2r_s$; the principal view for $\Lambda < 0$ starts at $r = 0.2\,\ell$, since a point of its lattice fell on $r_-$ itself, where the chart's components are not finite; `slices.kds_rstar` and `slices.kds_schild` place the moment $t = 0$ in the Kerr and Kerr-Schild charts, and `_tools/derivations/kerr_de_sitter.md` is the derivation.
Kerr-Taub-NUT is drawn at $a = m$ and $l = 5m/4$, where $r_+ = 9m/4$, $r_- = -m/4$ and no ring singularity is left: the principal null rays on the equator in the Boyer-Lindquist chart, in $t$ and $r$ and from above, and in Plebański and Demiański's chart in $\tau$ and $q$ at $p = l$; and the half of the axis that is regular, $\theta = 0$, in the chart with one Misner string and in both Kerr charts, which run through both horizons. Brill's charged Taub-NUT is drawn as a black hole at $l = 3m/4$ and $r_q = m$, where $r_+ = 7m/4$ and $r_- = m/4$, and as a wormhole with no mass at $r_q = 3l/2$, where $\Delta = r^2 + 5l^2/4$ has no root: the equator's plane of $t$ and $r$ in the chart with two Misner strings, where the cross term vanishes, and the regular half of the axis in the chart with one and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, which run through both horizons, each for the hole and, but for the outgoing chart, for the wormhole; and Brill's universe between the horizons on its plane of $\tau$ and the length $2l\psi$, where every ray winds without end toward $\tau_\pm$. `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp r_*$, $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, $u + 2r_*$ and $u$, and $2l\psi \mp r_*(\tau)$ with `slices.brill_rstar`, and `_tools/derivations/brill_charged_taub_nut.md` is the derivation.
Podolský and Ovcharenko's Kerr hole in Bertotti and Robinson's field is drawn at $a = 4m/5$ and $B = 1/(4m)$, the values of Ernst and Wild's hole on the `kerr_melvin` page, where $C = 60025/61374$, $r_+ = 1.684\,m$ and $r_- = 0.405\,m$: the axis, whose plane of $t$ and $r$ is $\Omega^{-2}$ times Kerr's axis with $Q$ in place of $\Delta$, and the equator with the circles of $\phi$ divided out, with the ergosurface at $2.021\,m$, where $Q = a^2$. The black string is drawn at $r_s = 1$ on its plane of the time and $r$ at fixed angles and fixed $z$, Schwarzschild's plane, in its static, ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein and Kerr-Schild charts, and at $r_h = 1$ in the static chart of six dimensions, Tangherlini's plane of five; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_*$, against $v$ and $v - 2r_*$, and in the Kerr-Schild chart against $cT + r$ and $cT + r - 2r_*$.
The throat of extreme Kerr is drawn at $r_0 = 1$ on its equator with $\phi$ divided out, one plane for each of its four charts, where the metric orthogonal to the circles is half a two dimensional anti-de Sitter space: the rays of no angular momentum are the throat's principal null rays, the same at every $\theta$, which `print_charts.py` checks, the global plane marks $y = \pm 1/\sqrt{3}$, where the published $g_{\tau\tau}$ vanishes, and the near-NHEK plane is drawn at $k = r_0/2$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r_0^2/r$, $ct \pm x$, $\tau \mp \arctan y$ and $ct \mp r_*$ with $r_* = r_0\ln(1 - r_0/r)$, and `_tools/derivations/near_horizon_extreme_kerr.md` is the derivation.
Randall and Sundrum's wall is drawn at $k = 1$ on its plane of $t$ and the fifth coordinate, every $x_i$ held fixed, in each of its four charts: out to $2.5/k$ on either side in the proper distance chart, where the cones close as $e^{-k|y|}$, with 45° rays in the conformally flat chart and in the Poincaré chart of one side from the wall at $z = 1/k$, and between two walls at $kr_c = 1/2$, whose edges $\phi = \pm\pi$ are one wall and are named in `periodic`; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp \mathrm{sgn}(y)(e^{|y|} - 1)$, $ct \mp w$ and $ct \mp z$, and `_tools/derivations/randall_sundrum.md` is the derivation.
Witten's black hole in two dimensions is drawn at $\lambda = 1$, the unit of length being $1/\lambda$, and $m = 1$, where the horizon is $x = 0$ and $w = 1$, on the whole plane of each of its seven charts: outside the horizon in Witten's proper distance and in the conformal chart, across it toward the singularity in the Schwarzschild gauge, in the dilaton chart, whose rays are the exponentials $w - m \propto e^{\pm 2\lambda ct}$, and in both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, and the Kruskal plane of $U$ and $V$ against $(V - U)/2$ and $(U + V)/2$, with its horizons marked as the two rays through the origin, its singularity $UV = 1$ declared in `singular_zero`, and `where="1 - U*V"` leaving what lies beyond it hatched and empty; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm \sigma$ with $\sigma = \ln\sinh r$, $\tfrac{1}{2}\ln|e^{2x} - 1|$ and $\tfrac{1}{2}\ln|w - 1|$, and against $U$ and $V$, and `_tools/derivations/witten_black_hole.md` is the derivation.
The black hole of Jackiw and Teitelboim's gravity is drawn at $L = r_h = 1$ in each of its five charts: the static chart across the horizon to $r = 0$, where the dilaton vanishes, oriented `ingoing`; the outside in the proper distance $\rho$; the Kruskal plane against $(V - U)/2$ and $(U + V)/2$, with `where="(1 - U*V)*(1 + U*V)"` hatching what lies beyond the boundaries $UV = -1$ and beyond $UV = 1$; the global square $0 < \sigma < \pi$, $|\tau| < \pi/2$, where `where_is_infinity` keeps the vanishing $g^{\sigma\sigma}$ of its two boundaries unmarked, since rounding finds it on one edge alone; and the Poincaré plane out to $z = 6L$, hatched beyond the hyperbola $c^2T^2 - z^2 = 4L^4/r_h^2$; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm \tfrac{1}{2}\ln|(r - 1)/(r + 1)|$, $ct \pm \ln\tanh(\rho/2)$, $U$ and $V$, $\tau \pm \sigma$ and $cT \pm z$, and `_tools/derivations/jackiw_teitelboim_black_hole.md` is the derivation.
Maldacena and Qi's eternal traversable wormhole is drawn at $L = 1$ in each of its three charts: the global strip $0 < \sigma < \pi$, $|t| < \pi/2$, with its middle $\sigma = \pi/2$ marked as a `throat` line and the two rays through it at $t = 0$ marked from boundary to boundary; the Poincaré plane out to $z = 3$, with the middle $z^2 - t_P^2 = 1$ as a world line of `curves` and the same two rays; and the thermal plane out to $\rho = 3$; `--verify` checks the rays against $t \pm \sigma$, $t_P \pm z$ and $t_R \pm \ln\tanh(\rho/2)$, and `_tools/derivations/eternal_traversable_wormhole.md` is the derivation.
Point particles in three dimensions are drawn at $\alpha = 3/4$, a deficit of $90°$: the plane of $t$ and the radius in the conical, wedge, circumference radius and isotropic charts, with $\ell = 1$, where `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm r$, $ct \pm R/\alpha$ and $ct \pm (\ell/\alpha)(\rho/\ell)^\alpha$; Gott and Alpert's planet from its centre to its edge at $\cos\chi_0 = 3/4$; and two particles at rest at $x = \pm d$, both of $\alpha = 3/4$ with $\ell = d$, on the plane $y = 0$ through them, in two views that each end on a particle, where a ray ends, checked against the proper distance `_two_particles_distance`. The beam of light passing the particle is `string_rays` at this deficit, and the moving chart's figure is `moving_wedge` at $v = 3c/5$; `_tools/derivations/point_particle_2plus1.md` is the derivation.
Coleman and De Luccia's bubble is drawn with a thin wall for their two cases, at their $\bar\rho_0 = \ell$, in units of the curvature radius $\ell$ of the vacuum that has one: the decay into flat space, de Sitter space outside a wall born with $\bar\rho = 4\ell/5$, and the decay of flat space, anti-de Sitter space inside a wall born with $\bar\rho = 4\ell/3$. The wall chart is drawn for both on its plane of $\psi$ and $\xi$, with `CDL_INTO_FLAT` and `CDL_OUT_OF_FLAT` for $\rho$ on both sides of the wall, the open universe for a new vacuum of zero, positive and negative energy, the conformal time chart for the negative one, and each static chart for both bubbles from $t = 0$, with the wall the edge of its domain, $r_w(t)$; `--verify` checks the wall chart's rays against $\psi \pm X$ with `cdl_conformal_distance`, the open universe's against $\ln(c\tau)$, $\ln\tanh(c\tau/2\ell)$ and $\ln\tan(c\tau/2\ell) \pm \chi$, and the static charts' against $ct \pm r_*$, and `_tools/derivations/coleman_de_luccia.md` is the derivation.
Senovilla's universe is drawn at $a = 1$ on its plane of $t$ and $\rho$, conformally flat, from $act = -1.5$ to $1.5$ and out to $\rho = 2/a$, with the radius $\cosh(3a\rho) = 2$ of its one circular light ray and the event on the axis where its density is greatest; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \pm \rho$.
The gravastar is drawn at $r_s = 1$ with its shell at $R = 5r_s/4$ round a ball of de Sitter space of radius $L = 2\,r_s$, where $C = 64/195$ and the shell has a positive surface density: the plane of $t$ and $r$ inside the shell and the line through the centre, the plane of $t$ and the tortoise coordinate $x$, where every ray runs at 45° and the chart is hatched beyond the shell at $x = 2.56\,r_s$, and Schwarzschild's plane from the shell out, each marking the shell; `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp x$ with $x = (L/\sqrt{C})\,\mathrm{artanh}(r/L)$ and against $ct \mp r_*$, and `_tools/derivations/gravastar.md` is the derivation.
Mass inflation is drawn as Ori's model at the numbers of `_tools/derivations/ori_shell.py`, in units of the final mass $m_0$: $r_q = 0.96\,m_0$, the charge of Reissner-Nordström's diagrams, Price's tail $m = m_0 - (m_0/50)(v_0/v)^{11}$ from $v_0 = 10\,m_0$ on, and a shell of mass $m_0/50$ crossing $v_0$ at $r = m_0$. The ingoing chart has two views against $v - r$: the tail falling in, with the event horizon and the ray the shell runs along marked, and the chart behind the shell, whose mass function is the declared function `ori_mass_behind`, integrated along the shell, and whose advanced time is zero on the Cauchy horizon, hatched by `where` beyond the shell and beyond $v = 0$; `--verify` checks the outgoing rays of both against `ori_shell.py`'s own integrators, and `_tools/derivations/mass_inflation.md` is the derivation. The double null and advanced charts leave their functions free and have no view.
