Hiscock's evaporating black hole is drawn in units of the mass $m_0$ of a shell of light that falls in along $v = 0$: `HISCOCK_MASS` falls as $\cos^2(\pi(v - 2)/12)$ from $v_1 = 2$ to $v_0 = 8$, so that $dm/dv \to 0$ as $m \to 0$, the surface of pair creation is $R = 3m$, and the outgoing chart's mass is the same mass on that surface, where $u = v - 18m$, a declared function, `hiscock_m_out`, with the chart's inner edge `hiscock_r_out`. The ingoing chart draws that model inside the surface, with `where` leaving what lies beyond it hatched and empty, and the simplest model, two shells at $v = 0$ and $6\,m_0$, with its Cauchy horizon; the event horizon is traced back from the last point of the singularity, since outgoing rays converge on it toward the past. The outgoing chart draws the radiation between $u_1 = -16$ and $u_0 = 8$, and the flat chart the space left after $u_0$. A row's `where` may name a declared function, and `zero_set` marks no curve where `where` is not positive; `--verify` checks the rays against $v$ and $u$, and `_tools/derivations/hiscock.md` is the derivation.
Lifshitz spacetime is drawn at $z = 2$, Kachru, Liu and Mulligan's own case, in units of $L$, on its plane of the time and the radial coordinate in each of its six charts, $x$ and $y$ held fixed: the cones opening toward the boundary in $r$, $u$ and $\rho$, the 45° rays of the tortoise chart, and the two null charts against $v - r$ and $v - s/2$, time functions where $r < 2^{1/3}L$ and $s < \sqrt{2}\,L$, on boxes that stay inside those. The inverse radius chart also draws three planes of $t$ and $x$, at $u = L/2$, $L$ and $2L$, whose null lines have the slopes $(L/u)^{z-1} = 2$, $1$ and $1/2$ and whose captions say they are no null geodesics for $z > 1$. `--verify` checks the rays against $ct \mp w$ with $w = L^{z+1}/(zr^z)$, against $v$ and $v + 2w$, and against $ct \pm ux/L$, and `_tools/derivations/lifshitz_spacetime.md` is the derivation.
Herdeiro and Radu's black hole with scalar hair is drawn as their configuration IV, $r_H = 0.1/\mu$ and $\omega = 0.82\,\mu c$ with $m = 1$, in units of the field's inverse length $\mu$, from the data they published, which `_tools/derivations/kerr_scalar_hair.py` reads into splines: on its axis, where the dragging drops out, and on its equator with $\varphi$ divided out, with the ergosurface at $0.365/\mu$ and the radius of the largest field marked, each row declaring the functions of its plane, `ksh_f0a` to `ksh_we` among `DECLARED_FUNCTIONS`, and reading no Kretschmann scalar; the Kerr member is drawn on the same two planes for Kerr's black hole of the same mass and angular momentum, $r_H = 0.982/\mu$ and $b = 0.442/\mu$; `_tools/test_kerr_scalar_hair.py` checks the rays against the tortoise coordinates, and `_tools/derivations/kerr_scalar_hair.md` is the derivation.
Wahlquist's fluid is drawn in its own rest frame at $k = 3/10$ and $b = 4/5$, in units of $r_0$, where $\eta_0 = 1.0246$ and $\gamma = 1.0288$: the equatorial plane $\eta = 0$ from the ring to the surface of zero pressure and the disc $\xi = 0$ from the ring to the centre, each a surface of mirror symmetry with $\phi$ divided out, the same body on the equatorial plane of Mars's two charts at $Q_0 = r_0^2$, $\nu_0 = 1$, $a_1 = a_2 = 0$, $\beta = k/r_0$ and $\mu_0 = k^2/(br_0)^2$, and Whittaker's sphere with the same $b$ on its plane of $t$ and $X$ and through its centre; `--verify` checks the equatorial plane and the sphere against `wahlquist_star`, quadratures of Wahlquist's formulas written out independently of the file.

### Checking the rays

    .venv.noindex/bin/python _tools/derivations/null_rays.py --verify

traces rays as the page's files are traced and measures, for every view with a closed form, how far the quantity each family should conserve drifts along a ray, together with the dust solutions and the equality of Natario's and Alcubierre's metrics on the plane of their axis.
For the principal null rays it adds the checks the next section describes, and the closed forms $ct \mp r_*$ and $\phi \mp r_\sharp$ of Kerr and Kerr-Newman, which the drawing never uses.
On each cylinder of $t$ and $\phi$, van Stockum's, Gödel's, Som and Raychaudhuri's and the two circles of Bonnor's dust cloud, it checks the straight lines against the slopes the captions state, and checks what light launched along each family does against what the caption says, from the published Christoffel symbols: stays on the cylinder as a null geodesic, or is turned toward the axis or away from it.
It writes nothing and exits non-zero if anything fails; run it after changing the method.
`--metric <metric_id>` checks only that spacetime's closed forms and dust, and leaves out the checks of turning and of the principal null rays.
It took 570 seconds on 29 September 2026.

### Rays that leave the plane

Off its axis, a light ray of Kerr that runs straight in or straight out turns in $\phi$ as it goes, so no plane of two coordinates holds it.
Such rays are the principal null congruence, and a row with `principal=True` draws it from the published Weyl tensor rather than from a formula for it.
At each point the script takes the Weyl tensor as an operator on bivectors, in a frame the published metric makes orthonormal, requires it to be of Petrov type D, and finds its principal plane: the timelike plane whose two null directions are the Weyl tensor's repeated principal null directions.
That plane's metric, in the basis whose projection onto the two drawn coordinates is their coordinate basis, takes the place of the coordinate plane's metric in the same null condition, so the tracing, the two families, the orientation rules and the cones are the plane method's own, and a cone is the future cone of the principal plane, as the legend says.
The row names in `leaves` the coordinates the rays move in off the drawn pair, and the script refuses the row if the published metric or the Weyl tensor depends on one of them, since only then are the drawn curves the projections of single rays.
It also refuses, naming the point, where the Weyl tensor is not of type D, where the principal plane has a part along a coordinate held fixed, or where it does not project one to one onto the drawn pair.

Kerr and Kerr-Newman each get two such views on the equator, where the ergoregion is widest and the ring singularity lies.
One is the projection onto $t$ and $r$, which carries the cones and both horizons; the other is the equatorial plane seen from above, $r$ and $\phi$ drawn as polar coordinates by `to_display=POLAR`, which carries the turning.
$r$ changes monotonically along every principal ray, so the two projections, which share it, fix each ray.
The view from above seeds twelve rays of each family evenly around the circle inscribed in its box, and `inside=True` stops each a thousandth of the drawing short of the edge of the domain, $r_+$, where $\phi$ winds without end.
Both views mark the ergosurface, $g_{tt} = 0$, with `mark_gtt`, whose marker carries its own legend.
Beside a curvature singularity the published components lose to rounding the digits the principal plane is found from, a direction error of rounding over $r^4$ beside Kerr's ring, so the plane is not taken where the Kretschmann scalar passes $10^{12}$ in the view's units, $r = 0.006\,GM/c^2$ beside the ring and under a pixel from it, and a ray stops there as it does wherever the metric is not finite.

Before a principal view is written, the script checks at points along the drawn $r$ that its rays satisfy the geodesic equation with the published Christoffel symbols and that their directions satisfy $C_{abc[d}k_{e]}k^bk^c = 0$ with the published Weyl tensor, and refuses to write the view if either misses.
`--verify` adds the closed forms, shows that the projection onto $t$ and $r$ is the same at $\theta = \pi/5$ as on the equator and equals the plane of $t$ and $r$ on the axis, shows that in Schwarzschild, Reissner-Nordstrom and Taub-NUT the principal plane is the plane of $t$ and $r$ their radial views already draw, and shows the script refusing van Stockum, whose Weyl tensor is of type I.

Kerr and Kerr-Newman are the only charts the extension draws something new for.
Every spherically symmetric chart that is not conformally flat has the plane of $t$ and $r$ as its principal plane, and so does Taub-NUT, whose twist sits in $g_{t\phi}$; Gödel's is the plane of $t$ and $z$ along its axis of rotation, which is flat.
van Stockum's Weyl tensor has four distinct principal directions and no repeated one, so its light rays, which frame dragging turns out of the plane of $t$ and $r$, form no principal congruence, and the pp-wave's one repeated direction, $\partial_v$, lies in the plane of its axis, already drawn.

### Rays of no angular momentum

The rotating BTZ hole turns its light rays in $\phi$ as Kerr does, and between its ergosurface and its outer horizon the plane of $t$ and $r$ at fixed $\phi$ has no null direction; its Weyl tensor vanishes, as every Weyl tensor in three dimensions does, so it has no principal directions either.
A row with `quotient="phi"` divides the circles of $\phi$ out: the plane's metric is $h_{ab} = g_{ab} - g_{a\phi}g_{b\phi}/g_{\phi\phi}$, the part of the published metric orthogonal to the circles, and it takes the place of the coordinate plane's metric in the null condition, as the principal plane's does for Kerr.
Its rays are the shadows on $t$ and $r$ of the null geodesics with no angular momentum, each lifted into the spacetime by $d\phi = -(g_{\phi t}dt + g_{\phi r}dr)/g_{\phi\phi}$, and the upper left block of the published inverse metric is the inverse of $h$, so the horizons marked from $g^{rr}$ are the zeros of $h$'s own.
The script refuses such a row unless every coordinate is drawn, held fixed or divided out and nothing published depends on the one divided out, and before it writes the view it checks every lifted direction null against the published metric and geodesic by the published Christoffel symbols, which it stamps with the view; the header's "Rays of no angular momentum" gives the details.
The BTZ hole's three charts each draw the hole without rotation on the plane $\phi = 0$ and the rotating one with $\phi$ divided out, at $M = 1$ and $J = 4\ell/5$, where $r_\pm^2 = 4\ell^2/5$ and $\ell^2/5$.

### Figures in three dimensions

Where the causal structure a reader comes for turns in a direction no plane of two coordinates holds, a coordinate system also gets a figure in three dimensions: a slice of one time and two spatial coordinates, every other coordinate held fixed, drawn from the published metric by `_tools/derivations/projections.py` and projected once from a fixed camera.
The file holds the projection, polylines, polygons and points of the page's own plane in the order they are painted, farthest first, with TeX labels, which is the form a conformal diagram takes.
So the page draws a figure with the conformal diagram's frame and labels, it prints, and an application can draw the same data with its own renderer.
A figure of light cones also carries its pieces in three dimensions under `turn`, from which the page draws it again from whatever side the reader turns it to.
`FIGURES` and `CAPTIONS` in that script are its table, and `null_rays.py` writes each figure into its spacetime's diagram file under `projections`, keyed by coordinate system beside the flat views under `systems`, which an application that reads only the flat views passes over.
A figure is stamped with the fields it was drawn from, as a flat view is.

van Stockum's cylinder, the Gödel universe, in Gödel's cylindrical chart, and Som and Raychaudhuri's universe each get the slice $z = 0$ of $t$, $r$ and $\phi$, with future light cones on the axis and at four places around each of the circles $r_c/2$, $r_c$ and $3r_c/2$, where $r_c$ is the zero of the published $g_{\phi\phi}$.
The cones tip over toward $+\phi$, one edge of each lies along the circle at $r_c$, and beyond it the circle is a closed timelike curve.
van Stockum's radius is drawn as the proper distance from the axis and Gödel's and Som and Raychaudhuri's as $r$ itself, since their $g_{rr} = -g_{tt}$, so that in all three the null directions straight out from the axis run at 45°.
Each also gets the cylinders of $t$ and $\phi$ at $r_c/2$ and $3r_c/2$ as flat views, drawn by the plane method with $\phi$ scaled by $r$ and `periodic` keeping the cylinder's two edges, which are one line, from being hatched.
Kerr and Kerr-Newman each get the equatorial slice of $t$, $r$ and $\phi$, drawn with $r$ as the radius down to the horizon, where the chart ends, with cones at four places around each of three circles: halfway through the ergoregion, on the ergosurface and at $3r_E/2$.
The horizon is the outer zero of the published $g^{rr}$ and the ergosurface the zero of the published $g_{tt}$ outside it, and before the figure is drawn every future generator on the inner circle is checked to move toward $+\phi$, none on the ergosurface to move back, and some on the outer circle to do so.
That is what no plane can show, since inside the ergoregion the plane of $t$ and $r$ at fixed $\phi$ has no null direction at all.
A figure of the principal null congruence alone would add nothing to the two flat views: $r$ changes monotonically along every principal ray, and its $t(r)$ and $\phi(r)$ are the same at every $\theta$, so the two projections fix every ray at every latitude.
In Boyer-Lindquist $t$ the cones near the hole are narrow, so they are drawn larger than van Stockum's, and with a cone every 45° round the ergosurface no label fits beside its circles, so the legend names them.
Alcubierre's bubble gets the slice $z = 0$ of $t$, $x$ and $y$ at the moment its declared profile centres it on $x = 0$, drawn cartesian with $t$ up: cones at the centre and around the circles $r_s = R/2$, $v_sf = 1$ and $r_s = 2R$, and the centre's world line $x = 2ct$.
Tippett and Tsang's bubble gets the slice $z = 0$ of $t$, $x$ and $y$, `time_machine_ring`, drawn cartesian with $t$ up: the wall $h = 1/2$ as the four circles where it meets $y = 0$ and $\xi = A$ and its sections at twelve angles of the plane of $t$ and $x$, each checked against the declared $h$; the circle $\xi = A$, checked timelike all the way round against the published metric; and cones at eight places round it, oriented by $\partial_\lambda$, at four on the axis of the ring and at four outside, oriented by $t$. `generators` takes a vector of the slice's coordinates for `orient` where a spacetime has no arrow of time of its own, and checks it timelike.
The circle $v_sf = 1$ is the zero of the published $g_{tt}$, the same circle about $x = 2ct$ a little later is checked to be its zero there too, and the world line is checked against the published metric to have $g(u, u) = -1$, so that its clock keeps $t$.
The flat view on the axis shows the tilt along the direction of motion; the figure shows the same shift tipping every cone along $x$ off the axis too, so that the cones tip over in a ball about the ship.
Natário's drive has no such figure yet, Lentz's soliton cannot be written down, and the Krasnikov tube's causal structure lies along its axis, where the plane of $t$ and $x$ already draws it.
The cosmic string's figure is its plane $z = 0$ seen from straight overhead with $t$ left out: a beam of light rays run with the published Christoffel symbols past the string, laid flat with the angle $(1 - 4G\mu/c^2)\phi$ so that each ray is checked straight, and carried across the wedge the deficit removes.
Gott's time machine gets the slice $z = 0$ of $t$, $x$ and $y$ in the centre of momentum chart, drawn cartesian with $t$ up: the two strings' world lines, the two faces of each wedge at one time of its string's rest frame, and a closed timelike curve round both strings, $A$ to $C$, across the wedge to the same event on the other face at an earlier $t$, on to $B$, and back below the lower string, with future light cones where its four stretches start.
The spinning string gets the slice $z = 0$ of $t$, $r$ and $\phi$ in the proper radius chart, drawn by `about_string` as van Stockum's is with the closed timelike curves on the inside: cones at four places around each of the circles $r_c/2$, $r_c$ and $3r_c/2$, none on the string, where the metric is singular, and the moment the embedding diagram draws as the floor from $r_c$ out.
Bonnor's rotating dust cloud gets the slice $z = 0$ of $t$, $\rho$ and $\phi$ in its cylindrical chart, drawn by `about_string` with $\rho$ itself as the radius and the arrows pointing clockwise, since inside $\rho = a$ the circles are future directed toward $-\phi$: cones at four places around each of the circles $a/2$, $a$ and $3a/2$, none on the axis, whose point of this slice is the singularity, and the moment the embedding diagram draws as the floor from $a$ out.
The point particle in three dimensions gets the cosmic string's figure in its conical chart, `string_rays` at $\alpha = 3/4$, where the two halves of the beam cross at a right angle, and in its moving chart the whole of $t$, $x$ and $y$ drawn cartesian with $t$ up by `moving_wedge`: the world line of a particle at $v = 3c/5$ and the two faces of the wedge it trails at three times, each pair of identified events joined, which the script checks to share $t$ and $x$, to be carried onto one another by the rotation through $\delta$ in the rest frame, and to stand $2\arctan(\gamma\tan(\delta/2)) = 103°$ apart.
Kerr-de Sitter gets Kerr's figure for $\Lambda > 0$, `ergoregion` with the brackets of its own horizon and ergosurface in units of $r_s$: the floor stops short of the second ergosurface, at $3.173\,r_s$ beside the cosmological horizon.
The draining bathtub gets the whole of its laboratory chart, `bathtub`, drawn polar with $t$ up: cones of sound at four places round the horizon, the ergosurface and $r = 3.2|A|/c$, each generator checked null, none on the horizon leading out and one on the ergosurface standing vertical, over a floor that is the embedding diagram's plane with six streamlines of the water on it; it is all lines and cones, so it turns.
Kerr-Taub-NUT gets Kerr's figure as well, `ergoregion` with the brackets of its own horizon and ergosurface in units of $m$; its equator is not totally geodesic, which its caption says.
Ernst and Wild's hole in Melvin's universe gets Kerr's figure too, `ergoregion` with the brackets of its own horizon and ergosurface in units of $m$, and a second one, `ergo_tube`: its meridional plane seen from the side with $t$ and $\phi$ left out, $r$ and $\theta$ as polar coordinates about the centre, in the stronger field $B = 3/(4m)$, with the region where the published $g_{tt}$ is positive filled, round the hole and in a tube each way along the axis, the ergosurface its edge, and the hole covered. Before it draws, it checks $g_{tt}$ positive beside the horizon on the equator and negative all along the axis, the region round the hole closed and apart from the tubes, $g_{tt}$ growing as the square of the height at a fixed distance from the axis, and $g(K, K) > 0$ far enough up the tube for $K = \partial_t + \Omega\,\partial_\phi$ at each of four constant rates $\Omega$; it is drawn straight onto the page, so it has no `turn`.
The black hole in a swirling universe and the swirling universe each get `ergo`, `swirl_ergo` in `projections.py`: the meridional plane seen from the side with $t$ and $\phi$ left out, polar about the hole at $j = 1/(16m^2)$ and Cartesian in $\rho$ and $z$ at $j = 1$, with the region where the published $g_{tt}$ is positive filled, one region above the equatorial plane and its mirror image below. Before it draws, it checks $g_{tt}$ negative on the axis, on the equatorial plane and beside the horizon, the same at a height and its mirror image, the ergosurface one curve each side ending on the box's top or bottom, apart from the horizon, $g_{tt}$ growing as the square of the height beside the axis, and in the universe the ergosurface on Gibbons, Mujtaba and Pope's $|4jz\rho| = 1 + j^2\rho^4$, lowest at $\rho = (3j^2)^{-1/4}$; it is drawn straight onto the page, so it has no `turn`.
Every stretch of Gott's curve is checked timelike and future directed against the published metric, the two ends of each crossing to lie on the faces at one rest time and to be carried onto one another by the rotation through the deficit angle, and the curve to close; it is all lines and cones, so it turns.
The throat of extreme Kerr gets the slice $\theta = \pi/2$ of $\tau$, $y$ and $\phi$ in the global chart, `throat`, drawn polar by `ThroatSlice` with each circle of constant $y$ at the radius $2r_0 + r_0\,\mathrm{arsinh}(y)/\sqrt{2}$, the proper distance along the throat: cones at four places around each of the circles $y = 0$, $y = \pm 1/\sqrt{3}$, the zeros of the published $g_{\tau\tau}$, and $y = \pm 3/2$, checked to turn both ways on the middle circle, to stand with one edge vertical on the dotted ones and to turn one way only beyond them, toward $-\phi$ where $y$ is positive and $+\phi$ where it is negative; it turns, as Kerr's does.
Bonnor's beam of light gets its plane $y = 0$ seen from the side with $t$ left out, `beam_rays`: light sent with the uniform beam runs straight, and each ray sent against it, integrated in the Cartesian chart with the published Christoffel symbols, is checked null, checked to keep the rate of $ct - z$ and $\dot x^2 + A(\dot t - \dot z)^2$, Bonnor's first integrals, and, where it stays inside the beam, checked against $b\cos(u/2R)$; it is drawn straight onto the page, so it has no other side and no `turn`.
Lifshitz spacetime gets its plane $y = 0$ of the inverse radius chart seen the same way, `lifshitz_rays`, with $x$ across and the depth $u$ down from the boundary: nine rays from one event at $u = 2L$, $15°$ apart, each integrated with the published Christoffel symbols in a parameter $\sigma$ with $d\lambda = d\sigma/u^2$, since the boundary lies at an infinite affine distance, and checked null, checked to keep its energy and its momentum along $x$, and checked against the catenary $u = u_0\cosh((x - x_0)/u_0)$ with $u_0 = 2L\sin\alpha$, Keeler, Knodel and Liu's turning point; the same rays at $z = 1$, from the same published metric, are checked to be straight lines that all reach the boundary. It has no other side and no `turn`.
The maximally supersymmetric plane wave gets the same figure in each of its three charts, `msw_rays`: the surface where every direction across the wave but the first is zero, seen from the side with the null coordinate left out, with light leaving the origin at slopes $\pm 1/2$, $\pm 1$ and $\pm 2$ and light running parallel to the axis at $\mu x = \pm 1/2$, $\pm 1$ and $\pm 3/2$, each ray integrated with the published Christoffel symbols, checked null, checked to reach the edge of its window, and checked against its closed form: $s\sin(\mu cu)$ and $a\cos(\mu cu)$ in the Brinkmann chart, where the first meet again at $\mu cu = \pi$ and $2\pi$, $y_1 = s$ and $a\cot(\mu cu)$ in the Rosen chart, and straight lines in the conformally flat chart; it is drawn straight onto the page, so it has no `turn`.
Schrödinger spacetime's global chart gets its surface $X = 0$ seen from the side with $V$ left out, `trap_rays`: five null geodesics launched at $T = 0$ with no velocity along $R$, integrated with the published Christoffel symbols and each checked null, of constant momentum along $V$, and on $R^2 = R_0^2\cos^2T + \sin^2T/R_0^2$, swinging in the trap between $R_0$ and $\beta^2/R_0$, with the edges $\omega T = \pm\pi/2$ of the Poincaré chart and the boundary $R = 0$; it is drawn straight onto the page, so it has no `turn`.
Kundt's waves get the slice $Y = 0$ of $T$, $X$ and $Z$ in the Kerr-Schild chart, `kundt_fronts`, drawn cartesian with $T$ up: the envelope $X^2 + Z^2 = c^2T^2$ as its circles at $cT = \ell$ and $2\ell$, six fronts $\alpha = \pm 30°$, $\pm 90°$ and $\pm 150°$, each the half line $cT(\sin\alpha, \cos\alpha) + x(\cos\alpha, -\sin\alpha)$ at those two times with the null line where it touches the cone, and two rays on each; every event drawn is checked against the chart's own $x$ and $u = \tan(\alpha/2)$, read from its definitions, and every ray to be null by the published metric. It is all lines, so it turns.
The wormhole time machine gets the slice $Y = 0$ of $T$, $Z$ and $X$ in the Lorentz chart of the flat space outside the mouths, drawn cartesian with $T$ up and $Z$ across, the mouths as their world lines: the left mouth at rest, the right one on its round trip, a dotted line joining the two at every $9\,r_0/c$ of proper time, the closed null geodesic from the left mouth at $c\tau = 41.8\,r_0$ to the right mouth at the same $\tau$, the Cauchy horizon as twelve generators and the rim of the future light cone of the event that geodesic leaves from, and a closed timelike curve from the left mouth at $c\tau = 60\,r_0$ to the right mouth at the same $\tau$.
Each step of both world lines is checked timelike and of the proper time it is marked with, the closed geodesic null, the mouths at one proper time spacelike separated before it and timelike after, and the closed curve timelike and future directed; the tests hold the published figure to the same physics, and `_tools/derivations/wormhole_time_machine.md` says why the horizon is that cone for small mouths.

A cone is the convex hull of its apex and its rim, every generator one Euclidean length in the drawing, so its size says nothing and its shape and tilt are the metric's; every generator is checked null against the published metric, and the published inverse is checked to be the inverse of the published metric on the slice.
Beyond $r_c$ the cones are so wide that from most directions the camera looks into their opening, and a cone projects to an oval with its apex inside; eight of its generators are therefore drawn faintly from the apex to the rim, which is what shows where the apex is and which way the cone opens.
A cone whose axis points along the camera's own direction is the worst case, so the cones on $r_c$ are turned by 45° from those on the other circles and none is placed where its neighbour's rim would cover it.

No two labels of a figure overlap at its own camera, by the conformal diagrams' rule, `slices.clear_labels`, which `projections.py` keeps before it writes and the tests hold every figure on disk to.
A label stands at a point of the figure and is moved off it only by its offset, which is in the page's units and so holds from every side the figure is turned to: the name of the wormhole time machine's proper times, $c\tau/r_0$, stands under the number $0$ at its point, its top a third of a number's height below that number's box, the room a spacetime diagram keeps between its numbers, where until 3 October 2026 it stood $2.5\,r_0$ lower in $T$, which the camera drew over the $0$.

Every class a figure paints needs a style in `_layouts/mfs.html`, as `.pj-<class>` on the screen and under `.mfs-print-body` in print, and a fill as `.pj-<class>-fill`.
A path the stylesheet does not know is painted as nothing at all, so a test holds every class of every figure to having both.

A coordinate system with neither a flat view nor a figure has no section on the page, and a spacetime with neither has no diagram file, which a full redraw removes if one is left behind; `build_mfs_data.py` refuses a diagram file that draws nothing.

### Turning a figure of light cones

The figures of Gödel, van Stockum, Som and Raychaudhuri, the spinning string, Bonnor's dust cloud, Kerr, Kerr-Newman, Kerr-de Sitter, the throat of extreme Kerr and Alcubierre carry `turn`, every piece of the figure in the drawing's $(X, Y, T)$, $T$ up, at six decimals, and the page turns them by the same hand as an embedding diagram.
The cosmic string's beam lies in the plane $T = 0$ seen from straight above, in the plane's own flat coordinates, so it has no other side and no `turn`.
Nor do the two figures of light rays seen from the side with $t$ left out, Bonnor's beam and Lifshitz spacetime's rays.
`turn` holds:

- `lines`: every line the figure draws before its cones, in the order painted, each `{"class", "points"}`, a closed curve ending on its first point.
- `cones`: every future light cone, each `{"class", "apex", "rim"}`, its apex and the ends of its generators, all of one Euclidean length, in the order the figure paints them at its own camera, farthest first.
- `ribs`: how many of each cone's generators are drawn from the apex to the rim, evenly round it; every rim's length is a multiple of it.
- `labels`: where each of `labels` stands, in order: `{"at": [X, Y, T]}`, a point it keeps from every side, or `{"circle": [rho, T], "angle": delta}`, the circle of radius $\rho$ about the axis at height $T$ that it names, at the point of that circle $\delta$ degrees round from the camera's azimuth, counterclockwise seen from above, so it keeps to the side of the circle nearest the reader.
- `slices`: each of `slices` in three dimensions, in order, its `lines` and `fills` as the published slice has them.
- `centre`: the point the figure turns about, on the axis halfway between the lowest and the highest point it draws.

At another camera, azimuth $a$ and elevation $e$, with the vectors `right`, `up` and `toward` of `projections.Camera`:

- Every line is projected and thinned by Ramer-Douglas-Peucker to 0.0005 of the box's units.
- Every cone is painted after the lines, farthest first by `toward` at its apex, two cones whose apexes lie within $10^{-5}$ of the box's larger side of each other in depth keeping their order in `cones`.
  A cone is the convex hull of its projected apex and rim, each point taken to twelve decimals, filled as `cone`; its rim, closed, as `cone-rim`; every generator from the first round to the last a `ribs`th of the rim apart, from the apex, as `cone-rib`; where the apex is a corner of the hull, the two edges of the hull that meet there, as `cone`; and its apex, as the point `cone-apex`.
- Each slice's lines and rings are projected and thinned as a line is, and drawn under everything else, as the published slice is.
- A label stands at its point, or on its circle at $\phi = a + \delta$, with its anchor and offset as published; a label on a circle gives way while it would overlap a label before it, as a label with `ring` does on an embedding diagram.
- The figure turns about `centre`, which stays where the figure's own camera puts it on the page.
  It is drawn at one scale $s$, never above 1, the largest that keeps it within the height it had at its own camera and within the width it had on each side of its axis, and moved up or down only as far as that height needs; the `box` stays as it is, and every point drawn stays inside it.

At the figure's own camera these rules give back the published figure, the same layers in the same order, every point of a cone and every label to the published rounding and every line within its thinning.
`_tools/turn_check.cjs` holds the page's drawing of every figure to that, and to its box from every side.

### What is not drawn

Kerr's and Kerr-Newman's planes of $t$ and $r$ are drawn on the axis only, and off it their principal null rays are drawn instead.
Off the axis the null curves of fixed $\theta$ and $\phi$ are not null geodesics, since $\Gamma^\theta{}_{tt}$, $\Gamma^\theta{}_{rr}$ and $\Gamma^\phi{}_{tr}$ turn a light ray launched along one out of the plane, and inside the ergoregion the plane has no null direction at all, which is why the equator is also drawn in three dimensions.
van Stockum's plane of $t$ and $r$ is not drawn: every null geodesic leaves it, and its Weyl tensor, of type I, gives no congruence to draw them by, so its cylinders of $t$ and $\phi$ and its figure are drawn instead.
The proper distance chart of Morris-Thorne leaves its functions free with no choice made for them, while its spherical chart draws the Ellis-Bronnikov member.
Lense and Thirring's weak field chart of the slowly rotating star holds where $m/r$ is small, and there its planes are flat to the eye.
Natário's plane flow chart, the Brinkmann chart of the pp-wave, Robinson and Trautman's chart of $x$ and $y$, Minkowski's double null chart, the Cartesian and null cylindrical charts of the Aichelburg-Sexl shock, and the global and conformal charts of the domain wall would each only repeat a plane drawn elsewhere, flat or the same as another chart's.

Nothing at all is drawn of Lentz's soliton, the Mixmaster universe or Gott's core.
Lentz's soliton exists only as a numerical integral over his rhomboid sources, so no soliton of the class can be written down, and a potential written in its place would draw another soliton of the class.
The Mixmaster's scale factors reach the singularity through an endless sequence of Kasner epochs, which a drawing of one solution follows only for a handful, and the one plane of time and an Euler angle that keeps its light rays, $t$ against $\psi$, would follow $a_3$ alone.
Gott's core, the cosmic string's interior, is flat on its plane of $t$ and $\chi$, and acts on light across it, in the cap of $\chi$ and $\phi$.
The Eguchi-Hanson space is Riemannian, with no time and no null direction in any chart, so it has no spacetime diagram; what it has to show, the bolt and the boundary at infinity, is in the three surfaces of its embedding diagram.
The gravitational instantons of 1977 and 1978 are Riemannian too, every chart of them, so they have no spacetime diagram; each chart's nut or bolt is in its own view of the embedding diagram.
Misner's and Brill and Lindquist's initial data for two black holes are one moment, a Riemannian space of three dimensions, so they have no spacetime diagram either; the two throats are in the three views of the embedding diagram.

## Conformal diagrams

`MFS/assets/data/conformal/<metric_id>.json` holds the conformal diagram of one spacetime: the whole spacetime brought to a finite drawing with light at 45°, or, where no picture of the whole is faithful, a totally geodesic surface in it that says so.
The page draws it under the heading "conformal diagram", just below the spacetime diagram, and it follows the chart chosen at the top: it shows the views that name that chart, then the views that name no chart, with buttons only when that makes more than one.
A chart none of whose views is its own shows no conformal diagram at all, as Vaidya's outgoing chart does, since the one drawn is the shell imploding in the ingoing chart.
The application reads the same files.

`_tools/derivations/conformal.py` draws them, one function per spacetime, each carrying its derivation in its docstring, reading the published metric through the checker's `Reader` with the `load` and `published_matrix` the null rays use.
It needs the same environment as `null_rays.py` and takes a few seconds for the whole collection:

    .venv.noindex/bin/python _tools/derivations/conformal.py
    python3 _tools/build_mfs_data.py

`--metric <metric_id>` redraws one spacetime, and `--verify` prints every check and writes nothing.
A drawing added with `--metric` alone never runs the others, so run the whole collection once before a commit: a new function spliced into the end of its neighbour takes that neighbour's `return views`, and the redraw of everything is what fails.
`_tools/test_derivations_source.py` reads the scripts without running them and holds every drawing to a closing `return` and every module in `_tools/derivations` to one definition of each top-level function and class, since Python keeps the later of two in silence.

Every map drawn is checked by the function that draws with it, at random points of the region it covers, against the published metric and inverse metric: lines of constant drawn null coordinate are light rays, the two families are distinct, the future is up, and the published inverse on the surface is the inverse of the published metric there.
Each spacetime adds the limits that place its horizons, infinities and singularities, and the published Kretschmann scalar must diverge wherever a line is drawn as a singularity and stay finite on every centre or throat drawn as regular.
The script refuses to write anything while one check fails; the docstring lists them.

A file records every coordinate system it read and a stamp over the fields it read from each, in `source`, computed by the same `diagram_source_version` as the null rays' stamps.
A diagram can read another spacetime's metric, as the interior Schwarzschild star reads the exterior of `schwarzschild.json`, and `build_mfs_data.py` refuses the file when any of those fields changes, naming the system and the command that redraws it.
The domains are not among the fields, since no conformal diagram reads one.

`DRAWN` in the script names the spacetimes that have a diagram and `NOT_DRAWN` those that have none: every event's future is the whole spacetime for Gödel, van Stockum, and Som and Raychaudhuri, the diagram is whatever a free function makes it for the warp drives, the Krasnikov tube, Tolman-Bondi, Szekeres and Bianchi, the Mixmaster has no surface that carries its causal structure, the Eguchi-Hanson space is Riemannian and has no causal structure at all, as are the gravitational instantons of 1977 and 1978 and Misner's and Brill and Lindquist's initial data for two black holes, one moment with no spacetime known around it, and so on.
Black Saturn has none either: no published chart covers the inside of its two horizons, and its Weyl coordinates end on them, so nothing of the diagram past the exterior could be read from a metric.
Neugebauer and Meinel's disc is drawn on its axis, the whole of it through the centre of the disc, a diamond with null infinity on all four sides, at $\mu = 3$ and $\ell = 12\,\rho_0$, and on the axis of its limit $\mu \to \mu_0$, a diamond with the horizon $r = 0$ on its left, $r_* = r + 2m\ln(r/m) - 2m^2/r$; $e^{2U}$ on the axis enters as numbers from `nm_disc.axis`.
Majumdar and Papapetrou's diagram is one hole's, the extremal Reissner-Nordström tower, its exteriors read from the isotropic chart and its interiors from `rn_metric.json` at $r_q = r_s/2$; its horizon has no surface gravity, so each region is placed by $p, q = \arctan$ of $u$ and $v$, shifted by $\pi$ from region to region, and the two-hole charts show no conformal diagram.
Misner space is drawn in the plane $y = z = 0$ of the Minkowski space that covers it, one view per chart, each tinting one copy of the spacetime between two lines the boost carries onto one another; its views are drawn at $\psi_0 = 2$, since at the $\psi_0 = 4\pi$ of its other diagrams one copy stretches by $e^{2\pi}$ along each light ray and fills the drawing to within a pixel.
Gott's time machine is drawn in the plane $Y = z = 0$ of the Minkowski space it is away from the strings, one view for each of Grant's charts: the wedge $X > c|T|$ with the first three polarised hypersurfaces, each checked to hold events null separated from their images, and the two light cones of the origin, which no closed timelike curve enters; a circuit of both strings carries an event off the plane, so each event is drawn once.
Gowdy's torus universe is the part of Minkowski's triangle between $\theta = 0$ and $\theta = 2\pi$ under $p, q = \arctan(t \mp \theta)$, one view for the areal time and one for $\tau = -\ln t$, each checked for a random $\lambda$ at every sample, with the singularity on $T = 0$ and every ray ending at the one point $i^+$; its sphere chart is the square $X = \theta$, $T = t$ under $p, q = (t \mp \theta)/2$, drawn for the inside of Schwarzschild's horizon, with the Kretschmann scalar checked to diverge on $t = 0$ and to be $3/(4L^4)$ on the horizon $t = \pi$, and the two diagonals where the gradient of the orbit area is null.
The wormhole time machine is drawn on the plane $X = Y = 0$ through both mouths in its Lorentz chart, Minkowski's diamond with $p, q = \arctan((cT - 40\,r_0 \mp Z)/20\,r_0)$: the two mouths' world lines for the trip its other diagrams declare, the lines joining them at one proper time, the closed null geodesic, checked to keep $p$ from one mouth to the other, and the region to the future of the event it leaves from tinted as the events with a closed timelike curve through them.
Ori's time machine is drawn on the plane $x = y = 0$ through its central circle, in the flat plane that covers it, one view for each chart, by $p = \arctan u$ and $q = \arctan 2v$ of the Brinkmann chart's $u = -2e^{-z/2}$ and $v = Te^{z/2}$, at $L = 2$ as Misner's views are; the plane is checked totally geodesic from the published Christoffel symbols, and the null line $q = 0$ is in the quotient the closed null geodesic $N$.
The maximally supersymmetric plane wave is drawn on its axis, which Berenstein and Nastase's map carries onto the slice of the Einstein static universe along a great circle of its 9-sphere, Marolf and Ross's cylinder: $\psi$ up from $-\pi$ to $2\pi$ and $\beta$ once round across, its two edges one line, with $p = \arctan(2\mu v) - \pi/2$ and $q = \mu cu + \pi/2$ and the conformal boundary the null line $\psi - \beta \in 2\pi\mathbb{Z}$. Each chart's view tints the band of $u$ it covers, worked out in the three sectors one turn of $\beta$ carries into one another, and the cylinder's metric is checked to be $|e^{i\psi} - e^{i\beta}|^2$ times the published metric on the axis.
The Aichelburg-Sexl shock is drawn on the plane $x = \rho_0/8$, $y = 0$ of its null Cartesian chart as Minkowski's diamond with the shock on $p = 0$, where $q = \arctan(v - \Delta v\,\theta(u))$ carries each ray moving left across the shock as one line and breaks each line of constant $v$ by the jump $\Delta v = (8GE/c^4)\ln 8$, which is checked against the published $\Gamma^v{}_{uu}$; `Plane(..., off_shock=True)` reads the delta as zero, since the maps are checked only off the shock.
Hotta and Tanaka's shock is drawn on the plane $\theta = \pi/2$ through the equator of its wave front, one view shown under every chart, by $p = \arctan(u/a) - \pi/4$ and $q = \arctan((v - \Delta v\,\Theta(u))/a) + \pi/4$ of the Kruskal chart with $\Delta v = -a/2$ at $8GE/c^4 = a$: ahead of the shock it is de Sitter's square under its diagonal, and behind it future infinity rises to $T = \pi/2 + \arctan(1/2)$ and the pole $\chi = 0$ bends toward the shock, the tall diagram of Leblond, Marolf, and Myers. Both maps are checked against the Kruskal and the global charts, the jump against the published $\Gamma^v{}_{uu}$, the plane totally geodesic in both charts, and a ray leaving the pole $\chi = \pi$ before $\eta = 2\arctan(1/2)$ to reach the pole $\chi = 0$; `_tools/derivations/hotta_tanaka.md` Step 7 is the derivation.
Bonnor's beam of light is drawn on the three planes of it that no Christoffel symbol turns a ray out of, each flat and each Minkowski's diamond with $\ell = 4R$: the axis of the uniform beam in the Cartesian chart and in the null cylindrical chart inside the beam, and the plane midway between two beams in the null Cartesian chart, where $q = \arctan(\sqrt{2}(v + Au)/\ell)$ with $A = (1 + 2\ln 2)/4$; each is checked totally geodesic against the published Christoffel symbols, and the chart outside the beam has no such plane and no view.
Bonnor's stars of charged dust are each Minkowski's triangle, the star a timelike tube, with $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/2m)$ and $r_* = \int U^2$ times the flat length from the centre: the two spheres at $r_0 = 2m$ with the exterior in both radii on the tube of the sphere of 1975, Lemos and Weinberg's cloud at $b = m/2$ in its own chart and on the half line $x \ge 0$ of the harmonic chart, and the upper half of the spheroid's axis of symmetry; $g_{tt}$ and the radial component are checked continuous at each surface, each $r_*$ against its closed form, and the Kretschmann scalar finite at each centre, the spheroid's evaluated as it stands, since sympy is slow to simplify it.
The black holes of string theory with three and four charges are five views, one for each chart: Kruskal's block of the charts off extremality, by `StringHoleTower` with the tortoise coordinate of `slices.sbc_rstar`, drawn as far as $t$ and $r > 0$ reach, with the inner horizon $r = 0$ for the upper edges of the black hole; the whole Reissner-Nordström tower of five dimensions for the areal chart at $r_0 = 3r_q/4$; and the exterior diamond $p, q = \arctan(ct \mp r_*)$ of each extreme hole, its horizon on the two left edges.
Penrose's impulsive wave is drawn on the half plane across the string through the break, one picture under the retarded chart and the flat chart on either side: $p = \arctan u$ and $q = \arctan(u + 2r)$ of the retarded chart's equator, Minkowski's triangle with the front on $p = 0$ and the string on $\tan q = \beta^2\tan p$, the three maps checked against their charts and against one another along the front; the null chart's plane $\rho = 1$ is the part $V > 0$ of a diamond.
Visser's thin shell wormhole, at $a = 5r_s/4$, is the full diamond in the tortoise coordinate $\ell_* = \ell + r_s\ln(1 + |\ell|/(a - r_s))\mathrm{sgn}(\ell)$ of its chart through the throat, with the throat on its axis, and Schwarzschild's chart on one side is its right half.
The Khan-Penrose spacetime is drawn on its plane $x = y = 0$ in Khan and Penrose's four regions, one view per chart, with $p$ and $q$ each $u$ or $v$ itself where it is positive and its arctangent where it is negative.
Belinski and Zakharov's wave of two solitons is drawn on its plane $x = y = 0$, the upper half of Minkowski's diamond with $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp z)/w)$, once with the lines of its canonical chart and once with those of its pole chart, the singularity $t = 0$ along the bottom and the light cone of the event $t = 0$, $z = 0$ marked; the canonical map is checked for random values of $f$ and the pole chart's published Kretschmann scalar to diverge on $\tau = 0$.
Ahead of a wave the metric is the published one with that wave's coordinate set to zero, whose Riemann tensor the script computes and checks to vanish, and those flat regions end in the fold singularities $u = 1$ and $v = 1$, drawn as singularities with the curvature zero.
The Bell-Szekeres spacetime is drawn on two planes at $a = b = 1$: the plane $x = y = 0$ in its four regions as Khan and Penrose's is, for the double null chart and the chart of $\xi$ and $\eta$, with the fold singularities $au = \pi/2$ and $bv = \pi/2$ and the Killing-Cauchy horizon $au + bv = \pi/2$; and the plane $\eta = 0$, $x = 0$, the anti-de Sitter factor in its strip, for the regular, global, Kruskal-Szekeres and Bertotti-Robinson charts, where the horizon is the pair of null lines closing the triangle above the collision, and one event is checked to land on one point through all four maps.
The Chandrasekhar-Xanthopoulos waves are drawn on the plane $x = y = 0$ in their four regions as Bell and Szekeres's are, in the null coordinates $u$ and $v$ their conventions define, for the chart of $\eta$ and $\mu$, the chart of $\psi$ and $\lambda$ and the Boyer-Lindquist chart, with the fold singularities $u = \pi/2$ and $v = \pi/2$ and the Killing-Cauchy horizon $u + v = \pi/2$; the published metric with $v$ set to zero is checked to be a vacuum and curved, and the Kretschmann scalar on the horizon to be Kerr's on its inner horizon.
The domain wall is the whole spacetime, each point a 2-sphere: Minkowski's triangle cut at the hyperbola $R^2 - c^2T^2 = 1/k^2$, which Minkowski's maps send to the vertical line $X = \pi/2$, and its mirror image in that line, so the wall runs from the middle of $\mathscr{I}^-$ to the middle of $\mathscr{I}^+$ between two centres and there is no $i^0$; `_tools/derivations/domain_wall.md` is the derivation.
Randall and Sundrum's wall is the whole diamond by $p, q = \arctan(k(ct \mp w))$ of its conformally flat chart, each point a flat space of three dimensions, the wall on the axis and the horizons $y = \pm\infty$ on the four edges, one view for each chart: the Poincaré chart covers the right half, and the two walls, at $kr_c = 1/2$, the strip between the two images of the second wall. The affine parameter of a ray from the wall to the horizon is integrated from the published $g_{tt}$ and checked to be $1/k$, so the edges are drawn as horizons and no infinity.
The Coleman-De Luccia bubble is the whole spacetime from the moment the bubble appears, each point a 2-sphere, one view for each of its two declared bubbles and both shown under every chart: outside the light cone of the bubble's centre $U = -e^{X - \psi}$ and $V = e^{X + \psi}$ are null, with $X = \int d\xi/\rho$ continuous through the wall, inside it they continue as $e^{\eta \mp \chi}$, and $p = \arctan(U/s)$, $q = \arctan(V/s)$ draw both sides with one map. The decay into flat space is the upper half of de Sitter's square with the wall running from $X = 2\arctan(1/2)$ to the middle of its future infinity and Minkowski space inside it running on to a null infinity of its own; the decay of flat space is the upper half of Minkowski's triangle with the wall running to $\mathscr{I}^+$ and anti-de Sitter space inside it, whose $a = 0$ at $c\tau = \pi\ell$ is the upper edge. Each chart of each side is checked against the map, the wall against the hyperbola of the flat side and against $r_w(t)$ of the curved side, and the Kretschmann scalar is checked finite on the centre, beside the wall and where $a$ returns to zero.
The universe from nothing is the upper half of de Sitter's square, from the waist $T = 0$, where the southern half of the 4-sphere is joined on, to future infinity, one view for each Lorentzian chart: $\tan T = \sinh(ct/\ell)$ in the closed slicing, $\cos T = \ell/a$ in the scale factor chart above its join, $T = \eta$ and $\tan T = ct/\ell$, each with $X = \chi$, checked against the published metric, against the hyperboloid and to put every waist on $T = 0$.
A free function does not by itself rule a diagram out: where every choice of it gives the same shape, as for the Tolman-Oppenheimer-Volkoff star and the Morris-Thorne wormhole, the diagram is drawn with a declared choice that moves only the lines inside, and the Malament-Hogarth toy is drawn for every conformal factor at once, since a conformal factor changes no null direction.
Herdeiro and Radu's black hole with scalar hair is drawn on the half axis outside the horizon, for configuration IV and for the Kerr member of the same mass and angular momentum, each a diamond with the horizon on its left under $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/\ell)$ at $\ell = 4/\mu$: for the hair $r_*$ is the tabulated `Hair.tortoise_fast`, $2r_He^{F_1 - F_0}\ln x$ plus a regular quadrature in $x = \sqrt{r^2 - r_H^2}$, and for Kerr the Boyer-Lindquist tortoise coordinate of the axis in $R = r + b$.
The boson star is Minkowski's triangle in both of its charts, drawn for the declared star of `boson_star.Star`: $r_* = \int (a/\alpha)\,dr$ by the trapezoid rule inside the radius where the solver hands the metric over to Schwarzschild's and in closed form beyond it, with $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/R_{99})$; the isotropic chart has the same $r_*$ as a function of the sphere, so its view is the same triangle with the lines of constant $R$, and both are checked against the map, the published $G^\theta{}_\theta$ and a regular centre.
Kodama's kink is the full diamond, drawn for the declared kink of `kodama_kink.Kink`: $r_* = \int e^{-h}\,dR$ by the trapezoid rule inside the field's edge and Schwarzschild's in closed form beyond it, odd about the throat, with $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/r_0)$; the areal chart has the same $r_*$ as a function of the sphere and covers the right half, and both are checked against the map, the published $G^R{}_R$ and a finite curvature at the throat.
The universe on a wall moving past a black hole is one side of the wall in the planar black hole's Kruskal square, at $\ell = \mu = 1$ and $k = 0$, by `BWUTower`, whose $r_* = \tfrac14\ln|(r - 1)/(r + 1)| + \tfrac12\arctan r$ and $UV = (1 - r)e^{2\arctan r}/(1 + r)$ are closed forms checked in sympy: the boundaries lie on $\tan p\tan q = -e^\pi$, the wall is $U = -e^{-2(a - \arctan a)}$, $V = -(1 - a)e^{2a}/(1 + a)$, from the middle of the past singularity to the corner of the boundary and the future singularity, and the region beyond it is drawn as removed. The static chart's four cells are checked against the published metric and the wall against its static time; the view names no chart, so both charts show it.
The travelling wave on a string has none either: inside the pulse its plane of $u$ and $v$ at a fixed place across the string is not totally geodesic.
Schrödinger spacetime has none: every event on a surface of constant time has the same future and the same past, so no plane with light at 45° is faithful to it.
Kundt's waves have none: no plane of two coordinates in any chart is totally geodesic and carries both null families, and their causal structure, flat space outside a light cone with the fronts rolled round it, is drawn in three dimensions by the figure of the Kerr-Schild chart.
Tippett and Tsang's bubble has none: the spacetime has no arrow of time, so it has no future and no past null infinity, and the flat interior alone is one copy of Rindler's wedge under a boost of rapidity $2\pi$, which fills the wedge to within a pixel, as Misner space does at $\psi_0 = 4\pi$.
Einstein, Infeld and Hoffmann's field has none: it holds in the near zone alone, between the bodies and the distance $c/2\Omega$ where the radiation zone begins, which has no infinity and no horizon to place.
Those have no file, which a full redraw removes if one is left behind, so the page has no conformal diagram section for them; `build_mfs_data.py` refuses a file that draws nothing, and the script stops if a metric file is in neither table, so a new spacetime needs a decision.

The Einstein-Rosen waves are drawn on the half plane of fixed $\phi$ and $z$, conformal to Minkowski's half diamond for every wave, since only the factor $e^{2(\gamma - \psi)}$ stands in front of $-c^2dt^2 + d\rho^2$; the maps are checked with random values of $\psi$ and $\gamma$ at every sample and with the pulse of Weber, Wheeler, and Bonnor, whose published Kretschmann scalar settles on the axis, at $\rho = 10^{-3}\,a$ and $10^{-4}\,a$ the same to a part in $10^4$ of its largest value, near $5.7\times10^5/a^4$ at $t = 0$.
The Nariai universe's de Sitter factor is the strip $|\eta| < \pi/2$ of its conformal chart with $X = \chi$ and its edges $X = 0$ and $2\pi$ one line; the static patch is the diamond about $\chi = \pi/2$, entered through $\tan\eta = \sqrt{1 - r^2}\sinh(ct)$ and $\chi = \mathrm{atan2}(\sqrt{1 - r^2}\cosh(ct), -r)$ at $\Lambda = 1$, and its antipode's patch is the diamond about $3\pi/2$.
The Kantowski-Sachs family has two views: the vacuum member, the inside of Schwarzschild's horizon, is cell II of the `Tower` of $1 - r_s/T$ with $T$ and $r$ in the places of $r$ and $ct$, and the dust universe symmetric in time is the strip $|\tau| < 1.2189\,b_0$ of Minkowski's plane, $d\tau = 2b_0\cos^2\eta\,d\eta/a$, which Minkowski's maps send to a lens between its two singularities; `_tools/derivations/kantowski_sachs.md` is the derivation.
The dilaton black hole's plane of $t$ and $r$ is Schwarzschild's in its Einstein metric and a power of $1 - r_d/r$ times it in each string metric, so one `ShiftedTower` draws all five charts: Kruskal's square with the tortoise coordinate moved by $r_s\ln k$, $k = (1 - r_d/r_s)e^{r_d/r_s}$, which divides $U$ and $V$ by $\sqrt{k}$ and puts the singularity $r = r_d$ on the straight lines $T = \pm\pi/2$.
Letelier's black hole in the global monopole's cloud of strings has on its plane of $t$ and $r$ the metric $1/(1 - \Delta)$ times Schwarzschild's with $r_h = r_s/(1 - \Delta)$ for $r_s$ and $(1 - \Delta)t$ for $t$, so its three charts are drawn by the `Tower` of $1 - r_h/r$ at that time, Kruskal's square; the Barriola-Vilenkin plane is Minkowski's, drawn as its triangle with the centre a timelike singularity.
Tangherlini's black hole is Kruskal and Szekeres's square in both of its dimensions, at $r_h = 1$: in five the `Tower` of the two roots $\pm r_h$ of $1 - r_h^2/r^2$, with $U = -e^{-u/r_h}$ and $V = e^{v/r_h}$, one view for each of its three charts, and in six `TangherliniSix`, whose tortoise coordinate carries an arctangent for the complex roots of $1 - r_h^3/r^3$ and whose surface gravity is $3/2r_h$; both tortoise coordinates vanish at $r = 0$, which puts the singularity on $T = \pm\pi/2$, and `_tools/derivations/tangherlini.md` is the derivation.
The Curzon-Chazy particle has two views in each chart at $m = 1$, with $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp x_*)/\ell)$ and $\ell = 4m$: its half axis is the whole diamond, whose left edges are $z = 0$, drawn as the edge of the chart with $g_{tt}g_{zz} = -1$ and the Kretschmann scalar's fall to zero checked, and its half plane $z = 0$ is Minkowski's triangle with the ring a timelike singularity on $X = 0$; `_tools/derivations/curzon_chazy.md` is the derivation.
Bonnor's rotating dust cloud has two views in each chart at $a = 1$, with $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp x_*)/\ell)$ and $\ell = 4a$: its half axis, flat, Minkowski's triangle with the singularity $z = 0$ a timelike line on $X = 0$, and its plane $z = 0$ outside the null circle with $\phi$ divided out, the triangle again with $\rho = a$ on $X = 0$, where $\rho_*$ vanishes; the Kretschmann scalar on the axis is checked against $4a^4(8a^4 - 27z^4)/z^{12}$ and the quotient metric against $-c^2dt^2/(1 - a^4/\rho^4) + e^{a^4/8\rho^4}d\rho^2$.
The first Morgan-Morgan disc, at $m = a/5$ and $\ell = 4a$, has two views in Weyl's chart, the whole axis as Minkowski's diamond with the disc down its middle and the half plane $z = 0$ as the triangle with the rim a timelike singular line inside it, and three in the oblate spheroidal chart, the triangle of the axis above the disc, the triangle of the plane outside the rim with the rim on $X = 0$, and the strip of the disc itself at $\ell = a$; the published Kretschmann scalar is checked to diverge at the rim as $1/\xi^2$ and $1/\eta^2$ and to be finite at the centre, and `_tools/derivations/morgan_morgan.md` is the derivation.
Zipoy and Voorhees's metric has four views in each chart at $m = 1$ and $\ell = 4m$, its half axis and its equatorial half plane for $q = 1$ and for $q = -1/2$, with $u, v = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/\ell)$: the oblate axis is the whole diamond, whose left edges are $r = 2m$, drawn as the edge of the chart with $g_{tt}g_{rr} = -1$ and the Kretschmann scalar's value $3/(4m^4)$ there checked, and the other three are Minkowski's triangle with $r = 2m$ a timelike singularity on $X = 0$; the prolate equator's curvature grows as the $3/2$ power, so its divergence is checked between $10^{-8}\,m$ and $10^{-10}\,m$ from the edge; `_tools/derivations/zipoy_voorhees.md` is the derivation.
Erez and Rosen's quadrupole has the same four views in each chart at $m = 1$, its half axis and its equatorial half plane for the prolate $q = 1$ and the oblate $q = -1/2$, with the tortoise coordinate taken by quadrature, `_erez_rosen_star` in `null_rays.py`: the prolate axis is the whole diamond, its left edges $r = 2m$ the edge of the chart with the Kretschmann scalar checked to be $3e^6/(4m^4)$ there, and the other three are Minkowski's triangle with a timelike singularity on $X = 0$. The oblate equatorial plane takes $\ell = 40\,m$ where the others take $4\,m$, since light from $r = 2.1\,m$ needs $69.9\,m/c$ to reach its singularity.
Schwarzschild's black hole in a tidal field has four views in each of its three charts at $m = 1$, its axis and its equatorial plane for the oblate $q = 1/12$ and the prolate $q = -1/12$: a `DistortedTower` of the one root $x = 1$ with the tortoise coordinate taken by quadrature, `_distorted_star` in `null_rays.py`, and $\kappa = e^{2q}/4m$ on both planes. Three planes are Kruskal and Szekeres's hexagon; on the oblate axis $x_*$ tends to $0.3443\,m$ and the edge $x \to \infty$ is the timelike curve $\tan p\tan q = -e^{2\kappa R}$, drawn as Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter's boundary is. The cells inside the horizon are checked against the prolate spheroidal and spherical charts' components read there, and the Kretschmann scalar at the horizon against $\tfrac34(1 - 12q)^2e^{4q}/m^4$ on the axis and $\tfrac34(1 + 3q)^2e^{10q}/m^4$ on the equator.
McVittie's mass has one view, for the same universe of dust and a cosmological constant as its spacetime diagrams, and it is the one diagram built from integrated rays: with $R = r_s\cosh^2x$ the radial null condition of the areal chart is $dx/d(ct) = (H/c \pm \tanh x\,\mathrm{sech}^2x/r_s)/2$, both families leave the singular sphere $x = 0$, and `McVittieRays` gives each event the times $s_{\rm out}$ and $s_{\rm in}$ at which its two rays left it, the outgoing one integrated in $x$ for every point at once and the ingoing one traced back in $\ln t$ to the event $x = 0$. The drawing's null coordinates are $p = F(s_{\rm out})$ and $q = -F(s_{\rm in})$ with $F(s) = \arctan(\tfrac12\ln 2s + (e^{\kappa s} - 1)/20)$ and $\kappa$ the surface gravity of Kottler's $r_-$, which puts the singular sphere on the line $T = 0$; future infinity is the curve of the last outgoing ray to cross each escaping ingoing ray, the cosmological event horizon is the ingoing ray that left at $ct = 0.0298\,r_s$, found by bisection and checked to hold $R = r_+$, and the edge $p = \pi/2$ is $R = r_-$ at $t = \infty$. The Kretschmann scalar is checked to diverge on $R = r_s$ between $1 - r_s/R = 10^{-10}$ and $10^{-12}$, a hundred times nearer since it grows only as the first power, and to take Kottler's value on $r_-$; the whole run takes about twelve seconds.
Sultana and Dyer's black hole is the part $\eta > 0$ of Kruskal and Szekeres's exterior and black hole, by Schwarzschild's ingoing map with $v = \eta + r$: a triangle between the big bang, a spacelike curve from the singularity at $(X, T) = (0, \pi/2)$ to $i^0$, the singularity $r = 0$ on $T = \pi/2$, and $\mathscr{I}^+$, with the event horizon from the big bang to $i^+$ and both trapping horizons from the corner to $i^+$. It has one view for each of its three charts, each checked against its own published metric, one event is checked to land on one point through all three, and the Kretschmann scalar to diverge on the big bang and at $r = 0$ and to stay finite on the horizon.
Melvin's universe is drawn on its half plane of fixed $\phi$ and $z$, Minkowski's half diamond, with the Kretschmann scalar checked finite on the axis, where it is $20B^4$; Ernst's black hole on its equator, conformal to Schwarzschild's plane of $t$ and $r$, is Kruskal and Szekeres's hexagon, a `Tower` of the one root $r_s$ checked against the published metric at $B = 1/(2r_s)$.
Ernst and Wild's Kerr hole in Melvin's universe is drawn on its axis and on its equator with $\phi$ divided out by Kerr's two towers, `kerr_axis` with the constant `factor` $k$ and `kerr_equator` with the `factor` $H = (1 + B^2A/4r^2)^2$, each checked against the published metric and divided out, since a factor changes no null line; both pass `kretschmann=False`, since the published scalar written out does not evaluate in seconds, and `_tools/test_kerr_melvin.py` holds the curvature finite at $r = 0$ on the axis and growing without bound toward the ring.
The eye of the storm is drawn on its axis at $m = 1$, $a = 3m/5$ and $\ell = m/5$, Bardeen's tower, each region inside $r_-$ closed by the centre of the disk, where $f = \Delta/(r^2 + a^2) \to 1$ and the published Kretschmann scalar is checked to vanish; `EyeOfTheStormTower` takes the two roots of the published $g^{rr}$ in forty digits and `slices.eos_rstar` integrates what is left of $1/f$ once its poles are taken out, as Bardeen's are, in the chart of Boyer and Lindquist and in the ingoing Kerr chart.
The swirling universe is drawn on its half plane of fixed $\phi$ and $z = 0$, Minkowski's half diamond under $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp \rho)\sqrt{j})$, with the Kretschmann scalar checked finite on the axis, where it is $-192j^2$; the black hole in it, on its equator, conformal to Schwarzschild's plane of $t$ and $r$, is Kruskal and Szekeres's hexagon, a `Tower` of the one root $2m$ checked against the published metric at $j = 1/(16m^2)$.
Levi-Civita's cylinder is drawn on its half plane of fixed $\phi$ and $z$ at $\sigma = 1/4$, Minkowski's triangle by $p, q = \arctan(ct \mp 4\rho^{1/4})$ with the axis a timelike singularity on $X = 0$, where the Kretschmann scalar is checked to diverge, one view for each chart; the Kasner form's $3r^{1/3}$ is checked proportional to $4\rho^{1/4}$, so both views are one triangle.
Robinson and Trautman's spacetime is drawn on its axis of symmetry, with the fronts its spacetime diagram declares: the region $u \ge 0$ by $p = \arctan U$, Kruskal's $U = -e^{-cu/4m}$, and $q$ a monotone function of the Kruskal $V$ each ingoing ray has once the fronts are round, the identity for $V \ge 0$ and on the rays that leave $r = 0$ the one that puts the singularity on the straight line $T = -\pi/2$; beyond $u = \infty$ Schwarzschild's black hole and second exterior are read from `schwarzschild.json` at $r_s = 2m$, the join Bičák and Podolský draw, and the view carries a restriction.

The Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter black hole, at $r_s = 2L$ where $r_h = L$, is drawn by `AdSHoleTower`, whose tortoise coordinate is summed over the positive root and the complex pair of roots of $f$ and vanishes at $r = 0$: the singularities lie on the straight lines $T = \pm\pi/2$, as Schwarzschild's do, and the conformal boundary on $\tan p\tan q = -13.9$, bowed outward to $X = \pm 2.617$ on $T = 0$, the diagram of Fidkowski, Hubeny, Kleban, and Shenker in the form that leaves the black hole room for its name; `_tools/derivations/schwarzschild_ads.md` is the derivation.
The topological black holes have thirteen views, each of their three spacetimes in every chart that holds it. The flat hole is drawn by `AdSHoleTower` as Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter is, with $\kappa = 3/(2L)$, $R = \pi L/(3\sqrt{3})$ and the boundary on $\tan p\tan q = -e^{\pi/\sqrt{3}} = -6.13$, in the static chart, both Eddington-Finkelstein charts, Lemos's chart and the brane's, whose lines of constant $z$ are lines of constant $r = L^2/z$. The hyperbolic hole without mass has the BTZ hole's plane of $t$ and $r$ and is its square, by `BTZTower`. The hyperbolic hole of negative mass is `HyperbolicHoleDrawing`, Reissner-Nordström's tower with each exterior ending on the conformal boundary, the timelike curve where $r_* \to R = -0.304\,L$, and each region inside $r_-$ on the singularity $r = 0$ on $X = \pm\pi/2$; its outgoing chart is the ingoing one reflected a period up the tower.
The quantum BTZ black hole has five views: its static hole in each of its three charts and its dressed cone are drawn by `AdSHoleTower`, since each $H$ has one positive root and a complex pair, and its rotating hole by `CarterAdSTower` and `HyperbolicHoleDrawing` as the charged hole in anti-de Sitter space is, which has the same tower; `_tools/derivations/quantum_btz.md` gives the numbers.
The anti-de Sitter soliton has five views, one for each chart: `soliton_pq` draws the strip $\sigma = (\pi/2)r_*/R$, $\eta = (\pi/2)ct/R$, a half strip from the tip to the conformal boundary for Horowitz and Myers's charts and the Poincaré chart, and the whole strip through the tip for the polar chart, with two free particles released from rest at $\rho = L/2$ and $L$, each checked to keep its energy by the published metric.
The topological star has five views, one for each chart: Minkowski's triangle by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/\ell)$ at $\ell = 6r_B$ for Bah and Heidmann's chart, with the bubble on $X = 0$, and for the Einstein metric, whose left edge is singular; the whole diamond for the chart about the bubble, with $r_*$ negative on the side $\psi = \pi$ and two free particles released from rest at $\rho = 2$ and $4$, each checked to keep its energy by the published metric; `TopologicalStringTower` for the black string, Kruskal and Szekeres's square with the bubble on $UV = 1$, $T = \pm\pi/2$; and the whole diamond for the extremal string's exterior, with the degenerate horizon for its left edges.
Flat space with supertranslation hair has three views, one for each chart, on the equator at $C = \tfrac{1}{2}\ell(3\cos^2\theta - 1)$: Minkowski's triangle by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp (\rho - 5\ell/2))/\ell)$, the supertranslation horizon standing where Minkowski's centre stands, reached by the Bondi charts through $ct = cu + W + \ell = cv - W - \ell$ and $\rho = W + \ell$; each marks the shells of the embedding as the points $t = 0$, $\rho = \rho_k$.
Kaigorodov's spacetime has five views, one for each chart that divides a Killing direction out of the slice $y = 0$: Minkowski's triangle by $p, q = \arctan((v \mp x_*)/L)$ with $x_* = \tfrac{2}{5}x^{5/2}/L^{3/2}$, the conformal boundary a timelike line on $X = 0$ and the two null edges the singularity $x \to \infty$, and the Poincaré chart's by $\arctan((ct \mp z_*)/L)$. The Kretschmann scalar is $24/L^4$ at every event, so the edges are drawn as a singularity on the tidal tensor of the falling observers with no momentum along $u$ or $y$, which is checked from the published Riemann tensor to have $E_{ab}E^{ab} = 3 + \tfrac{9}{2}x^{10}$ at $L = 1$, with their proper time to $x = \infty$ finite; every view carries a restriction, the free charts and the stationary region have no view, and `_tools/derivations/siklos.md` is the derivation.
The charged black hole in anti-de Sitter space, at $r_s = 27L/8$ and $r_q^2 = 11L^2/8$, is `HyperbolicHoleDrawing` on a `CarterAdSTower`, which takes any $f$ with two real roots and a complex pair: Reissner-Nordström's tower with each exterior ending on the conformal boundary, the timelike curve where $r_* \to R = 0.405\,L$, and each region inside $r_-$ on the singularity $r = 0$ on $X = \pm\pi/2$, one view for each of its three charts, the outgoing chart's exterior and its moment $t = 0$ a period up the tower.
Boulware and Deser's black hole, at $r_0 = 13r_h/12$ and $\ell = 5r_h/12$, is Kruskal and Szekeres's square by `BoulwareDeserTower`, whose tortoise coordinate is `slices.boulware_deser_rstar` and vanishes at $r = 0$, where $f$ is finite, so the singularity lies on $T = \pm\pi/2$: one view for each of its three charts. The other branch, at $r_0 = \ell$, is a strip, $X = \pi r_*/2R$ and $T = \pi ct/2R$ with $R = 1.198\,\ell$ the tortoise coordinate at infinity, between the singularity $r = 0$ and the conformal boundary, both timelike.
Kerr-de Sitter is drawn on its axis for each sign of $\Lambda$. For $\Lambda > 0$ `CarterAxis` places the twelve kinds of region that the four roots of $\Delta_r$ cut the axis into through Kottler's one function $g$, smooth across $r_+$ and $r_c$ and continuous across $r_-$ and $r_n$: the pattern Akcay and Matzner drew, which repeats in every direction, with the infinities $r \to +\infty$ and $r \to -\infty$ one curve of the drawing met from its two sides. For $\Lambda < 0$ `CarterAdSTower` is the tower of Kerr's axis with both ends of the axis timelike curves. Each has a view for Carter's chart and one for each Kerr chart; the limit at $r_-$ is taken in $r_*$, since $g$ closes on $\pi/2$ there at the rate $\kappa_c$.

The black hole threaded by a cosmic string, at $b = 0.9$, is Kruskal and Szekeres's hexagon, the `Tower` of the one root $r_s$, each point a sphere with the wedge $2\pi(1 - b)$ missing: four views, one for each chart, the static chart and the chart with the wedge removed each checked against its own published metric.

The Kaluza-Klein monopole, at $m = 1$, is Minkowski's triangle by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/\ell)$ with $\ell = 4m$, the nut a regular centre on $X = 0$ where the Kretschmann scalar is checked to settle at $3/(32m^4)$, one view for each chart, each point a squashed 3-sphere; `_tools/derivations/kaluza_klein_monopole.md` is the derivation.
The Kaluza-Klein black holes of one charge are Schwarzschild's square by `KaluzaKleinTower`, a `Tower` on `slices.kkbh_rstar`, which vanishes at the singularity $r = 0$, so that Kruskal's $UV = 1$ there, with the surface gravity $1/(2\sqrt{q\,r_s})$: the electric hole with the circle divided out, the magnetic hole on its half axis and the Einstein metric of four dimensions share the one map, and the two ingoing charts are carried through it by $ct + r_* = v - \sqrt{q/r_s}\,r + D(r)$, $D$ the smooth part of $r_*$, and by $ct + r_* = v$. The hole of equal charges is Reissner and Nordström's tower at $\rho_+ = 2\,r_s$ and $\rho_- = r_s$, and carries no slice, since the holes embedded are those of one charge.

Myers and Perry's black hole has five views: with one spin in five dimensions at $a = 3/5$, the plane transverse to the rotation in the Boyer-Lindquist and ingoing charts, the `Tower` of the roots $\pm r_+$ of $f = (r^2 - r_+^2)/(r^2 + a^2)$, and the plane of rotation with $\phi$ divided out, an `EquatorTower` of the same roots, each Schwarzschild's square with $r = 0$ on $T = \pm\pi/2$, a conical singularity with the Kretschmann scalar $72\mu^2/a^8$ on the transverse plane and the ring where it diverges on the plane of rotation; with equal spins at $a = 2/5$, `MyersEqual`, a tower of the horizons $\rho_\pm$ with the singularity $\rho = 0$ timelike, each point a squashed 3-sphere; and with one spin in six dimensions at $a = 3/2$, `MyersSix`, the square again. Every view carries a restriction, and `_tools/derivations/myers_perry.md` is the derivation.

Damour and Solodukhin's wormhole, at $r_s = 1$ and $\lambda = 1/5$, is the full diamond by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp x)/\ell)$ with $x$ the tortoise coordinate of the chart's time, zero at the throat, which stands on the diamond's axis, one view for each of its five charts: the two charts of $t$ and the areal $r$ cover the right half, $\ell$ is $4r_s$ in Damour and Solodukhin's time and $\sqrt{1 + \lambda^2}$ times that in the rescaled time, and one event is checked to land on one point through all five maps, as the Kretschmann scalar at the throat is against $(1 + 24\lambda^4)/4\lambda^4r_s^4$.

The wormhole of Einstein-Dirac-Maxwell theory, at $r_0 = 1$ and $Q_e = 1/2$, is the full diamond by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp x)/4r_0)$ with $x$ its tortoise coordinate, zero at the throat on the diamond's axis, one view for each of its three charts, the areal chart covering the right half; one event is checked to land on one point through all three maps, as the Kretschmann scalar at the throat is against $2(3Q_e^4 - 2Q_e^2r_0^2 + 3r_0^4)/r_0^8$.

Interstellar's wormhole, at $a = \rho$ and $M = \rho/2$, is the full diamond by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp \ell)/\rho)$, its proper distance being its own tortoise coordinate, one view for each of its three charts: the proper distance chart covers the whole diamond, the cylinder chart the band between the mouths, and the flare chart the part beyond $\ell = a$; the Kretschmann scalar is checked to be $4/\rho^4$ on the cylinder.

Kiselev's black hole has six views, one for each chart. With the black hole, at $w = -2/3$ and $r_q = 8\,r_s$, `KiselevMap` is Kottler's map with $r_-$ and $r_+$ for his two horizons, and Kottler's chain of regions: its tortoise coordinate has the residues $1/f'(r_\mp)$, which sum to $-r_q$, so it falls as $-r_q\ln r$ where Kottler's tends to a constant, both null edges of the region beyond $r_+$ are $r = \infty$, and since $g_{tt}g_{rr} = -1$ makes $r$ an affine parameter on every radial ray they are null infinity, two edges at 45° that meet at $i^\pm$, where Kottler's infinity is a spacelike curve. The static chart, the chart of the linear term and the two Eddington-Finkelstein charts are drawn as Kottler's are. Without the black hole the one surface gravity $1/2r_q$ serves both null coordinates, so the centre is the straight line $X = \pi/2$, a timelike singularity, and the whole spacetime is two static triangles and the two diamonds between them; the hyperbolic chart covers one triangle by $u, v = 2r_q(\eta \mp \chi)$, and the conformally flat chart that triangle and the contracting diamond by Minkowski's map of the flat plane it is conformal to, which is checked to be `KiselevMap`'s in both cells; `_tools/derivations/kiselev.md` is the derivation.

Einstein and Rosen's bridges have one view for each chart, each set in the extension through its horizon, Schwarzschild's hexagon: the neutral bridge's sheet $u > 0$ is the exterior I and its sheet $u < 0$ the exterior I', where the time of the extension runs against $t$, and the whole of $u = 0$ at a finite $t$ is sent to the bifurcation sphere; the charged bridges take `Tower` with the roots $r_+$ and $r_-$ of $r^2 - r_sr - r_q^2$, whose $r_*$ vanishes at $r = 0$ and puts that singularity on $T = \pm\pi/2$, checked on the published Kretschmann scalar.

Simpson and Visser's black bounce has twelve views at $r_s = 1$, its three geometries in each of its four charts. At $a = r_s/2$ it is `BounceTower`, a `Tower` whose tortoise coordinate is the closed form of `null_rays._sv_rstar`: Carter's tower for the axis of Kerr, the regions beyond the second horizon asymptotically flat, both horizons of one surface gravity, and the sphere of least area $r = 0$ the straight line $T = \pi/2$ through the middle of each black hole, since $G(u) + G(-v) = \pi/2$ where $r_* = 0$. At $a = r_s$ the horizon is a double root, so each region is placed by $\arctan((ct \mp r_*)/\ell)$ with $\ell = 2\,r_s$, moved by $\pi$ from one region to the next as Majumdar and Papapetrou's are, the regions $r > 0$ on the right and the regions $r < 0$ on the left, each a whole diamond. At $a = 2\,r_s$ it is the full diamond with $\ell = 4\,r_s$ and the throat on its axis. Each chart's view tints what that chart covers and draws its own lines, those of constant $v$ or $u$ straight at 45°, and every map is checked against the published metric of the chart it is drawn for, across each horizon the chart crosses; `_tools/derivations/simpson_visser.md` is the derivation.
Fisher, Janis, Newman and Winicour's scalar field, at $\gamma = 1/2$, is Minkowski's triangle by $p, q = \arctan((ct \mp r_*)/\ell)$ with $\ell = 4b$ and $r_*$ zero on the singularity, a timelike line on $X = 0$ where the Kretschmann scalar is checked to diverge, one view for each of its four charts, each point a 2-sphere; the harmonic view is drawn at $k = 1$, so with $\ell = 8k$, one event is checked to land on one point through all four maps, and the Kretschmann scalar against its closed form.
Witten's black hole in two dimensions, at $\lambda = m = 1$, is Kruskal's hexagon by $p = \arctan U$ and $q = \arctan V$ with its own Kruskal coordinates, $UV = 1 - e^{-2\Phi}/m$ in every chart, so the singularity $UV = 1$ lies on the straight lines $T = \pm\pi/2$, each point a single event; it has one view for each of its seven charts, the charts of $t$ and $x$ or $w$ tinting the exterior and the black hole, every map is checked against the published metric of its chart in each region it covers, one event is checked to land on one point through all seven, and the Kretschmann scalar to diverge as $w \to 0$ and stay finite on the horizon.
The black hole of Jackiw and Teitelboim's gravity, at $L = r_h = 1$, is the square $|X|, |T| < \pi/2$ by $p = \arctan U$ and $q = \arctan V$ of its Kruskal chart, which is its global chart itself, $T = \tau$ and $X = \sigma - \pi/2$: the boundaries $UV = -1$ on $X = \pm\pi/2$ and the lines $UV = 1$, where the dilaton vanishes, on $T = \pm\pi/2$, drawn as `singular` with a legend that says the curvature is finite there, as the BTZ square's $r = 0$ is.
