Every embedding diagram draws part of one moment of its spacetime, or of several moments in turn, and `_tools/derivations/embedding_slices.md` works out how each moment meets every other diagram of its spacetime.
`_tools/derivations/slices.py` is that table in code.
It reads each moment and how far the embedding reaches along it from the embedding file itself, and says what the moment is in the chart of each spacetime diagram's view: a line of constant time, the Eddington-Finkelstein curves of Schwarzschild's $t = 0$, de Sitter's static $t = 0$ in the flat slicing, the fronts $u = u_k$ of the pp-wave and of the Aichelburg-Sexl shock, Novikov's slice outside Oppenheimer and Snyder's dust, and the whole equator seen from above on Kerr's and Kerr-Newman's views of the principal rays.
`.venv.noindex/bin/python _tools/derivations/slices.py` checks every chart transformation a moment is carried through by pulling one published metric back onto the other, and Novikov's shells against the published exterior, twenty-five checks in about a second.
`HIDDEN` in it names the drawings on which a moment of the spacetime lies and is not drawn, each with the reason: FRW's flat and open universes and Tolman-Bondi's marginally bound cloud are other spacetimes than the ones embedded, Vaidya's and Bonnor and Vaidya's outgoing charts draw the time reverse of the shell embedded, the Kaluza-Klein black hole of equal charges is another member of its family than the holes of one charge embedded, and beyond $r_c$ Gödel's, van Stockum's and Som and Raychaudhuri's circles are closed timelike curves, as the spinning string's and those of Bonnor's dust cloud are inside it.

Each drawing draws its slices with the map it draws everything else with: `null_rays.py` writes them into each flat view, `projections.py` into each figure and `conformal.py` into each conformal view.
`null_rays.py --slices` rewrites only the slices of the diagram files, from the embedding files as they stand, tracing no ray, and redraws the figures, which take seconds; run it, and `conformal.py`, after an embedding diagram moves a moment.
Each slice records a stamp over the embedding surface it marks, computed by `embedding_moment_version` in `build_mfs_data.py` over the view's settings and the surface's label, time and pieces, and the command refuses a slice whose stamp no longer matches, or that names an embedding view or surface the embedding file does not draw, in `--check` and when writing alike.
The `Slices` tests hold every point of every slice to its moment and every line's ends to the embedding's reach or the drawing's box, from the numbers in the files alone, and every moment to appearing on every drawing it lies on and on no other.

### The file, which the application reads

A flat view of a spacetime diagram, a figure in three dimensions and a view of a conformal diagram may each carry `slices`, a list with one entry for each surface of each embedding view whose moment lies on the drawing, in the order of the embedding file's views and surfaces:

- `view` and `surface`: the embedding view's `id` and the surface's place in its `surfaces`.
- `label`: the moment as TeX, the surface's own `label` in a sequence and otherwise the moment in the drawing's own words, as `$t = 0$`, `static $t = 0$` or `$t = 0$, $r = b$`.
- `version`: the stamp above; the application can ignore it.
- `lines`: polylines, `points`: points, and `fills`: regions, each a list of rings filled by the even odd rule, any of them possibly empty, all in the drawing's own coordinates: a flat view's unit square, as its rays, the right half of a view through the centre, which the reader mirrors as it mirrors the rays; a figure's plane of the page, as its layers; and a conformal view's $(X, T)$, as its layers.
- `place`, present where the drawing names the moment beside it: `at`, a point in the same coordinates, `anchor`, the corner of the label pinned there as a conformal label's anchor, and `dx` and `dy`, how far that corner stands from `at`, in the units the page draws the drawing in, a spacetime diagram's plot 520 wide and a conformal diagram 628 wide with its margin, $y$ down: to the edge of the line's stroke, 1.3 from the line, or of the point's dot, 5.1 from its centre.
  On a view through the centre `at` may have a negative first coordinate, the mirror of the point at its absolute value.

Draw a slice's regions under everything else on the drawing, and its lines and points over every other line and under the points that mark ends and events, as a solid line 2.6 wide of `--green-light`, #4db84a, on the dark ground and `--green-dark`, #2a6e2a, in print, a region tinted with the same green at 12%.
Green marks nothing else on these drawings but in dots, Kerr's ergosurface, so a solid green line reads at once as the new mark.
Set a label with `place` at its point, on a ground of its own as a conformal diagram's small labels are, white, as every word of a drawing is, and at the size of every other word of its drawing.
A slice without `place` is a region alone, the floor of a figure or the equator seen from above, and is named in the legend instead.

`slices.place` chooses each label's side for the drawing as it is drawn at the usual text size: at the right hand end of the slice's lines, on the line's upper edge over the rest of it, then on its lower edge, then past the end; then at the left hand end; then at a point 85% of the way along; for a point, round from above and to its right against the edge of its dot.
A label stands exactly on the edge of what it names, and labels may touch; nothing is added between them.
The first side that stays in the drawing and overlaps no other label wins, and among those, the first that covers none of its own line and none of another slice's.
A conformal diagram and a figure are drawn larger with the reader's text, their labels with them, so the place holds at every size.
A spacetime diagram's words grow around a plot that does not, so at a larger text size the page chooses, for all the labels of a plot together, the sides of their points and of the far ends of their lines that leave none past the plot and fewest over another label, in the same order, the file's own first, and a label wider than the room beside its point slides along to stay in the plot.
Where no choice keeps them clear, as at three times the usual text on a phone, the plot is drawn larger, a tenth at a time and up to three times, until one does, and `fitPlots` holds it there, since no word of a drawing is set smaller than the caption beside it.
At the usual text size the choice is the file's place, and it returns there once the text is smaller again; `fitSliceLabels` in `_layouts/mfs.html` carries the details.

Each drawing's legend adds one entry for the slices of each embedding view it marks, shown with them: "the slice of the embedding diagram", "the slices of the embedding diagram" where the view is a sequence, and after it the moment where the drawing does not name it, as "the slice of the embedding diagram, $t = 0$".
Where two embedding views' entries would read the same, as Reissner-Nordström's outside $r_+$ and inside $r_-$, they are one entry.
Only the slices of the embedding view shown are drawn, so choosing another view of the embedding diagram draws its slices in place of the others, as a slider's moment would; the print copy prints every view of the embedding diagram, and so every slice.
A sequence's moments are all drawn at once, since the movie that plays the sequence passes through every one of them, and so are a stack's, which marks each moment as a ring at its time.
A moment of a stack is a slice that carries `curve` beside `surface`, the ring the stack marks at that time, and its stamp is taken over that ring's label and time as well.
The ideal string's cone unrolled is the same moment as the cone about Gott's core, marked on each drawing of the conical chart; Gott's own conformal diagram, a spacetime whose core replaces the apex of the ideal string's cone, marks only its own.

### The shade of a conformal region

The captain asked on 30 September 2026 that choosing Minkowski space, de Sitter space or anti-de Sitter space on the Einstein static universe's conformal diagram shade the part of the embedding diagram's sphere that region covers, in pink and without glow.
Each region is Hawking and Ellis's, found from the map each conformal view draws with and never written by hand: `conformal.covered()` takes the map from the other spacetime's own chart into the strip, finds by bisection in time the point of each radius at the conformal time $\eta$, and keeps the values of $\chi$ reached.
Minkowski space enters by its spherical chart through `mink_pq`, anti-de Sitter space by its static global chart through `ads_global_pq`, and de Sitter space by its closed slicing through `ds_closed_pq`, its hyperboloid embedding read backwards, since the static patch covers only the diamond $|\eta| + \chi < \pi/2$ about the pole.
`embedding.py` checks each region against Hawking and Ellis's, $\chi < \pi - |\eta|$, the whole sphere for $|\eta| < \pi/2$ and $\chi < \pi/2$, at $\eta$ from $-3$ to $3$ in steps of $1/2$, and `conformal.py` checks each region the strip draws against the same map, so the two diagrams agree.
The conformal diagram has no slider, so the shade is the region at the moment both diagrams draw, $t = 0$, which is $\eta = 0$, and its caption paragraph says so.

An embedding view's `shades` is a list with one entry for each view of the conformal diagram whose region the view shades, in the order of the conformal file's views:

- `view`: the `id` of the view in the spacetime's conformal file that the shade answers; the conformal file carries nothing new, and `build_mfs_data.py` refuses a shade whose view, surface, piece or circles do not exist.
- `surface` and `piece`: the surface's place in `surfaces` and the `id` of the piece shaded, a profile and never a grid piece.
- `from` and `to`: the values of the piece's `x` between which the moment is shaded, each written as the double of a point of its profile, so the shade ends on circles of the surface; at $\eta = 0$ Minkowski space shades $0$ to $\pi$, the sphere but its antipode $i^0$, which is one point, de Sitter space $0$ to $\pi$ and anti-de Sitter space $0$ to $\pi/2$.
- `T`: the conformal time of the moment shaded, the conformal view's own $T$, $0$ for the Einstein static universe.
- `reach`: the region at other conformal times, each `[T, from, to]` rounded to seven decimals, or `[T, null, null]` where the region covers none of the moment, as de Sitter space beyond $|\eta| = \pi/2$; a client with a slider of time can shade from it, and the tests hold it to Hawking and Ellis's.
- `layers`: the figure's fills with the shade in place of its piece's tint, at the figure's camera, in the form of the figure's own fill layers; the page paints them in place of the figure's fills and under all of its lines, and a client that draws the surfaces itself can ignore them.
- `legend`: the shade's legend entry, `["fill", "shade", text]`.
- `caption`: one TeX paragraph, read after the view's caption while the shade is shown.

While the conformal diagram shows a view that a shade answers, draw the shade and no other: the cones of its piece between `from` and `to` in `--pink-light`, #ff6da2, at 16% on the dark ground, the rest of that piece clear, every other piece as `tint` says, its legend entry first and every fill entry whose class the shade's `layers` do not paint hidden, and its caption paragraph after the view's caption.
Nothing about the shade glows, and it is shown at once with the view, with no transition.
A view the conformal diagram shows with no shade answering it leaves the figure as published.
A turned figure keeps its camera and takes the shade, as `shade()` in `MFS/assets/turn.js` draws it, and `turn_check.cjs` holds every shade drawn at the figure's camera to its published `layers`.
The print copy prints the page as it was first drawn, with the chart's own conformal view shown and so no shade.

## Curvature figures

`MFS/assets/data/curvature/<metric_id>.json` holds the curvature figure of one spacetime: its Kretschmann scalar $K$, and its Ricci scalar $R$ where that does not vanish, plotted from the formulas the spacetime's metric file publishes, as the captain asked on 22 October 2026.
The page draws its Kretschmann scalar under the heading "Kretschmann scalar diagram", after the Kretschmann scalar, and its Ricci scalar under the heading "Ricci scalar diagram", after the Ricci scalar, and the application reads the same files.
A spacetime has one where its curvature carries physics its formula does not show at a glance, as the research list of October 2026 sets out spacetime by spacetime; a spacetime without one has no file and the page no section, and no table has to name it, as `NOT_DRAWN` names the spacetimes without a conformal diagram.
Alcubierre's warp drive, Ehlers and Kundt's A II and B I metrics, Bardeen's regular black hole, the Bach-Weyl ring, Bartnik and McKinnon's solitons, Belinski and Zakharov's two solitons, the black hole fireworks, black Saturn, Bonnor's stars of charged dust, Bonnor's rotating dust, Bonnor and Vaidya's radiating star, Born and Infeld's point charge, Boulware and Deser's black hole, Bowers and Liang's star, the braneworld universe, the Brans-Dicke sphere, Brill's charged Taub-NUT, the black ring of bubbling anti-de Sitter space, the C-metric, Chandrasekhar and Xanthopoulos's colliding waves, the charged de Sitter black hole, the charged black hole (`rn_metric`), the Curzon-Chazy particle, Damour and Solodukhin's wormhole, Datt and Ruban's T-models, the charged dilaton black hole, the Eguchi-Hanson space, Schwarzschild's black hole in a tidal field, Einstein's static field of 1912, Einstein's cluster, the Einstein-Rosen waves, the eye of the storm, the Erez-Rosen quadrupole, the scalar field of Fisher, Janis, Newman and Winicour, the global monopole, Gowdy's polarised wave on the torus, the gravastar, the gravitational instantons, Hayward's regular black hole, Hiscock's evaporating black hole, the inside of a holographic superconductor, Schwarzschild's star of uniform density, Israel's collapsing shell of dust, Interstellar's wormhole, the Kerr-Newman black hole, Khan and Penrose's colliding plane waves, the Kantowski-Sachs cosmologies, Kastor and Traschen's two holes, Kiselev's black hole, Kerr's black hole, the Kerr-Bertotti-Robinson black hole, Kerr's black hole in Melvin's universe, the Kerr-Taub-NUT black hole, McVittie's mass, Melvin's magnetic universe, Majumdar and Papapetrou's two holes, Brill and Lindquist's two black holes, the perturbed Friedmann universe, the Myers-Perry black hole, the extreme Kerr throat, Nordström's scalar gravity, the quantum Oppenheimer-Snyder black hole, Roberts's collapsing scalar field, Schwarzschild's black hole, the regular radiation universe, the Simpson-Visser black bounce, the black hole in a swirling universe, Taub-NUT space, Visser's thin shell wormhole, the three-brane and its throat, the topological star, the polytropic star, Vaidya's radiating star, Van Den Broeck's warp drive and the Zipoy-Voorhees metric have one.

`_tools/derivations/curvature.py` draws them, one function per spacetime, with what it draws and what it checks in its docstring.
It needs the environment of "The test environment" above and took 26 seconds for both on 22 October 2026:

    .venv.noindex/bin/python _tools/derivations/curvature.py
    python3 _tools/build_mfs_data.py

`--metric <metric_id>` redraws one spacetime, and `--verify` prints every check and writes nothing.
A full run removes the file of a spacetime its table `DRAWN` does not list.

The `eguchi_hanson` figure at $a = 1$ draws $K$ against $r/a$ from the bolt to $3a$ on a logarithmic scale from $10^{-4}$ to $10^3$, and the two-centre chart's $K$ over $0 \le \rho \le 3a$, $|z| \le 3a$ in cells of $a/20$ from $10^{-2}$ to $10^3$, with the bolt between the centres $z = \pm a$ as a `front`; its stored Ricci scalar vanishes, and the generator checks $K = 384/a^4$ on the bolt in all three charts, $K$ constant on each confocal ellipse, and the clips.

The `ellis_bronnikov` figure plots both scalars against proper distance over $-3 \le r/\ell \le 3$ at $\ell = 1$, as its radial spacetime diagram does, with the two sides of the throat together.
Its stored scalars depend on $r$ alone, so a line plot shows their whole variation.
Its two panels use a logarithmic scale for $K\ell^4$ from $10^{-3}$ to $10^2$ and a signed logarithmic scale for $R\ell^2$ from $-10$ to zero, linear within $10^{-3}$; both curves lie within their panels.
The generator checks every sampled value in sixty digits against the stored formula, the proper radial distance and the minimum sphere at the throat, the finite extrema $K = 12/\ell^4$ and $R = -2/\ell^2$ there, the symmetry of both curves, their monotone approach to zero, their length scaling, and the falloffs $12\ell^4/r^8$ and $-2\ell^2/r^4$.

The `exponential_metric` figure plots both scalars against the isotropic radius over $0 \le r/m \le 6$ at $m = 1$, the one chart that covers both sides of the throat $r = m$, which a bar marks.
The far side $r < m$ reaches a horizon of infinite area at $r = 0$, squeezed into a short interval of $r$, where both scalars peak and then vanish; its two panels are linear, $K m^4$ from 0 to 0.8 and $R m^2$ from $-0.6$ to zero, and both curves lie within them.

The `kerr_melvin` figure maps $K$ over the meridian half plane of the Boyer-Lindquist chart in the oblate frame about the ring, over $0 \le \sqrt{r^2 + a^2}\sin\theta \le 10m$ and $|r\cos\theta| \le 10m$, at $m = 1$ and $a = 4m/5$, in one view at $B = 1/4m$ and one at $B = 0$, Kerr's own hole.
Both are coloured on one signed logarithmic scale from $-10^3$ to $10^3$, linear within $10^{-6}$, which $|K|$ passes only within $0.23m$ of the ring; the horizons $r_\pm$, the zeros of the published $g^{rr}$, and the ring are marked, and the cells within $m$ of the axis are read in thirty digits, where the published terms cancel to a few digits in doubles.

The `holographic_superconductor` figure plots both scalars of the Kasner inversion chart against $\rho$ from $10^{-6}$ to $10^6$ on a logarithmic axis, at $\beta = 1/2$ and $\ell = 1$, with the singularity at $\rho = 0$, so time runs from right to left.
$K$ falls as $\rho^{-5}$ before the inversion and $\rho^{-3}$ after it, which is $\tau^{-4}$ in the proper time of each Kasner epoch, and the same published $K$ at $\beta = 1$, the exact Einstein-Maxwell universe with a constant scalar field, is dashed beside it and tends to the finite $20/\ell^4$ at $\rho = 0$, where $p_t = 1$ and the horizon is regular.
Its two panels use a logarithmic scale for $K\ell^4$ from $10^{-36}$ to $10^{18}$ and a signed logarithmic scale for $R\ell^2$ from $-10^{10}$ to zero, linear within $10^{-30}$; every curve lies within its panel.
The planar chart's scalars hold the free $f$ and $\chi$, which only a numerical solution fixes, and the final Kasner chart's are one power of $\tau$, so the figure draws the inversion alone.
The generator checks both end slopes of $K$ within a hundredth, $R$ against $-(1 - \beta^2)\rho^{\beta - 2}/2\ell^2(1 + \rho^\beta)^2$ and the $\beta = 1$ scalars against their closed forms, in sixty digits.

The `interstellar_wormhole` figure plots both scalars against proper distance over $-6 \le \ell/\rho \le 6$ at $\rho = a = 1$ and $M = 1/2$, the parameters of its other drawings, from the cylinder chart over $|\ell| \le a$ and the flare chart beyond each mouth, the far flare read at $-\ell$ through `horizontal`.
Its two panels use a logarithmic scale for $K\rho^4$ from $10^{-4}$ to $10^2$ and a signed logarithmic scale for $R\rho^2$ from $-10$ to $10$, linear within $10^{-4}$; every curve lies within its panel, and the mouths $\ell = \pm a$ are marked `front`.
The generator checks every curve in sixty digits against the stored formulas, the cylinder's $K = 4/\rho^4$ and $R = 2/\rho^2$, the flare meeting it with $r = \rho$, the jumps at each mouth to $K = 128/\pi^4M^2\rho^2 + 4/\rho^4$ and $R = 2/\rho^2 - 16/\pi^2M\rho$ where $r''$ jumps from 0 to $2/\pi M$, and the fall of $K$ from each mouth outward.

The `kerr_bertotti_robinson` figure is one line movie of $K$ against $r/m$ at $m = 1$ and $B = 1/4$, the parameters of its other drawings, near the axis at $\theta = 0.1$ and on the equator, for $a = 0$ from the static chart and $a = 4/5$ from the Boyer-Lindquist chart, whose stored $K$ the reader writes out through $N$, $F$, $W$, $\omega$, $P$ and $Q$.
Each curve runs from $0.02m$ outside the outer horizon, half a pixel, since the stored $K$ is $0/0$ on it, to $r = 20m$; doubles hold the Boyer-Lindquist $K$ to $10^{-9}$ at $\theta = 0.1$, and that curve lies within a pixel of the axis's own values.
Both panels use a signed logarithmic scale for $Km^4$ from $-10$ to $10$, linear within $10^{-2}$, every curve lies within them, and each frame dashes on each panel the constant $K$ tends to far out in that direction, the stored $K$ read in 200 digits at $r = 10^4m$, drawn as Bertotti and Robinson's $K = 8/b^4$ at the $b$ that gives it.
At $a = 0$ those limits are $8B^4(1 - B^2m^2)^2 = 7.03B^4$ on the equator and $8B^4(1 + B^2m^2)^4 + 48B^{12}m^8 = 10.20B^4$ on the axis, $10.17B^4$ at $\theta = 0.1$, and at $a = 4/5$ they are $6.44B^4$ on the equator and $9.42B^4$ at $\theta = 0.1$.
The generator checks every curve in sixty digits against the stored formulas, that both stored Ricci scalars vanish, each far limit against the stored $K$ at $r = 10^5m$ and the two at $a = 0$ against their closed forms, and that the Boyer-Lindquist $K$ at $a = 0$ is the static one.

The boson star has one line view of the heaviest ground state, with central field amplitude $\sigma(0) = 0.271$, total mass $M = 0.633$ and frequency $\omega = 0.853\,\mu c$, in the same units $G = c = \mu = 1$ and over the same range $0 \le \mu r \le 20$ as its areal spacetime diagram.
Its two panels draw $K/\mu^4$ logarithmically from $10^{-7}$ to $10^{-1}$ and $R/\mu^2$ on a signed logarithmic scale from zero to $10^{-1}$, linear within $10^{-4}$ of zero; both curves lie wholly within their panels, each twice as wide as tall.
The declared functions `boson_a` and `boson_alpha` carry the existing numerical star's metric profiles and their first two derivatives into the stored scalar formulas.
The samples start at $\mu r = 0.01$, a quarter of a plot unit from the centre, to avoid removable divisions by zero in those formulas, whose finite central limits the generator checks symbolically against the regular power series.
The generator checks all 4001 samples of each scalar again in sixty-digit scalar arithmetic with the numerical profiles held, the central values $K = 0.0780214\,\mu^4$ and $R = 0.0667868\,\mu^2$, the monotone fall of $K$, the one maximum $R = 0.0684706\,\mu^2$ at $\mu r = 0.9176$, the matter trace, the vacuum limits $48M^2/r^6$ and zero, the clip and the absence of horizons.
The Ricci formula cancels near zero, so its error there is measured against a millionth of its central value, as a central curvature bounds the polytrope's cancellation error.
Both scalars depend on radius alone, so a second coordinate would repeat the curve, and no movie adds information to the still.

The `levi_civita` figure is one line movie of the Weyl chart's $K$ against $\rho/\rho_0$, with $C = 1$ and $\rho_0$ the fixed length unit in the stored powers of $\rho$.
Its nineteen frames take $\sigma = k/40$ for $k = 1, \ldots, 19$, through $1/4$, the value of the spacetime's other drawings; the flat members $\sigma = 0$ and $1/2$ have no frame.
The radial interval $1/16 \le \rho/\rho_0 \le 4$ joins the embedding's inner radius to the spacetime diagram's outer radius, and the logarithmic scale $10^{-4} \le K\rho_0^4 \le 10^4$ holds every frame whole.
The stored scalar depends on $\rho$ and the parameter $\sigma$ alone, so a spatial map would repeat each curve along its second coordinate; the movie shows the changing radial power across the family in the collection's line format.
The stored Ricci scalar vanishes for every $\sigma$.
The generator checks the stored power law, its least radial exponent $3$ at $\sigma = 1/4$, where $K\rho_0^4 = 3/(4x^3)$ with $x = \rho/\rho_0$, the equality at $\sigma$ and $1/2 - \sigma$, the absence of $C$, the vanishing of every stored Riemann component at both flat values, and the absence of horizons.
It evaluates all 4001 samples per frame again in sixty-digit arithmetic, checks every written point against the stored scalar, and holds every sampled value to the stated logarithmic limits.

The `mass_inflation` figure is a radial line movie behind the outgoing shell, in the ingoing chart with the declared function `m = ori_mass_behind(v)` and the existing charge $r_q = 24m_0/25$ in units of the final exterior mass $m_0$.
Its sixteen frames hold the interior advanced time at $v/m_0 = -10^{-k}$ for integer $k = 0, \ldots, 15$, approaching the Cauchy horizon at zero, over $0.1 \le r/m_0 \le 0.7$, a band within the shell in every frame and within the region of the existing spacetime diagram.
Every frame stays inside the existing numerical shell table; no extrapolated profile is drawn.
The movie advances uniformly in $-\log_{10}(-v/m_0)$, so each decade is visible for one step while each frame's label states its actual advanced time.
The logarithmic scale $10^2 \le K m_0^4 \le 10^{14}$ contains every sampled and written value without clipping, and the panel is 1.25 times as wide as tall.
The Ricci scalar vanishes.
An evenly spaced map of native interior time cannot resolve the inflation layer over the radial range, so the movie uses the collection's existing line format; its radial shape changes as the charge term proportional to $r^{-8}$ gives way to the growing mass term $48m(v)^2/r^6$.
At $r = 0.7m_0$, the scalar is $149/m_0^4$ at $v = -m_0$, $123/m_0^4$ at $v = -10^{-9}m_0$, and $7.31\times10^8/m_0^4$ at $v = -10^{-15}m_0$, a late rise through more than six orders of magnitude.
The generator checks every one of the 4001 radial samples per frame in sixty-digit arithmetic with the numerical mass profile held, every retained point, the logarithmic limits, the shell domain and matching relation, the change in radial shape, the stated magnitudes, and the two asymptotic powers directly from the stored scalar.
Each apparent horizon in the band is a sign change of the stored inverse metric, marked in its own frame.

The `datt_ruban_t_models` figure plots both scalars of Ruban's chart against $\eta$ over $(0, 2\pi)$ for uniform dust with $r_s = 1$, $\epsilon = 1$ and $\mu = 1/\pi$, the T-sphere movie's values, on logarithmic axes from $1$ to $10^9$ for $K r_s^4$ and from $10^{-1}$ to $10^3$ for $R r_s^2$, each least at $\eta = \pi$ with $K = 7/r_s^4$ and $R = 1/r_s^2$.

The `hiscock` figure colours the stored Kretschmann scalar in two maps at $m_0 = 1$, with the mass of every drawing of the spacetime: the ingoing chart over $0 \le r \le 6m_0$ and $-2 \le v \le 10m_0$ in 120 by 240 cells, and the outgoing chart over $0 \le r \le 16m_0$ and $-20 \le u \le 12m_0$ in 128 by 256 cells, with its declared mass `hiscock_m_out`.
Each map declares its chart's domain, $r \le R$ inside the surface of pair creation and $r \ge R$ outside it and the shell, and leaves the cells beyond it clear, so the two maps meet along $R = 3m$.
Both colour $K\,m_0^4$ on a signed logarithmic scale from 0 to $10^4$, linear within $10^{-6}$, and a larger value takes the top colour; the apparent horizon $r = 2m$ is marked in the ingoing map, and the stored Ricci scalar vanishes.
The generator checks every drawn cell against $48m^2/r^6$, forty cells of each map in sixty digits, the zero of $K$ before the shell and after the last ray, the equal $K$ of the two charts on the surface where $u = v - 18m$, and the zero of $g^{rr}$ at $r = 2m$.

The `tolman_bondi` figure is a heat map of the stored Kretschmann scalar over comoving shell label and proper time, for the cloud released from rest that the embedding draws.
Its density at release is proportional to $1 - r^2/r_b^2$, its total mass satisfies $2GM/c^2 = r_b/2$, and its declared functions are $R = r\,\mathtt{lw\_r}(t\sqrt{k},1)$ and $E = -kr^2/2$, with $k = (5 - 3r^2)/4$ inside $r_b = 1$ and $1/(2r^3)$ outside.
The existing declared cycloid and its derivatives enter the stored scalar directly, with no new profile reader.
The map spans $0 \le r/r_b \le 1.5$, including the vacuum exterior, and $0 \le ct/r_b \le \pi/\sqrt{5}$, from release to the first central collapse; both coordinates change the curvature, so the map shows the different rates of shell collapse together.
Its 240 by 225 cells use a logarithmic scale from $10^{-1}$ to $10^6$ for $K r_b^4$; higher values take the top colour, confined to $r < 0.29r_b$ and $ct > 1.36r_b$, and no cell falls below the lower limit.
The surface is a solid white line on a dark casing at fixed $r = r_b$, and the first singularity is marked at the upper left corner.
The generator checks every cell against the dust mass-density identity, the stored Ricci scalar against $2M'/(R^2R')$, the stored radial pressure against zero, independent sixty-digit cycloids at 58 cells, the release and collapse times, the absence of shell crossings, the Schwarzschild exterior, and the colour clip.
It checks the central values $375/(16r_b^4)$ at release and $36443.137/r_b^4$ at $ct = 1.3r_b$, the surface values $3/r_b^4$ and $14.285770/r_b^4$ at those times, and the central growth $80/[27c^4(t_c-t)^4]$ toward the first singularity.

The `bonnor_magnetic_dipole` figure colours the stored Kretschmann scalar over the meridian half plane at $m = 1$ and $b = 2\sqrt{2}\,m$, the parameters of its existing diagrams.
It uses the landed `meridian` frame, $r\sin\theta$ across and $r\cos\theta$ up, over the existing radial interval $4 < r/m \le 8$, with the complement left clear by its declared domain.
The strut $r = 4m$ is a solid white semicircle on a dark casing, with the degenerate horizons at its two endpoints; these are coordinate lengths, and the whole inner semicircle represents the axis between the holes.
Both radius and polar angle change the curvature, so the map shows their variation together.
Its 160 by 320 cells use a logarithmic scale from $10^{-4}$ to $10$ for $K m^4$, and every coloured cell lies within it.
The generator evaluates every cell from the stored formula in sixty digits because its numerator loses digits near a pole, checks the double evaluation against it, and the curvature tests evaluate every written cell again.
It checks the vanishing Ricci scalar, reflection symmetry, length scaling, domain and scale limits, the strut's vanishing azimuthal circumference and chord accuracy, and the double zero of $g_{tt}$ at each horizon.
The stated values are $0.00124866/m^4$ on the axis and $0.000194185/m^4$ on the equator at $r = 8m$, and the limits $131/(64m^4)$ along the exterior axis and $0.0574134/m^4$ along the equator toward $r = 4m$.

The `bubbling_ads` figure colours the rings chart's ten-dimensional $K$ over $0 \le r/L^2 \le 3$ and $0 \le y/L^2 \le 1.5$ for the black ring $L^2 < r < \sqrt{2}\,L^2$ at $L = 1$, the ring of its conformal view "rings".
Its declared $G$ and $V$ are Lin, Lunin and Maldacena's closed form for concentric circles from `bubbling_ads.functions_text()`, and every one of its 200 by 100 cells is evaluated in sixty digits, since the double evaluation loses digits far out and toward $y = 0$, where the chart is singular.
Its colours run logarithmically from $10$ to $10^4$ for $K\,L^4$, and the white of the plane $y = 0$ is drawn along the bottom edge.
The generator checks the vanishing $R$, the single disc's $K = 80/L^4$ and the plane wave's $K = 0$, the colouring and the shrinking spheres toward $y = 0$, $K$ finite there on the axis, the ring and its edges, its approach to $80/L^4$ far out, and the scale.

### The three shapes

A curvature figure has one or more views, each of one of three shapes.

- A line view plots one coordinate across and stacks one or more panels over it, each a scalar on an axis of its own, as Bardeen's $K$ stands above its $R$: the spacetime's own curve, any comparison read from another spacetime's published scalar, as Schwarzschild's of the same mass, or from another chart's, as each of Einstein's clusters beside the other, and a vertical line at each horizon.
- A map colours a box of two coordinates, drawn at one scale on both axes, by a scalar at the centre of each cell of an even grid over it, as the Bach-Weyl ring's $K$ over the half plane of Weyl's $\rho$ and $z$, with points and curves marked on it; the box may instead be the meridian plane of a radius and an angle from the axis drawn as polar coordinates, as the C-metric's $K$ over $r\sin\theta$ and $r\cos\theta$, or the meridian plane of oblate spheroidal coordinates about a ring, as the Kerr-Newman black hole's $K$ over Kerr and Schild's $\sqrt{r^2 + a^2}\sin\theta$ and $r\cos\theta$.
- A movie is either of those stepped through a parameter or a time, a frame for each value, as Bardeen's black hole is stepped through its charge $g$ from $0$ to $r_s/2$, and it plays as an embedding diagram's movie does.

Two scalars never share an axis, since they have different units and different ranges: $K$ and $R$ each have a panel of their own.

### Scales

An axis of values, a panel's vertical axis or a map's colour, is of one of three kinds, and the position of a value $v$ along it, in the axis's own units, is

- `linear`: $v$;
- `log`: $\log_{10}v$, for $v > 0$ alone;
- `symlog`: $\mathrm{asinh}(v/2L)/\ln 10$, with $L$ the scale's `linear` width, which is linear in $v$ within $|v| < L$ and tends to $\pm\log_{10}|v/L|$ far outside it, so that a scalar that changes sign and spans decades, as Bardeen's $R$ does, is drawn whole with its zero in sight.

The axis runs from the position of its `from` to the position of its `to`, and a value beyond either end is cut at that end: a curve leaves its panel there, and a cell takes the colour of the end it passes.
Every view says in its `clip` how each axis of values is scaled and where a value is cut, and the page prints it after "scale".
A logarithmic scale suits a scalar of one sign, a signed one a scalar that changes sign, and a linear one a scalar that spans no decades; the research list gives the clip each spacetime needs.

### The construction

Every value is the published formula evaluated at its point, through the checker's `Reader`, with every name the chart holds as a function written out, as Bach and Weyl's $\psi$ and $\gamma$ are in their elliptic integrals, and $c$ set to 1.
`Published` in the script reads a scalar, a component of the inverse metric or a component of the Riemann or the Weyl tensor that way, and stops on a value that depends on a coordinate or a parameter the figure neither draws nor sets, since the figure would then hold at one value of it alone.
A curve may hold the chart's other coordinates at numbers it states, as the Curzon-Chazy particle's $K$ is drawn against $r$ at $\theta = 0$ and at $\theta = \pi/2$, and `Held` reads the published value at them.
A chart that leaves a function free, as Bonnor and Vaidya's outgoing chart leaves its mass $m(u)$ and its charge $q(u)$, is drawn with each such function declared, as the rows of `null_rays.py` declare theirs: a closed form in sympy's spelling of the chart's coordinates and parameters, with the names of `DECLARED_FUNCTIONS` beside the elementary ones, as `(3 - tanh(2*(u - 1)))/4` for the mass of that star as it radiates.
`Published` puts each declared form in place of its function before it reads anything else, and stops on a free function left undeclared, as it stops on a stray coordinate.
The parameters are those the spacetime's other drawings take, and each curve and each map gives them as the doubles it was evaluated at.
A curve is sampled at 4001 points across its axis, or across the part of it a comparison's spacetime or the spacetime's own chart covers, as Reissner and Nordström's $r > 0$ beside Brill's whole line of $r$ and Bowers and Liang's star from its centre to its surface, and kept where it is finite and within a quarter of its panel's height of the box, each stretch carried one sample further each way so that the line drawn runs to the edge it leaves by.
Each stretch is thinned by Ramer, Douglas and Peucker in the panel's own units, 520 across, so that it strays from the samples by less than $0.05$ of them, and its points are written as the doubles they were computed at.
A horizon is a change of sign of the published $g^{xx}$ along the drawn coordinate $x$, found by bisection to the last digit, as the null ray diagrams find theirs.
A map's cells hold its scalar at their centres, read in the map's frame, written to six significant figures.
A map whose chart covers part of its box alone, as the cosmological chart of Khan and Penrose's colliding waves covers the triangle $|\sigma| \le \tau$, declares the chart's domain as a closed form in sympy's spelling of its coordinates and parameters, as `Abs(sigma) <= tau`, and a cell whose centre lies outside it is `null`, where the page leaves the plot's ground showing.

The script checks two dozen points of every curve, and forty cells of every map and the cells beside its singularity, against the published formula evaluated again in sixty digits, which must agree to a part in $10^9$.
Each spacetime's function also checks every figure its captions state: for Bardeen's black hole $K = 24r_s^2/g^6$ and $R = 12r_s/g^3$ at the centre, the zero of $R$ at $r = 2g$, its least value and where it lies, and the two horizons meeting at the extremal charge; for the Bach-Weyl ring its growth as $e^{a/\pi^2l_1}$ from outside the ring and its fall as $e^{-a/\pi^2l_1}$ from inside, measured in eighty digits at $l_1$ from $10^{-6}$ to $10^{-8}\,a$, its growth as $e^{\ln^2(l_1/a)/2\pi^2}$ from directly above, its value on the axis against Semerák's closed form of 2016, and the two points of the axis where $K$, his closed form and every published component of the Riemann tensor vanish; for the C-metric $R = 0$, $K$ at $\alpha = 0$ against Schwarzschild's published scalar, its values $1024/243m^4$ and $16/243m^4$ at the two poles of the black hole horizon and their ratio $64$, the identity $K = 48\alpha^6m^2(d/r)^6$ with $d = r\cos\theta + 1/\alpha$ that makes every level a conic with its focus at $r = 0$, the parabola of the level $48\alpha^6m^2$ and $K$ tending to it along $\theta = 0$, the growth as $48m^2/r^6$ toward $r = 0$, the Hong-Teo chart's published $K$, the horizons on twelve rays and across every point marked, and the circumference of a small circle about each half axis from the published $g_{\theta\theta}$ and $g_{\phi\phi}$, $2\pi$ times its radius about $\theta = 0$ and half that about the string; for the point mass $R = 0$, $K$ against $8m^2(6r^2 - 4mr + m^2)/(r - m)^8$, $\Phi = 1 - m/r$ and $g_{\theta\theta} = (r - m)^2$, no change of sign of $g^{rr}$, the growth of $K$ as $24m^4/(r - m)^8$, measured in sixty digits, $K$ falling at every $r$ drawn and above Schwarzschild's $48m^2/r^6$ at every $r > m$, their difference having no root there, $K$ tending far out to $1 + 22m/3r$ times Schwarzschild's, and Schwarzschild's $48/m^4$ at $r = m$; for the dust universe $K = 48(8 - 12\Phi + 5\Phi^2)/L^4\Phi^8$ and $R = -12/L^2\Phi^3$ with $\Phi^2 = g_{rr}$, both the same at $t$ and $-t$, $K$ least and $R$ nearest zero at $t = 0$, $R$ negative at every $t$ and equal to $-24\pi G\rho/c^2$ with $\rho = \rho_0/\Phi^3$, and the growth of $K$ as $3L^4/2(L - |ct|)^8$ and of $R$ as $-3L/2(L - |ct|)^3$, measured in sixty digits; for the plane wave $\Phi = 1 + \tfrac{1}{2}\cos(k(x - ct))$ the published $K$ and $R$ vanishing and its $G^t{}_t$ not; and every clip.
For Taub-NUT space it checks the published $R$ vanishing, $K$ the real part of $48(m + il)^2/(r + il)^6$, and so $48(m^2 + l^2)\cos(6\psi - 2\beta)/\Sigma^3$ with $\psi$ the angle of $r + il$ and $\beta$ that of $m + il$, at every $r$ of every frame, and Schwarzschild's published scalar at $l = 0$, $K$ finite at every $r$ drawn, the horizons $r_\pm = m \pm \sqrt{m^2 + l^2}$ in every frame, $r_+$ rising and $r_-$ falling as $l$ grows, $K = 48(l^2 - m^2)/l^6$ at $r = 0$ in sixty digits, below zero for $l < m$, zero at $l = m$ and above zero beyond, and $K$ odd in $r$ at $l = m$; at $l = m/2$ $K$ inside its panel, greatest and least between the horizons, and changing sign six times on the range drawn, at $r = l\cot(\beta/3 + \pi/12 + k\pi/6)$, the last inside $r_+$; $K$ falling off far out as $48(m^2 - l^2)/r^6$ on both sides in every frame, and at $l = 2m$ its six changes of sign out to $100\,m$, the last at $18.6\,m$, past the range drawn, with $K$ below zero outside the first and the last; $K$ at $l = m/20$ within a percent of Schwarzschild's outside $r = 1.316\,m$; Schwarzschild's $K$ leaving the top of its panel once, at $(48/10^4)^{1/6}\,m$; and the $l$ below which $K$ passes the top and the bottom of its panel, each crossed once as $l$ grows and held to every frame.
For the topological star it checks the same $K$ and $R$ in Bah and Heidmann's chart and the ingoing chart, the chart about the bubble's at $r = r_B + (r_B - r_S)\rho^2/4$ and the extremal chart's at $r_S = r_B = m$ and $r = \rho + m$, the Einstein metric's $g_{tt}$, $g_{rr}$ and $g_{\theta\theta}$ as $\sqrt{g_{yy}}$ times the star's, $K$ on the bubble, $291/64r_B^4$ in the star and $16384/729r_S^4$ in the black string, positive and falling at every $r > r_B$, the numerators of $K$ and of its derivative having no root there, the growth of the Einstein metric's $K$ and $R$ toward the bubble as $15/1024r_B(r - r_B)^3$ and $3/32\sqrt{r_B}(r - r_B)^{3/2}$ in the star and as $5/144r_S(r - r_B)^3$ and $-\sqrt{3}/12\sqrt{r_S}(r - r_B)^{3/2}$ in the black string, measured in sixty digits at $r - r_B = 10^{-20}$ and $10^{-30}$, its $K$ positive and crossing the $K$ of five dimensions once, at $2.891\,r_B$ and at $4.428\,r_S$, above it inside and below it outside, $K$ falling off far out as $12(r_S^2 + r_Sr_B + r_B^2)/r^6$ and the Einstein metric's as Schwarzschild's $3(2r_S + r_B)^2/r^6$, $R = r_Sr_B/2r^4$, $3/8r_B^2$ and $32/27r_S^2$ on the bubble, the Einstein metric's $R$ above the star's at every $r$, both falling off as $3r_B^2/8r^4$, and in the black string changing sign once, at the horizon, and below the black string's at every $r$, no horizon in the star and the one at $r_S$ in the black string, and every clip.
For Schwarzschild's black hole it checks $K = 12r_s^2/r^6$ and $R = 0$ in all three charts, the published Weyl tensor equal to the published Riemann tensor, the one horizon at $r = r_s$ in the ingoing and the spherical chart, $K = 12/r_s^4 = 3c^8/4G^4M^4$ there, $K$ positive and falling at every $r$ drawn, the eigenvalues $0$, $-r_s/r^3$ and $r_s/2r^3$ twice of $R^a{}_{bcd}u^bu^d$ from the ingoing chart's published Riemann tensor for bodies falling straight in at three energies, at radii inside and outside the horizon, with $K$ eight times the sum of their squares, the stretch $c^2/r_s^2$ at the horizon within five percent of $10^{10}\,\mathrm{s^{-2}}$ at the Sun's mass and of $10^{-8}\,\mathrm{s^{-2}}$ at $10^9$ of it, and where $K$ leaves the top of its panel.
For Roberts's collapsing scalar field it checks $K\,\ell^4e^{-4\tau}$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu\,\ell^2e^{-2\tau}$ the same at every $\tau$, measured in sixty digits, the double null, advanced and areal charts' $K$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ against the scaling chart's at the same event, $K = 3(R^\mu{}_\mu)^2$ in all five charts, the spheres shrinking to a point at $u = v = 0$, $K$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ vanishing on the first ray $x = 0$ with $K/x^2$ tending to $12p^4$, $K$ positive and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ negative at every $x > 0$ drawn, one greatest $K$ and one least $R^\mu{}_\mu$ at the same $x$ for $p \le 1$, at $p = 1$ the closed forms $3(1 - e^{-2x})^2e^{-4x}$ and $-(1 - e^{-2x})e^{-2x}$ and their extremes $3/16$ and $-1/4$ at $x = \tfrac{1}{2}\ln 2$, the fall of $K$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ far out as the areal radius to the powers $-6$ and $-3$ at $p = 9/10$ and $-4$ and $-2$ at $p = 1$, measured in sixty digits, at $p = 1$ the growth of $K$ as $12/u^2v^2$ and of $R^\mu{}_\mu$ as $2/uv$ toward the null singularity $u = 0$ along every ray of constant $v$, where $g_{\theta\theta}$ vanishes, at $p = 2$ the spheres along a ray growing up to the apparent horizon $\tanh^{-1}(1/p^2)$ alone and shrinking to zero area at $x_s = \tfrac{1}{2}\ln 3$, toward which $K$ grows as $1/108(x_s - x)^4$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ as $-1/18(x_s - x)^2$, measured in sixty digits, $K = 27/4$ at the apparent horizon, $K$ below the threshold's and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ above it at every $x$ at $p = 9/10$ and the reverse at $p = 2$, and every clip, found by bisection and held to the samples each curve is cut at.
It refuses to write while any check fails.

### Checking the figures

`build_mfs_data.py` loads every file with `load_curvature`, and refuses one drawn from a field its metric no longer publishes, naming the command that redraws it, as it refuses the other diagrams.
It refuses as well a file out of the shape below: a panel or a map outside 1:2 to 2:1, or a stack of panels outside it as a whole; a scale of no known kind, a logarithmic scale reaching zero, a tick outside its axis or a label that is not TeX; a curve that does not run one way across its axis or holds another coordinate at no number, a map read in a frame of no known kind or about a ring whose radius it names among no parameter it was evaluated at, a value that is not a number, is written as `-0.0` or is not above zero on a logarithmic scale, and a curve or a map drawn from a chart or a scalar the file's `source` does not stamp; a movie out of order or saying how it loops; a curve or a map that leaves a function of its chart free undeclared, declares one its chart does not leave free, or writes one as no closed form; a map that leaves a cell null without declaring its chart's domain, declares a domain that is not a closed form, or leaves every cell null; and a class drawn that the legend does not name, or named and not drawn.
It stamps each file into the index as `curvature`, and holds the symbols of every text to "Every symbol is defined" above, as it holds a drawing's.
`_tools/test_build_mfs_data.py` holds every text to the rules for prose, for the captain's voice and for dashes, as it holds the other diagrams', plays every movie through the page's own player, and holds the names of its frames to `MfsTurn.steady()`, and `node _tools/derivations/tex_check.cjs` sets every TeX string of the files.
`node _tools/page_timing.mjs` draws the figures as a reader sees them, on a desktop, on a phone at three times the usual text and in print, and holds every number and name to the rules of "The reader's text size" above and the figure to having no glow.

### The file, which the application reads

The application downloads the file for a spacetime whose index entry carries `curvature`, the stamp of the whole file, which changes when the file does and not otherwise.
Every number is a plain JSON number, never written as `-0.0`, every text is TeX in `$...$`, inside prose where the text is prose, and nothing in the file needs any relativity to draw.

    {
      "metric": "bardeen",
      "source": [{"metric": "bardeen", "system": "static", "fields": [...], "version": "..."}, ...],
      "views": [view, ...]
    }

`source` is what `build_mfs_data.py` checks the file against, one entry for every coordinate system the figure read, which may be another spacetime's, as Bardeen's figure reads Schwarzschild's scalars from `schwarzschild.json`; the application can ignore it.
`views` holds at least one view.
A view is:

- `id`: unique in the file.
- `label`: the name of its button, TeX, wanted only when the figure has more than one view to show.
- `system`, optional: the coordinate system the view belongs to. A view without it belongs to every chart, as every view does today, since a scalar is the same in every chart. Show the views that name the chart being read and then the views that name none, as the conformal diagram does.
- `kind`: `"line"` or `"map"`.
- `x`: the horizontal axis, `{"name", "from", "to", "ticks"}`: its name as TeX, as `$r/r_s$`, the values of the drawn coordinate at its two ends in the unit the name gives, and its ticks, each `{"at", "label"}`, at a value with its label as TeX, inside the axis and in order.
- `panels`, a line view's: one or more, stacked from the top, each `{"id", "name", "aspect", "scale", "ticks"}`: an id unique in the view, the name of its vertical axis as TeX, as `$K\,r_s^4$`, its width over its height, between 1:2 and 2:1, its scale, `{"kind", "from", "to"}` with `"linear"` beside them for a signed scale, as "Scales" above defines them, and its ticks, each at a value of the scalar.
- `y`, a map's: its vertical axis, as `x` is. A map is drawn at one scale on both axes, so its width over its height is `(x.to - x.from)/(y.to - y.from)`, between 1:2 and 2:1.
- `colour`, a map's: `{"name", "scale", "ticks"}`, the quantity the colour shows, as `$K\,a^4$`, its scale and the ticks of its colour bar.
- `map`, a map's: `{"metric", "system", "scalar", "x", "y", "frame", "fixed", "params", "nx", "ny", "cells"}`: the chart and the scalar the values are, `"kretschmann"` or `"ricci_scalar"`; the chart's names of the two drawn coordinates, as `"\\rho"` and `"z"`; optionally the frame `"meridian"`, in which `x` names a radius and `y` an angle from the axis and the box is their meridian plane, $X = x\sin y$ across and $Y = x\cos y$ up, so that a cell is read at $x = \sqrt{X^2 + Y^2}$ and $y = \mathrm{atan2}(X, Y)$ of its centre $(X, Y)$, or the frame `"oblate"`, with `focus` the name among `params` of the radius $f$ of a ring, in which the box is the meridian plane of oblate spheroidal coordinates about that ring, $X = \sqrt{x^2 + f^2}\sin y$ across and $Y = x\cos y$ up on the sheet $x \ge 0$, so that a cell is read at the root $x^2 \ge 0$ of $x^4 - (X^2 + Y^2 - f^2)x^2 - f^2Y^2 = 0$ and at $y = \mathrm{atan2}(Xx, Y\sqrt{x^2 + f^2})$, while without a frame the box's axes are the two coordinates themselves; the parameters it was evaluated at, by the names the chart spells them with; and `cells`, `ny` rows from the bottom of the box to its top, each of `nx` values from its left to its right, the value of row $j$ and column $i$ being the scalar at $x = x_0 + (i + \tfrac12)(x_1 - x_0)/n_x$ and $y = y_0 + (j + \tfrac12)(y_1 - y_0)/n_y$, the box running from $(x_0, y_0)$ to $(x_1, y_1)$. Where the chart leaves a function free, the map carries `functions` as well, each free function's name, as `"m"`, with the closed form it was evaluated with, as `"(3 - tanh(2*(u - 1)))/4"`. Where the chart has coordinates beyond the two drawn and the scalar holds one of them, as Ehlers and Kundt's inertial charts hold $Y$, the map carries `fixed`, as a curve does: the chart's other coordinates by the names it spells them with, each at the number every cell was evaluated at, as `{"Y": 0}`, which the checker puts back in; a map whose scalar needs no coordinate off its box carries none, as every other map does. Where the chart covers part of the box alone, the map carries `domain`, the closed form in sympy's spelling of the chart's coordinates and parameters that holds where the chart does, as `"Abs(sigma) <= tau"`, and each cell whose centre it does not hold at is `null`, which a reader leaves undrawn; a map without `domain` has no null cell.
- `curves`, a line view's: the curves drawn in every frame, each `{"panel", "class", "metric", "system", "scalar", "coordinate", "fixed", "params", "points"}`: the panel it stands on, its class, the chart, scalar and parameters it was evaluated from, the chart's name of the drawn coordinate, optionally `fixed`, the chart's other coordinates the curve holds fixed, by the names the chart spells them with, each at the number it was evaluated at, as `{"\\theta": 0}`, and its points `[x, value]`, at least two, `x` strictly increasing inside the axis. A scalar that leaves its panel and comes back is two curves of one class. A curve carries `functions` where its chart leaves a function free, as a map does.
- `horizontal`, optional on a curve: `{"expression", "values"}`, the plotted horizontal coordinate as a closed form in the chart's plain coordinate names, its numeric parameters, its declared functions, and its fixed coordinates, together with one native coordinate value for each point, in the same order.
  For example, `{"expression": "a*sin(chi)", "values": [0, 0.7853981633974483]}` maps two samples of the native coordinate `chi` to areal radius at the fixed proper time of a dust ball.
  The existing `points` remain `[x, value]` in plotted coordinates, so a reader draws them unchanged; a value checker evaluates the scalar at the corresponding native coordinate and separately evaluates `expression` to recover `x`.
  Native values are finite and strictly monotone, increasing or decreasing; plotted `x` still strictly increases.
  The expression admits numbers, `pi`, arithmetic, powers, and the elementary functions `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan`, `sinh`, `cosh`, `tanh`, `sqrt`, `exp`, `log`, and `Abs`, each of one argument.
  The build refuses malformed expressions, undeclared names, coordinates outside the chart, missing native samples, and samples out of order.
- `marks`: the marks drawn in every frame. On a line view each is `{"class", "x"}`, a vertical line through every panel at that value of the drawn coordinate; on a map each is `{"class", "at"}`, a point `[x, y]`, or `{"class", "points"}`, a polyline of them.
- `movie`, optional: `{"variable", "seconds", "frames"}`, as an embedding diagram's: the name of what steps, as `$g$`, how long one pass takes, and at least two frames, each `{"label", "value"}` with the frame's own `curves` and `marks`, and on a map its own `map` where its cells change, drawn over what the view draws in every frame. The values strictly increase, and each label is one formula with its number after an `=`, as `$g = 0.333\,r_s$`, for `MfsTurn.steady()`. A movie carries no word for how it repeats and does not turn.
- `legend`: `[kind, class, text]` for each class drawn, the kind `line` or `point`; every class drawn is named once, and nothing is named that is not drawn.
- `caption`: a list of paragraphs, the first opening with a compact noun phrase naming what is drawn, its values in parentheses, as every caption on the page does.
- `ricci_sentences`, optional: on a view that draws a panel of each scalar, the sentences of `caption` the page shows under the Ricci scalar's panel, each `[paragraph, sentence]` counted from 0, in order; every other sentence it shows under the Kretschmann scalar's, the sentence about both included. A paragraph's sentences each run to a full stop that one space follows outside TeX, so every sentence of such a caption ends with a full stop, and the page puts each place's sentences back together in their order, a paragraph left with none dropped. Without it the whole caption stands under the Kretschmann scalar. `curvature.py` writes it for every view that draws both scalars, an empty list where no sentence is the Ricci scalar's alone, and the application can ignore it.
  Such a caption opens "The Kretschmann scalar $K$ and the Ricci scalar $R$ of", or $R^\mu{}_\mu$ in place of $R$, and each place opens on its own scalar: "The Kretschmann scalar $K$ of" under the one and "The Ricci scalar $R$ of" under the other, the rest of that first sentence as written, as `per_scalar` writes it, except that the Ricci scalar's place stands its scalar beside only what its own panel draws: it drops the caption's "beside ..." where that panel draws no comparison, and names a comparison that vanishes there as its legend does, "beside Schwarzschild's $R = 0$", as `RICCI_BESIDE` in `curvature.py` sets it and `curvature.py` checks against the legend; `build_mfs_data.py` refuses a caption with `ricci_sentences` that opens otherwise.
  Every other sentence of such a caption names one scalar, or states how $K$ stands to $R$, as $K = 5(R^\mu{}_\mu)^2/3$ does and stays with $K$: a fact of each, as "Far out $K$ approaches Schwarzschild's and $R$ falls off as ...", is written as two sentences, one for each place.
- `per_scalar`, optional: on a view that draws a panel of each scalar, what each of its two places states of its own scalar alone, `{"kretschmann": place, "ricci_scalar": place}`, each place `{"opening", "clip", "legend"}`: the caption's first sentence naming that place's scalar alone, which `build_mfs_data.place_opening()` holds to the caption's own, its scale, TeX prose printed after "scale" in place of `clip`, and its legend, entries as `legend`'s, naming every class its panels draw, a curve on one of them in any frame or a mark, and nothing else. A comparison takes the name of what that panel draws of it, as Schwarzschild's $R$, which vanishes, is "Schwarzschild's $R = 0$" where the view's own legend names Schwarzschild's black hole. `curvature.py` writes it for every view that draws both scalars, and the application can ignore it, reading `clip` and `legend` as before.
- `settings`: TeX prose giving the parameter values drawn at, printed after "drawn at".
- `input`, optional: TeX prose naming a declared function or matter, printed after "drawn with".
- `clip`: TeX prose saying how each axis of values is scaled and where a value is cut, printed after "scale".

The classes drawn today are `K` for a spacetime's own Kretschmann scalar, `R` for its Ricci scalar and `reference` for a comparison, among the curves, and `horizon`, `ergosurface`, where $g_{tt}$ vanishes, `string` for a cosmic string, `singular` for a point or, on a map, a curve where the scalar diverges, `zero` for a point where it vanishes and `front` for the front of an impulsive wave, among the marks.

### Drawing the figure

The page lays a curvature figure out as it lays out a spacetime diagram, by the same grid, on the same ground and with the same words, tick marks and frame, so that its words grow with the reader's text around a plot never narrower than at the usual size, and the figure scrolls sideways in its own frame where they need more room than the panel has.
Each panel's vertical name, its numbers and its plot stand in a row, 1.5 em of the figure's words apart from the next, and the horizontal axis's numbers and name stand under the last; a map's colour bar, its numbers and its name stand below those.
`cvFigure()` in `_layouts/mfs.html` writes the rows and the areas of the grid on the figure, since their number follows its panels, and `fitPlots()`, `fitAxisNames()` and `fitTicks()` take every panel of a figure and its colour bar.
Each plot is 520 units wide and 520 over its `aspect` tall, its curves cut at its box, its grid drawn at its ticks, and a panel whose scale runs through zero draws its zero as a faint line.

The figure stands in two places: its panels of the Ricci scalar under "Ricci scalar diagram", after the Ricci scalar, and its other panels under "Kretschmann scalar diagram", after the Kretschmann scalar.
A panel is the Ricci scalar's when a curve drawn on it in any frame is read from `ricci_scalar`, and a map when its cells are, as `curvature_ricci_panels()` in `build_mfs_data.py` reads it too; a figure of the Kretschmann scalar alone stands after the Kretschmann scalar only.
Each place shows the views that draw a panel of its own, with a button for each where it has more than one, and each view there its own panels, its caption opened on its own scalar and split as `ricci_sentences` says, and its own legend and scale from `per_scalar`.
Without `per_scalar` a place takes the view's legend entries for what its panels draw, their curves and every mark, since a mark runs through every panel, and the view's whole `clip`.
Both places print the view's settings and the function it is drawn with.
A view chosen in one place is chosen in the other where the other shows it, and a movie in both places is one movie: its two figures show one frame, and the button of either plays and pauses both.

A spacetime's own $K$ is drawn in the pink of the light rays that move right, `--pink-light`, and its $R$ in the cyan of those that move left, `--mfs-cyan-light`, each 2 wide; a `reference` in dashes of `--text-mid`; a `horizon` in the dashes a spacetime diagram marks $g^{rr} = 0$ with, in the cyan of the light rays on a line view and in white on a map, whose colours are that cyan; an `ergosurface` in the dots a conformal or an embedding diagram marks one with, in white, as it is drawn on maps alone; a `string` in the gold of a world line, 3 wide; a `front` in solid white, 2 wide; a `singular` point in `--pink-dark` ringed in black, and a `singular` curve on a map solid in `--pink-dark`, 3 wide; and a `zero` point as an open white circle, each point a circle of radius 5 units drawn over the frame.
Every line marked on a map, as the C-metric's horizons and string, Bonnor and Vaidya's anti-trapped spheres and Khan and Penrose's wave fronts are, stands on a solid black casing 4 units wide, so that it reads on every colour of the ramp, its own colour where the ramp is dark and the casing where it is near white.
A `singular` curve stands on one 8 units wide, since where a map's chart ends at a singularity its null cells meet the drawn ones in steps half a cell out to either side of it, which the casing covers.
On paper they take the print copy's inks, as the print stylesheet sets them, and a line on a map stands on a white casing.
A map's cells are an image of a pixel each, the bottom row of the file at its bottom, which the browser smooths as it draws it across the plot, and a null cell is a clear pixel, under which the plot's ground shows, with no value under the pointer.
A cell's colour is its value's place on the scale, from 0 at `from` to 1 at `to` and clamped at both, on a ramp of nine stops, mixed linearly between neighbouring stops in sRGB.
A scale that runs through zero takes the ramp of two hues, with zero at its middle grey and each side running as far toward its end as the farther end of the scale reaches, and any other scale the ramp of one hue:

| ramp | on the dark ground | on paper |
| --- | --- | --- |
| one hue | `#04191e` `#093139` `#104a56` `#186574` `#1f8295` `#279fb6` `#30bed9` `#38dfff` `#d2f6ff` | `#ebf8fc` `#c2e1ea` `#99cad8` `#71b3c5` `#4a9cb0` `#268499` `#046c7f` `#005160` `#003742` |
| two hues | `#79e6ff` `#20bedb` `#1a8fa5` `#215e6b` `#2e2e2e` `#813b50` `#c65377` `#ff77a0` `#ffc1cf` | `#036071` `#1f889e` `#6bb1c3` `#aed2db` `#f2f2f2` `#eebbc5` `#e3839a` `#cb3e6c` `#9e0148` |

Each ramp was worked out in OKLCH: the one hue at the page's cyan, hue 214, its lightness rising from 0.20 to 0.95 on the dark ground and falling from 0.97 to 0.31 on paper, and the two hues the cyan and the pink of the light rays about a neutral grey, each arm in equal steps of lightness, every stop brought inside sRGB by lowering its chroma alone.
`CV_RAMPS` in `_layouts/mfs.html` holds them.
The colour bar runs under the map from `from` to `to` through the same colours, at 65 places along it.

The values under the pointer stand at the top right of each plot, as a spacetime diagram's do: the coordinate to three decimals, a panel's value at the pointer's height to four figures, and on a map the value of the cell under the pointer, each in the room of the longest of its kind.
A movie's play button stands in the top left corner of the ground, in the size and colours of an embedding diagram's, and the name of the frame shown stands above the plot, every frame's name held in one cell, so that nothing in it moves as the movie plays.
`wireCurvature()` wires the movie to the page's one movie player, which plays it forward, back and forward again, only while it is on the screen, and holds it at its first frame for a reader who asked for reduced motion.
Nothing in the figure glows.
The print copy prints every view, a movie at its first frame, and a map and its bar in paper's colours; it thins the numbers of each axis and of each colour bar at the size they print at, as the screen does, and again once the copy is laid out for print, at the width it prints at, 390 pixels on a phone, where until 22 October 2026 the nine numbers under Bonnor and Vaidya's bar and the C-metric's ran into each other.

### Which spacetimes

Alcubierre's warp drive has two maps and one line view of its Cartesian chart at $v_s = 2$, $R = 1$ and $\sigma = 4$, the drive its other drawings declare, read at one moment of the plane of the ship's path: the maps colour $K$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ over the half plane of $x - x_s$ and the distance $\rho$ from that path, $|x - x_s| \le 5R/2$ and $\rho \le 5R/2$, the window of the research list, in cells a fiftieth of $R$ on a side, 250 across and 125 up, and the line view draws both scalars along the path itself, $x$ from $-3\,R$ to $3\,R$, the box of its spacetime diagram.
Each map holds the chart's $t$ and $z$ at $ct = 0$ and $z = 0$ in its grid's `fixed`, as Ehlers and Kundt's maps hold their $Y$, since neither is a coordinate it draws; at that moment $x - x_s$ is $x$, and on that plane $\rho$ is $y$.
Both scalars are axisymmetric about the path and the same at $y$ and $-y$, which is checked, so the half plane carries the whole plane.
$K$ is coloured on a signed logarithmic scale, linear within $0.1$ of zero, from $-10^3$ to $10^4$, its bar ticked at every second decade, and $R$, written $R^\mu{}_\mu$ since $R$ is the radius of the bubble, on one linear within $0.1$ of zero from $-10^2$ to $10^2$; both scalars change sign, both fall inside the bubble to under a hundredth of what they reach in the wall, and every cell lies within its colours, $K$ from $-234.1$ to $1481$ and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ from $-30.78$ to $39.33$.
The line view stacks the two scalars on panels of those same two scales, so a curve and a cell of one scalar read alike, and every value drawn lies within its panel.
Each map marks the sphere where the shift reaches the speed of light, the change of sign of the published $g^{xx} = 1 - v_s^2f^2$, as the half circle $r_s = 1.000168\,R$, and the line view marks the two points where that sphere crosses the path.
The published formulas are $0/0$ at the ship, the limit of the flat patch about it, so the line view's curves stop $0.003\,R$ away, a quarter of a plot unit, and each scalar is drawn there as two curves; no cell centre falls on the ship, since the cells of a map stand at half a cell from its edges.
Both formulas cancel to a small value in the flat space on either side of the wall, so every value checked is held to sixty digits over a floor of a millionth of the greatest value its scalar reaches, as the boson star's Ricci scalar is.
The maps show what a curve along one direction cannot: the whole of each scalar standing in the wall, a shell about $r_s = R$, and the cone about the path each keeps its sign in far outside the wall, $K$ positive within $64.7$ degrees of the path and $R^\mu{}_\mu$ negative within $77.4$ degrees of it.
The line view holds the wall itself, whose two crests of $K$, $1493/R^4$ at $r_s = 0.942\,R$ and $938/R^4$ at $1.232\,R$, stand either side of a layer where $K$ is negative, from $1.070\,R$ to $1.102\,R$, which is finer than a cell.
Both scalars are even in $x - x_s$ while the ship holds its speed: the one odd term of the published $R^\mu{}_\mu$ is $2v_s'\,\partial_xf$, whose fore and aft difference $4v_s'\,\partial_xf$ the generator measures, and every odd term of $K$ carries that same $v_s'$, so under $v_s = 2 + ct/R$ the crest of $K$ is $1208/R^4$ ahead of the ship against $1807/R^4$ behind it.
The fore and aft asymmetry the drive is known for is in the expansion of the riders, $\theta = v_s\,\partial_xf$, which is odd in $x - x_s$ at every speed and which the embedding diagram draws as a trough ahead of the ship and a crest behind it.
The file is 699 KB, and 216 KB as the server compresses it.

Ehlers and Kundt's A II and B I have two maps at $b = 1$, the length their other drawings take, of $K$ on the inertial plane of $X$ and $T$ at $Y = Z = 0$, over the boxes of the two inertial spacetime diagrams: inside the future cone of the tachyon's line, $|X| \le 3b$ with $0 \le T \le 6b$, and outside that cone, $|X|, |T| \le 3b$, each of 200 cells by 200 and each naming its own chart.
The inertial charts write $K$ as $12b^2/\sigma^6$ and $12b^2/r^6$ in the interval from that line, $\sigma = \sqrt{T^2 - X^2 - Y^2}$ and $r = \sqrt{X^2 + Y^2 - T^2}$, so each is a function of $T$, $X$ and $Y$, and the generator evaluates every cell at $Y = 0$, which the map writes into its grid as `fixed`, so that the checker reads every cell back, and which the settings and the caption of each view state for the reader.
The levels are the hyperbolas of constant interval and the colours cool away from the cone, from $12/b^4$ at the edge each chart stops on down to $12/6^6b^4$ at the top of the first box and $12/3^6b^4$ at the corners of the second, so the first map colours $K\,b^4$ logarithmically from $10^{-4}$ to $10^2$ and the second from $10^{-2}$ to $10^2$, and every cell drawn lies inside its scale.
Each chart carries one side of the cone alone, $\sigma > b$ and $r > b$, which the maps declare as their domains, so the band $|T^2 - X^2| < b^2$ about the cone is null in both, 12562 cells of the first box and 25064 of the second, and the clip and the caption of each view say where its region stops and why.
That band is A II's static region $0 < r < b$, whose chart publishes neither $T$ nor $X$ and so cannot be read on this plane; $K$ runs through it from $12/b^4$ at the horizon up without bound on the cone, which the first map marks as a `singular` line along $T^2 = X^2$ and the Kruskal chart reads as its singularity $UV = -1$.
The horizon $\sigma = b$ and the neck $r = b$ are drawn as `horizon` curves, the landed class, and beyond the neck the metric continues into a second region of the same shape, so the cone is never reached from B I and only A II's map marks it.
Both charts publish $R = 0$, so no panel draws the Ricci scalar and each caption states the vacuum in one sentence, as Visser's thin shell wormhole does.
The generator checks each published $K$ against $12b^2$ over the sixth power of its interval and, on $Y = 0$, against $12b^2/|T^2 - X^2|^3$ on the side its chart holds, the declared domains against the published ones, each chart's published $g^{ZZ}$ changing sign at its horizon where $K$ tends to $12/b^4$, the cells the same at $X$ and $-X$, the values the captions state on the axis of each box, the growth $12b^2/|T^2 - X^2|^3$ toward the cone measured at $|T^2 - X^2|$ from $10^{-4}$ to $10^{-12}$, A II's $K$ against the Kruskal chart's at $UV = (\sigma/b - 1)e^{\sigma/b}$ with the radius there falling below $10^{-60}b$ on the cone, and 299 cells again in sixty digits.
The file is 639 KB, and 126 KB as the server compresses it.

Bardeen's regular black hole has two line views at $r_s = 1$, with $r$ from 0 to $2\,r_s$, the box of its spacetime diagrams: one at $g = r_s/3$, the charge its other drawings take, and a movie of $g$ from 0 to $r_s/2$ in steps of $r_s/60$, 31 frames, through the extremal charge $2r_s/3\sqrt{3}$, where its two horizons meet.
Each stacks $K$ on a logarithmic axis from $10^{-1}$ to $10^5$ over $R$ on a signed one, linear within $0.01$ of zero, from $-10$ to $10^4$, with Schwarzschild's two scalars of the same mass dashed beside Bardeen's and the horizons marked.
The Bach-Weyl ring has one map at $m = a/2$, its $K$ over $0 \le \rho \le 3a$, $|z| \le 2a$ of Weyl's chart in 150 cells across and 200 up, each a fiftieth of $a$ on a side, coloured on a logarithmic scale from $10^{-3}$ to $10^3$, with the ring and the two points of the axis where $K$ vanishes marked; the ring is a vacuum, so it has no $R$ to draw.
Bartnik and McKinnon's solitons have three line views at $\ell = 1$, one for each of the solitons with one, two and three zeros of the Yang-Mills amplitude $w$, as the embedding diagram gives each a view of its own, each one panel of its $K$ against the areal radius $r$ from zero to $8\,\ell$, the box of that chart's spacetime diagram and of the embedding diagram.

The areal chart leaves $\sigma$, $m$ and $w$ free, and each view declares all three from `bartnik_mckinnon.py`'s solution, the profiles the spacetime diagrams declare, with `bm_w` among the declared functions for the amplitude the stored scalars do not carry.
Each curve starts $0.004\,\ell$ from the centre, a quarter of a plot unit, where the stored formula's removable divisions by the radius stay away from zero, so that it carries the centre's $384b^4/\ell^4$ to the figures the caption prints: de Sitter's $24/L^4$ at $L = 1/2b$, since the mass function grows as $2b^2r^3$ at a regular centre and $1 - 2m/r$ opens as $1 - 4b^2r^2$, a core of constant curvature, finite where the vacuum of the same mass diverges.
Schwarzschild's $K$ of the soliton's own mass is drawn dashed as a `reference`, as the vacuum paraboloid of that mass stands beside each embedding surface: it is above the soliton's $K$ at every radius drawn, passes the top of the panel inside $0.83$ to $0.88\,\ell$ and is cut there, and the soliton's own $K$ approaches it far out as the field dies away, $1.2$, $16.6$ and $22.9$ percent below it at the edge of the plot.
The published Ricci scalar vanishes on each solution, since the stress tensor of a Yang-Mills field is traceless, so no panel draws it and each caption states the vanishing in one sentence, as the three-brane states its own.
The published $g^{rr} = 1 - 2m/r$ is positive at every radius drawn, so no horizon is marked, and its least value is the neck the embedding diagram climbs, $0.242$ at $r = 1.60\,\ell$ for one zero, $0.0351$ at $1.13\,\ell$ for two and $0.00297$ at $1.02\,\ell$ for three.
$K$ falls at every radius, the fall steepening into the neck to $r^{-5.18}$, $r^{-7.63}$ and $r^{-10.64}$ a little inside it, and each added zero holds more of the curvature there, $0.519$, $3.31$ and $6.61/\ell^4$.
The scalars depend on $r$ alone, so a map over $r$ and an angle would be one colour along every sphere, and the number of zeros is no continuous parameter for a movie to step, so each soliton has a still of its own.
The generator checks every point against the published formula in sixty digits, the centre against $384b^4$, the fall at every radius and its steepening, the neck against the one the solution tabulates, the vacuum above each curve with the radius it leaves the panel at, the approach to it far out and the shortfall at the edge of the plot, the last zero of the declared amplitude, the vanishing of the published Ricci scalar against the published $K$ at every sample, the published $g^{rr}$ positive throughout, and across the three views the neck deepening toward $r = \ell$ and the masses rising toward $\ell$, the extreme Reissner-Nordström throat the last caption names.

Belinski and Zakharov's wave of two solitons has two views at $w = 1$ and $\cosh\beta = 5/4$, the wave and the constants of its other drawings: a map of its $K$ over the plane of $z$ and $ct$, and a movie of $K$ along $z$ as $t$ advances.

Each crest passes out of the map's box by its side at $ct = 2.66\,w$, with the trough behind it running on to the corner, and the movie, twice as wide, holds both to the last frame.
Far ahead of the pulses $K\,w^4$ approaches $\tfrac{3}{4}\sinh^{-4}\beta\,(w/ct)^3 = \tfrac{64}{27}(w/ct)^3$ and far behind them $\tfrac{3}{4}\cosh^{-4}\beta\,(w/ct)^3 = \tfrac{192}{625}(w/ct)^3$, the Kasner universe that expands alike in $x$ and $y$ taken with each of the two constants of $f$ the pulses stand between, in the ratio $\tanh^4\beta = 81/625$.
The generator checks the vanishing of the published Ricci scalar on the declared wave in sixty digits, the declared functions against the pole chart's published $K$, every written value against the published formula in sixty digits, the evenness in $z$, that $K\,w^4(ct/w)^3$ holds along four levels of $\sinh^2\tau/\cosh^2\xi$ at three times each, the crest and the trough at five times against their two constants and against the level each stands on, the two Kasner limits and their ratio, the two sign changes and the places of the trough and the crest at $ct = 2\,w$, the time at which each crest passes out of the map's box by its side, the movie holding both crests to the last frame, every cell inside $\tau \le 2$ and $|\xi| \le 3$, the published $g^{zz}$ positive at every $z$ drawn, and every clip.
