The black hole fireworks have one map at $r_s = 1$, $V_0 = 3/5$ and $E$ at $r = r_s/2$, the construction of their other drawings, of $K$ over the plane of $r$ and the ingoing Painlevé-Gullstrand time, in 300 cells across and 162 up, each a hundredth of $r_s$ across and $0.010018\,r_s$ up.
The box takes $0 \le r \le 3\,r_s$ from that chart's spacetime diagram and runs from $ct = -2.8\,r_s$ up to $ct = -1.17704\,r_s$, the moment the shell reaches $E$.
The research list of October 2026 asks for a heat map, since a line plot of this spacetime's $K$ against $r$ is Schwarzschild's own curve and what the bounce adds is where in the spacetime that curvature stands.
A map's box is two coordinates of one chart, and the chart that carries this one is the ingoing Painlevé-Gullstrand: it covers the flap before the bounce, runs on through the apparent horizon down to the shell, and holds the shell and the surface of time symmetry in closed form.
The exterior publishes $K = 12r_s^2/r^6$, a function of the radius alone, so the levels are the spheres of constant $r$ and the colours brighten inward, from $0.0166265/r_s^4$ at the right edge of the box to $643.188/r_s^4$ in the cell beside $E$, where the classical value is $768/r_s^4$; $K\,r_s^4$ is coloured logarithmically from $10^{-2}$ to $10^3$ and every cell drawn lies inside it.
The chart holds outside the shell alone, which the map declares as the domain $t \ge 2r_s\ln\left(V_0/(\sqrt{r/r_s} + 1)\right) + 2\sqrt{rr_s} - r$, the ingoing time at which the shell passes $r$, so the 32288 cells inside the shell are null, where space is flat and $K$ vanishes, which the caption states in one sentence.
The top edge is the Painlevé-Gullstrand time of $E$, the last moment of this chart whose whole slice outside the shell is classical: a hair above it the slice passes the edge of the quantum region, which has no metric, so no cell of the map falls in that region and the caption alone carries it.
The apparent horizon $r = r_s$ is drawn as a `horizon` line, the landed class, from the shell's crossing at $ct = -1.40795\,r_s$ up to the top of the box, and the 557 cells drawn inside it are trapped spheres.
The exterior and the flat interior both publish $R = 0$, so no panel draws the Ricci scalar and the caption states the vacuum in one sentence, as Visser's thin shell wormhole does.
The view names no chart, so it stands under all six of them, and its caption and its settings give the plane it is drawn on.
The generator checks the published $K$ against $12r_s^2/r^6$ and its holding no time, the vanishing of both published Ricci scalars and of the interior's $K$, the shell's closed form against `black_to_white_hole.py`'s own ingoing time at 1999 radii, $K = 12/r_s^4$ at the horizon and $768/r_s^4$ at $E$ in sixty digits, the declared domain holding exactly where Kruskal's $V \ge V_0$ with every cell drawn inside region II and no centre within a thousandth of a cell of the shell, the top edge against the edge of the quantum region, which stands above it at every radius and meets it at $E$, $\Delta$ at $r = 7r_s/6$ and $ct = -1.09637\,r_s$ on $U + V = 0$, the published $g^{rr}$ changing sign at $r = r_s$ alone, $K$ falling with the radius along every row of cells, the brightest and the dimmest cell drawn, and 104 cells again in sixty digits.

Black Saturn has one map of its polar chart at $L = 1$, $\kappa_1 = 7/8$, $\kappa_2 = 9/16$, $\kappa_3 = 3/7$, and $\beta = 0$, the values of its embedding diagram, a ring in balance around a hole with no angular momentum of its own: its $K$ over the quarter plane of $r\sin\theta$ and $r\cos\theta$ in the frame "meridian", as the research list of October 2026 asks.
That quarter plane is Weyl's meridian half plane folded along the axis $\rho = 0$, which runs up the left edge, where $z = r^2/2L^2$ is positive, and along the bottom edge, where it is negative, so both horizons are segments of the left edge and the plane $z = 0$ is the diagonal.
The polar chart leaves $V$, $\Omega$, $W$, and $\nu$ free and publishes both scalars in them and their first and second derivatives along $r$ and $\theta$, so the map declares all four as `bs_V`, `bs_O`, `bs_W`, and `bs_nu`, which `_tools/derivations/black_saturn.py` builds from the names Weyl's chart defines, $e^{2V} = (G_yH_x^2 - (\omega_\psi + qH_y)^2)/H_xH_y$, $\Omega = (\omega_\psi + qH_y)e^{-2V}/H_x$, $e^{2W} = G_y$, and $e^{2\nu} = k^2H_xP$, whose own definitions reach down to the solitons $\mu_i$ of the rod ends.
Each derivative is carried through that arithmetic as a second order jet, a value with its two first and three second partial derivatives, so every one is exact at the precision it is worked in; the generator holds all six of each function to central differences of the function itself, to $1.7 \times 10^{-29}$, and to `print_charts.black_saturn_functions`, the chart printer's own reading of the same names, to $2 \times 10^{-40}$.
The box is $0 \le r\sin\theta \le 9L/5$ and $3L/10 \le r\cos\theta \le 9L/5$ in 30 cells across and 25 up, each three fiftieths of $L$ on a side, which holds both horizons, the plane of the ring between them, and the fall-off above the hole.
The chart degenerates on the axis $\rho = 0$, where both horizons lie, and the stored $K$ is published as one sum of 481 terms over a denominator, so beside the hole's horizon a double makes those terms infinite at $\theta = 0.012$ and carries them at $\theta = 0.014$; Weyl's chart, whose $K$ is published factored, goes the same way within $0.05\,L^2$ of the axis above the hole.
Every cell is worked out in forty digits, through the stored expression with the four functions put in as numbers, and the box stands each cell $0.017$ of a radian from the axis and $0.33\,L$ from the centre of the chart, where a double reads the stored scalar to the six figures written as well, within $1.4 \times 10^{-6}$ of them.
$K$ is coloured on a signed logarithmic scale, linear within $10^{-2}$ of zero, from $-10^2$ to $10^4$ in units of $1/L^4$, since it changes sign between the two horizons; it passes the top at the two cells beside the ring's horizon, within $0.03\,L$ of it, and the least value drawn, $-8.4/L^4$, stays inside the bottom.

$K$ depends on $\theta$ as strongly as on $r$ near $r = 2m$, so a line plot on the axis and the equator would miss what the map shows: the curvature crowding toward the axis in the prolate field and toward the equator in the oblate one, and the axis of the oblate field finite at the singularity.
The movie shows what the stills of $q = -1/2$ and $1$ cannot, the round field of Schwarzschild between them and the zero on the axis moving out from the horizon as $q$ grows.
Its function checks $R = 0$ in both charts, the prolate spheroidal chart's $K$ against the spherical chart's at $x = r/m - 1$, $y = \cos\theta$ and $\delta = 1 + q$, $K$ at $q = 0$ against Schwarzschild's published scalar of $r_s = 2m$, $K$ on the axis against $48m^2(1 + q)^2(r - (2 + q)m)^2f^{2q}/r^6(r - 2m)^2$ and its zero at $r = (2 + q)m$, the growth of $K$ toward $r = 2m$ as $(r - 2m)^{-2(q^2 + q + 1)}$ off the axis and as $(r - 2m)^{2q - 2}$ along it, measured in sixty digits, its limit $3/4m^4$ along the axis at $q = 1$, $K$ tending far out to $48(1 + q)^2m^2/r^6$, its values on the circle $r = 3m$, its fall from the axis to the equator at $q = -1/2$ and rise at $q = 1$ on every circle drawn, $K$ above zero off the axis on a grid four times as fine as the map's in every frame, and the clip.
The file is 1054 KB, and 354 KB as the server compresses it.

Schwarzschild's black hole has one line view at $r_s = 1$, one panel of its $K$ against $r$ from 0 to $6\,r_s$, the range of the research list, with the horizon $r = r_s$ marked.
It draws $K$ on a logarithmic axis from $10^{-4}$ to $10^4$, ticked at every second decade, as the quantum Oppenheimer-Snyder black hole's is, which holds the curve from $r = 0.326\,r_s$ out, in a panel 1.25 times as wide as it is tall, so that the eight decades it falls through read beside the caption; the spacetime is a vacuum, so it has no $R$ to draw, and the caption says so, as Taub-NUT space's does.
The spherical chart covers $r > r_s$ alone, so the curve is read from the ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein chart, whose domain is $r > 0$, and the horizon from that chart's published $g^{rr} = (r - r_s)/r$; the three charts publish the same $K$, so the view names none, and the caption says $K$ is the same function of $r$ inside the horizon, in the Eddington-Finkelstein charts.
No mark stands at $r = 0$, where the curve leaves the top of its panel, since a line view marks a coordinate with a vertical line and $r = 0$ is the panel's left edge.
$K$ depends on $r$ alone, so a map over $r$ and $\theta$ would be one colour along every circle, and $r_s$ is the spacetime's one length, so a movie of it would change the scale and nothing else; it has neither, and no comparison, since it is the comparison every other black hole's figure dashes.
The caption reads $K$ as the tidal field a falling body feels, the stretch $r_sc^2/r^3$ along the radius and the squeeze of half that across it, whose squares sum to $c^4K/8$, and gives the stretch at the horizon, $c^2/r_s^2$, at the Sun's mass and at $10^9$ of it, with the Sun's $GM$ the IAU's nominal $1.3271244\times10^{20}\,\mathrm{m^3/s^2}$.
The file is 4.5 KB, and 2.1 KB as the server compresses it.

## Checking the physics

`build_mfs_data.py` checks the shape of the data. It does not read the mathematics.
`_tools/derivations/verify_metrics.py` does, for every coordinate system of every metric file.

It builds $g_{\mu\nu}$ from the line element each entry prints, computes the inverse metric, both Christoffel variants, Riemann, Ricci, the Ricci scalar, Kretschmann, Einstein and Weyl in sympy, and compares all of it against every value the entry publishes.
A component the entry omits has to vanish, so a missing symbol is caught as well as a wrong one.
It names each disagreement by file, system and symbol, and exits non-zero if any remain.

It also checks that every term of every published expression carries the dimensions of its left hand side, which needs no algebra at all.
That pass names the file, the system, the field and the term it could not balance, and it is what catches a component printed in the bare chart rather than in $x^0 = cT$.

sympy is not installed system wide, so it runs in the environment of "The test environment" above:

    _tools/setup-env.sh
    .venv.noindex/bin/python _tools/derivations/verify_metrics.py

The whole collection took 160 seconds on 29 September 2026, and `--system <metric_id>/<system_id>` checks one system in seconds, which is what to use while editing a single entry.
The slowest systems are the three charts of `gowdy`, each with three free functions of two coordinates, which took three and a half minutes together on 1 October 2026, then the general flow chart of `natario` and the potential flow of `lentz`, each under a minute, then `mixmaster` at about forty seconds, and the Gaussian normal chart of `brane_wall_universe` at about two minutes, whose Riemann, Ricci and Einstein tensors are compared with its three names written out; Kerr takes about seven seconds and the interior Schwarzschild solution about two.
`--budget <seconds>` changes how long sympy may spend on one tensor, and the default of 120 is several times what almost every tensor in the collection needs; `SYSTEM_BUDGET_SECONDS` raises it for the one system that needs more, the toroidal chart of `bach_weyl_ring`, whose Kretschmann scalar takes 100 to 130 seconds.
`--dimensions-only` runs the dimensional pass alone, which takes about a second over the whole collection, so there is no reason not to run it on every edit.

The speed is `norm`, the routine every tensor passes through, which puts an expression into a canonical form so that one that vanishes is exactly zero.
It reads the expression as a rational function of generators, reduces $\cos^2$ by $1-\sin^2$, and keeps every denominator as a product of irreducible factors, so it never takes a polynomial gcd; its docstring records what else was measured and why it lost.
Until 22 September 2026 it called sympy's general `simplify` instead, which took the whole collection about forty minutes and never finished Kerr's Riemann tensor at all.
It splits a radical into the square roots of the irreducible factors of its radicand, which is an identity only where each factor is positive.
A root above the square, as the cube root in Senovilla's $\cosh^{-2/3}(3a\rho)$, is split the same way and taken as a generator $w$ with $w^q = b$: its powers are folded below $q$ in a numerator, below a fraction line it may stand only as a factor of its own, $1/w = w^{q-1}/b$, and a sum that holds one there is left to sympy's general simplifier; `_tools/test_roots.py` holds it, and `_tools/derivations/senovilla.md` Step 2 is the worked example.
`Geometry` takes the inverse metric and its determinant by cofactors, `_adjugate`, with every minor put through `norm`, since sympy's own `adjugate()` and `det()` did not finish on a metric with three cross terms, Kerr-de Sitter's Kerr-Schild chart.
`Geometry.kretschmann` sums over the pairs $a < b$ and $c < d$ alone, four times, since the Riemann tensor is antisymmetric in each pair, and raises a pair at a time by the metric of bivectors, $g^{a\alpha}g^{b\beta} - g^{a\beta}g^{b\alpha}$; raising all 256 components one index at a time took three minutes on the pole chart of `belinski_zakharov`, past the budget of 120 seconds, and the pairs take seconds.
In two dimensions the Riemann tensor is all trace, $R_{\mu\nu\rho\sigma} = \tfrac{1}{2}R\,(g_{\mu\rho}g_{\nu\sigma} - g_{\mu\sigma}g_{\nu\rho})$, so `Geometry.weyl_llll` returns zero for a chart of fewer than three coordinates and never divides by $n - 2$; Witten's black hole is the one such spacetime, and `_tools/test_witten_black_hole.py` holds its Weyl and Einstein tensors to vanishing and its Kretschmann scalar to $R^2$.
A power of a sum, a product or a reciprocal whose exponent holds a parameter, as Zipoy and Voorhees's $(1 - 2m/r)^{q}$, is split over the irreducible factors of its base in the same way, so that every spelling of one base comes to the same generators; `_tools/derivations/zipoy_voorhees.md` Step 2 is the worked example.
An exponential that stands to a fractional power, as the $e^{\pm 3\rho/2L}$ of the anti-de Sitter soliton's $\cosh(3\rho/2L)$, is written on the root every one of its powers is a whole power of, $e^{\rho/2L}$, by `_whole_exponents`, and that root is the generator; an expression with whole powers alone is handed back as it came, and `_tools/derivations/ads_soliton.md` Step 3 is the worked example.
Every radicand in the collection is positive factor by factor on the region its entry describes, and that reading is what lets the interior Schwarzschild radicals cancel.
An entry whose radicals change sign inside the region it claims would need that looked at again.

Both passes exit clean on the collection as it stands, and neither leaves anything `UNCHECKED`: since 22 September 2026 every published expression of every system is compared.
The last to be reached were the seventy Tolman-Bondi expressions that name a third derivative, which the reader now declares, and the inverse metric of Ellis-Bronnikov, which the entry now publishes.
`UNCHECKED` does not set the exit code, so a new one shows only in the output, and a clean run is one that prints none.
Every system publishes its inverse metric, because the page prints it beside the metric, so a system without one is reported `UNCHECKED` as missing it rather than excused.
Both passes print what failed on stderr and print the single line saying nothing failed on stdout, so a run piped through `2>&1 | tail` can look clean when it is not.
Read the exit code, and run the script before and after a change so that a failure you did not cause is not mistaken for one you did.

`derivations/audit-2026-09-18.md` groups and counts the disagreements the collection carried when the dimensional pass was added, and says which of them are the checker's fault rather than the physics'.
It is a dated snapshot of 433: the 320 that were Weyl components copied from the entry's own Riemann components have since been corrected, the 50 nested radicals cancel since the change to `norm`, and the rest were corrected entry by entry, leaving none.
`derivations/weyl.md` is the working behind those corrections, and is the thing to read before touching any `weyl_tensor`: a Weyl tensor equals Riemann only in a vacuum, it can be nonzero in a slot where Riemann vanishes, and two of the entries that publish one are conformally flat and so publish nothing.

Nothing is ever passed in silence. A value that cannot be parsed, a system with no time coordinate declaration, or a tensor sympy cannot finish in the budget is reported as `UNCHECKED` with the reason, separately from the disagreements.

## Printing a chart by machine

`tov`, `malament_hogarth`, `mixmaster`, `lentz`, `einstein_static`, `schwarzschild_de_sitter`, `schwarzschild_ads`, `milne`, `aichelburg_sexl`, `domain_wall` and `kantowski_sachs`, the cylindrical chart of `godel`, both charts of `c_metric`, of `melvin`, of `thin_shell_wormhole`, of `levi_civita`, of `curzon_chazy`, of `zipoy_voorhees` and of `mcvittie`, the three charts of `nariai`, the four charts of `global_monopole`, the four charts of `tangherlini`, the three charts of `majumdar_papapetrou`, and the two charts of `robinson_trautman`, the four charts of `string_black_hole`, and the five charts of `dilaton_black_hole`, the four charts of `gott_time_machine`, the two charts of `szekeres`, the five charts of `spinning_string`, the three charts of `gowdy`, the two charts of `photon_rocket`, the four charts of `light_beam`, the three charts of `wormhole_time_machine`, the three charts of `ori_time_machine`, and the four charts of `reissner_nordstrom_de_sitter`, the three charts of `string_wave`, the three charts of `bardeen`,, the four charts of `hayward` and the three charts of `regular_black_hole_pure_gravity`, the two charts of `eye_of_the_storm`, the four charts of `fisher_jnw`, and the five charts of `kerr_de_sitter`, the three charts of `hartle_thorne`, the seven charts of `witten_black_hole`, and the two charts of `som_raychaudhuri`, and the five charts of `coleman_de_luccia`, and the chart of `senovilla`, the five charts of `roberts`, the three charts of `gravastar`, and the five charts of `einstein_rosen_bridge`, and the three charts of `reissner_nordstrom_ads`, the three charts of `bonnor_vaidya`, the six charts of `kiselev`, the three charts of `mass_inflation`, the four charts of `boulware_deser`, and the six charts of `kaluza_klein_black_hole`, the three charts of `israel_wilson_perjes`, the three charts of `sultana_dyer`, the five charts of `ads_soliton`, the three charts of `eguchi_hanson`, and the four charts of `bartnik_mckinnon`, the five charts of `misner_brill_lindquist`, the three charts of `israel_shell`, the two charts of `belinski_zakharov`, the five charts of `charged_shell`, the three charts of `brill_waves`, the twelve charts of `ab_metrics`, the eight charts of `bonnor_charged_dust`, the three charts of `bach_weyl_ring`, the five charts of `quantum_btz`, the five charts of `topological_star`, the five charts of `jackiw_teitelboim_black_hole`, the three charts of `eternal_traversable_wormhole`, the five charts of `freund_rubin`, the four charts of `vuorio_warped_ads`, the five charts of `universe_from_nothing`, the three charts of `interstellar_wormhole`, the three charts of `supertranslation_hair`, the three charts of `big_rip`, the two charts of `kerr_scalar_hair`, the three charts of `lqc_bounce`, the three charts of `maximally_supersymmetric_plane_wave`, the two charts of `kodama_kink`, the two charts of `swirling_universe_black_hole`, and the two charts of `brane_wall_universe` have their mathematics written by `_tools/derivations/print_charts.py`, which defines each of those charts, computes every tensor with the checker's own `Geometry`, and prints each value through `chart_printer.py` beside it:

    .venv.noindex/bin/python _tools/derivations/print_charts.py --metric tov

`--system <id>` after it prints one chart of that spacetime alone, so that each chart can run in a process of its own, as the two charts of `belinski_zakharov` are run.

A chart with several free functions of two coordinates can print every tensor in a minute and still never finish its Kretschmann scalar, since `write()` factors the whole scalar at once.
The free chart of `kerr_scalar_hair`, four functions of $r$ and $\theta$, has a scalar of about 95,000 characters on which sympy's `factor` ran past half an hour; its spec passes `kretschmann_text`, the scalar written over the Riemann tensor in an orthonormal frame, $K = 4\sum_P R_{PP}^2 + 8\sum_{P<Q} s_Ps_QR_{PQ}^2$ over the six pairs of frame indices, 8,600 characters printed in seconds and checked against the computed scalar.
Run a new chart with `python -u` so that the timing of each block reaches the log as it finishes and a slow step can be named.

Every printed value is read back through the checker's `Reader` and compared with the value it came from before anything is written, and `verify_metrics.py` then checks the file like any other.
The script writes only the coordinate system's mathematics, the line element and the domains; the prose, the convention and the description of each parameter stay in the metric file and are carried over, and a parameter with no description stops the script.
A chart can pass a function that regroups the numerator of each value, which is how TOV's curvature is printed around $(\partial_r\Phi)^2 + \partial_r^2\Phi$ and Bianchi IX's around $a_1^2\cos^2\psi + a_2^2\sin^2\psi$, and a Ricci or Kretschmann scalar can be given in a structured form, which is checked against sympy before it is used.
A chart can instead pass a whole `pretty`, as Gödel's cylindrical chart passes `chart_printer.hyperbolic`, which rewrites the exponentials the checker's `Geometry` hands back in $\sinh r$ and $\cosh r$.
Levi-Civita's charts pass `radial_powers`, which hands the printer each power of the radius whose exponent holds a parameter, as $\rho^{4\sigma}$, as a placeholder with its text, since the printer writes rational exponents only.
Zipoy and Voorhees's charts pass `named_powers`, which writes the part of every exponent that holds a parameter on the two ratios the chart names, as $f^{2q}h^{q(2+q)}$ with $f = 1 - 2m/r$ and $h = 1 + m^2\sin^2\theta/(r^2 - 2mr)$, and leaves the whole part in the rational function, factored.
A chart whose values hold only on a surface of its parameters passes `after`, as Levi-Civita's Kasner form does: its Ricci tensor is checked to vanish on the checker's own parametrisation of Kasner's circle and written as vanishing.
Szekeres's stereographic chart passes `reduce` as well as `pretty`: its $E$ is held as a function while the tensors are built, `reduce` writes every value in $\partial_pE$, $\partial_qE$ and their derivatives along $r$, among which no relation is left, so that a value which vanishes for Szekeres's $E$ is exactly zero, and `pretty` writes each factor back in $E$ and its derivatives along $r$.
Its axisymmetric chart passes `szekeres_polar`, which writes every even power of $\sin\theta$ in $\cos\theta$, cancels the fraction and keeps the shortest factoring of each side, since the term in $dr\,d\theta$ leaves $\sin^2\theta - 1$ standing in a value factored as it comes.
Teo's two charts pass a `pretty` that factors each value with $\sin^2\theta$ written as $1 - \cos^2\theta$, so that $r + 16a^2b_0\cos^2\theta$ comes out as a factor, and then writes every other sum in whichever of the two leaves it fewer terms; `teo_wormhole.md` beside the script records why the example is published in place of the canonical metric.
The isotropic and moving string charts of `string_wave` pass a `pretty` that writes every power of $x^2 + y^2$ whose exponent holds $b$ as a power of the cone's conformal factor, $(\rho/\ell)^{2b-2}$, multiplies the factors that hold it out into one bracket, and writes a value that holds the string's acceleration as two parts, the cone's and the wave's; the moving string chart's $\rho$ holds $A$ and $B$, so it passes `reduce` as Szekeres's chart does.
`Chart.block` groups a tensor's components by value, and asks the exact comparison only of two values that agree or are opposite at one random point, `Chart.fingerprint`; comparing every pair exactly took over twelve minutes for one mixed Riemann tensor of Gowdy's, and the three charts now print in under five.
The photon rocket's Robinson-Trautman chart passes `RocketForms.reduce`: its $p$ is held as a function, every second derivative of it over the sphere is written by $\partial_\theta^2 q = A - q$ and its two companions, and the constant $A$ by the unit four-velocity, which leaves $p$, $\partial_\theta p$, $\partial_\phi p$ and their derivatives along $u$ with no relation among them.
Bardeen's charts pass `bardeen_pretty`, which factors each value in $r$, $Q = \sqrt{r^2 + g^2}$ and $r_s$, where no relation is left, and writes every odd power of $Q$ back as that power of $r^2 + g^2$, so that no radical is rationalised away; `bardeen_source` checks each chart against Ayón-Beato and García's nonlinear electrodynamics before it is written.
Hartle and Thorne's chart passes `hartle_thorne_orders`, which writes each value order by order, Schwarzschild's part, the part linear in $a$, the part in $a^2$ that Kerr has, and the part in $q - a^2$ with the logarithm written $L$, and the Painlevé-Gullstrand chart writes each value as a rational function plus one times $\sqrt{2m/r}$; `hartle_thorne_check` holds the first to being Kerr's metric to second order at $q = a^2$ and the other two to being its first order pulled back.
The four charts of `kastor_traschen` are written by the script too, with `KastorTraschenForms`, which writes the time out of every value by the sum its chart names, $U = H\tau + V$ or $\Omega = 1 + V/a$, so that no time stands in any printed value; the Cartesian chart is checked against the Einstein-Maxwell equations with $\Lambda = 3H^2/c^2$ and to be Majumdar and Papapetrou's at $H = 0$, every other chart to be the Cartesian one pulled back, and the isotropic chart to be the lukewarm hole's cosmological chart at $r_s = 2m$; `kastor_traschen.md` records each chart's source.
The eight charts of `bonnor_charged_dust` are written by it too: the harmonic chart with $U$ free, Bonnor's sphere of 1965, Bonnor and Wickramasuriya's sphere of 1975, the field outside either in the isotropic and in the areal radius, their spheroid inside and out, and Lemos and Weinberg's cloud. A chart that names its potential holds $U$ as a function with the slope `RATES` declares, so every value is rational in $U$, and `bonnor_charged_dust_check` holds each chart to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with dust whose charge density equals its mass density, to the density its source states, and to $U$ and its slope continuous across each surface; `bonnor_charged_dust.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `universe_from_nothing` are written by it too: Vilenkin's closed slicing, his 4-sphere, which is Riemannian, the scale factor chart, one line element Riemannian below $a = \ell$ and Lorentzian above it, the conformal chart and Feldbrugge, Lehners and Turok's quadratic gauge; `universe_from_nothing_check` holds each to the sphere or the hyperboloid of radius $\ell$ pulled back, the scale factor chart on both sides of its join, to $R_{\mu\nu} = (3/\ell^2)g_{\mu\nu}$, no Weyl tensor and the signature its convention states, and the halves to meeting at rest on a 3-sphere of radius $\ell$; `universe_from_nothing.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `draining_bathtub` are written by it too: Visser's laboratory chart, the Kerr-like chart of Basak and Majumdar as Berti, Cardoso and Lemos correct it, checked to be the first pulled back, and the vortex filament; `c` is the speed of sound there, and `_tools/derivations/draining_bathtub.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `unruh_acoustic_hole` are written by it too: the laboratory chart of Visser's canonical acoustic black hole and Unruh's time, checked to be the first pulled back; `c` is the speed of sound there too, and `_tools/derivations/unruh_acoustic_hole.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `string_bh_three_four_charges` are written by it too: the charts that name $f$ and the harmonic functions $H_i$ hold them as functions with declared slopes, so each value is a rational function of $r$, $f$ and the $H_i$, and `string_bh_charges_check` holds each chart to Einstein's equations with its gauge fields and scalars and to the published charts it reduces to; `_tools/derivations/string_bh_three_four_charges.md` records each chart's source.
A reprint reproduces every published file byte for byte, as running the script with no `--metric` on 1 October 2026 showed for all of them, in thirteen minutes.
sympy factors a polynomial in several variables by Wang's algorithm, which lifts the factors at random points drawn from a generator sympy seeds from the operating system, so on 2 October 2026 one tensor of Brill's charged Taub-NUT took 2 seconds in one run and over 800 in another, whatever `PYTHONHASHSEED` was.
`verify_metrics.py` draws those points from `WANG_SEED`, so each polynomial is lifted at the same points in every run and a chart prints in the same time each time.
`_tools/test_print_charts.py` holds `tov` to that and one chart to printing the same twice, and reprints Brill's universe under several hash seeds and states of sympy's generator within a time bound; it needs sympy, which the suite's environment has.
A symbol a chart names in `overrides` and in `factors` is written in a product where a symbol would be, and one it leaves out of `factors` closes the product as a bracketed sum does, which is why TOV passes `factors` without its collected bracket.
A chart the script writes replaces the chart of its id, or joins the spacetime's other charts after them, so Gödel's Cartesian chart stays as it was written.
A parameter keeps the description it has in that chart, or in another chart of the same spacetime.
`tov.md`, `malament_hogarth.md`, `mixmaster.md`, `lentz.md`, `godel.md`, `schwarzschild_de_sitter.md`, `majumdar_papapetrou.md`, `levi_civita.md`, `curzon_chazy.md`, `robinson_trautman.md`, `mcvittie.md`, `dilaton_black_hole.md`, `tangherlini.md`, `gott_time_machine.md`, `zipoy_voorhees.md`, `szekeres.md`, `light_beam.md`, `spinning_string.md`, `gowdy.md`, `photon_rocket.md`, `wormhole_time_machine.md`, `ori_time_machine.md`, `reissner_nordstrom_de_sitter.md` `string_wave.md`, `bardeen.md` and `hayward.md`,, `quantum_oppenheimer_snyder.md`, `hartle_thorne.md`, `coleman_de_luccia.md`, `einstein_rosen_bridge.md` and `kiselev.md` in the same folder record why each chart is the one published; `godel.md` also records the transformation from the Cartesian chart, whose pullback sympy checks symbolically.
The three charts of `van_den_broeck` are written by the script too: the Cartesian chart is checked to be Alcubierre's published metric at $B = 1$, the comoving spherical chart to be the Cartesian one pulled back at $f = 1$ and to carry Van Den Broeck's energy density and largest curvature component, and Krasnikov's proper distance chart to pull back onto the comoving one and to carry his Einstein tensor; `van_den_broeck.md` records why each is published.
The four charts of `tippett_tsang` are written by the script as well: the Cartesian chart, Tippett and Tsang's equation (1) with $h$ free, is held to Minkowski's metric at $h = 0$, to the determinant $-(1 - 4h(1 - h)c^2t^2/(x^2 + c^2t^2))$, which vanishes with $g_{tt}$, $g_{xx}$ and $g_{tx}$ where $x = 0$ and $h = 1/2$, and, with their own bubble, to an Einstein tensor whose only components on the plane $y = z = 0$ are $G_{yy}$ and $G_{zz}$, to no energy density on the moment $t = 0$, to a null energy condition that fails in the wall and to a Kretschmann scalar that diverges at the singular events; the polar chart to being it pulled back and to their equation (6) for the null directions; the interior chart to being it at $h = 1$; and Rindler's chart to being the interior pulled back and to the acceleration $1/\xi$ on its closed timelike curves. The Cartesian chart holds its time explicitly beside a free function of it, so `Chart.named_time` writes the time as $ct$ and leaves $h$ and its derivatives as they are printed; `tippett_tsang.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `kaluza_klein_monopole` are written by the script as well, each after the first checked to be Gross and Perry's chart pulled back, and `kaluza_klein_monopole.md` records why each is published and why the Cartesian chart is not.
The six charts of `kaluza_klein_black_hole` are written by it as well: `kaluza_klein_check` holds the electric, magnetic and dyonic charts to the general dyon of Gibbons and Wiltshire, Rasheed and Larsen at their own $p$ and $q$, which is not published and is held to being a vacuum at random points in forty digits, the electric chart to the published static chart of `black_string` boosted, the magnetic chart to the published chart of `kaluza_klein_monopole` at $r_s = 0$, the dyonic chart to carrying the published metric of `rn_metric`, the ingoing charts to being the static ones pulled back, and the Einstein chart to being the electric chart reduced along $y$ and to the field equations of four dimensions; the magnetic and dyonic charts pass `szekeres_polar`, which writes the sines their potential $1 - \cos\theta$ mixes in in the cosine, and `kaluza_klein_black_hole.md` records each chart's source and why the general dyon is not published.
The six charts of `bell_szekeres` are written by the same script, each pulled back onto the double null chart and checked against the Einstein-Maxwell equations before it is written, and `bell_szekeres.md` records each chart's source and map.
The five charts of `myers_perry` are written by it as well: the ingoing chart is checked to be the Boyer-Lindquist one pulled back, the two-spin chart to be it at $b = 0$, the equal-spin chart to be the two-spin one at $b = a$ pulled back, the six-dimensional chart to be Tangherlini's at $a = 0$ and flat at $\mu = 0$, and each to be Ricci flat; `myers_perry.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `black_saturn` are written by it as well. Weyl's chart and the polar chart leave the four functions of a lapse and shift form free, as Gowdy's charts leave theirs, and define Elvang and Figueras's solitons, polynomials and functions as names in units of $L$; `black_saturn_check` holds the vacuum equations the parameters state to making every Ricci component vanish, the functions read from those names to making it vanish at three points in forty digits and to being flat space's at infinity, and the polar chart to being Weyl's pulled back. The polar chart's Kretschmann scalar is printed by `black_saturn_polar_scalar` as one expanded sum, since sympy does not factor it. The ring chart is the ring alone, exact, with `black_saturn_ring_pretty` writing each value around the constant $C$ the chart defines, held to a vanishing Ricci tensor and to being Weyl's chart at $\kappa_1 = 1$ and $\beta = 0$ pulled back at three points in twenty digits; `black_saturn.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `damour_solodukhin` are written by it too, each after the first pulled back onto Damour and Solodukhin's own, with the throat's $G^r{}_r$, $G^\theta{}_\theta$ and Kretschmann scalar checked in each, and `damour_solodukhin.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `einstein_dirac_maxwell_wormhole` are written by it too, the two through the throat pulled back onto the areal chart, each held to Einstein's equations with Blázquez-Salcedo, Knoll and Radu's electric field and a traceless Dirac stress with no energy density, and `einstein_dirac_maxwell_wormhole.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `interstellar_wormhole` are written by it too, each held to the curvature $-r''/r$ and $(1 - r'^2)/r^2$ of an ultrastatic spherical metric and the flare to meeting the cylinder at the mouth with $r = \rho$ and $r' = 0$, its values printed in $x$, $\arctan(x)$, $r$, $1 + x^2$ and $\pi^2 - 4\arctan(x)^2$, and `interstellar_wormhole.md` records each chart's source.
The six charts of `kiselev` are written by it as well: the static chart and the two Eddington-Finkelstein charts keep the state parameter $w$, name Kiselev's term $h = (r_q/r)^{3w + 1}$, and are printed as polynomials in it, since every derivative of $h$ is $h$ times a rational function; the chart of the linear term is his example $w = -2/3$, and the hyperbolic and conformally flat charts are that example with no black hole, the first printed by `kiselev_hyperbolic` as a power of $e^\chi$ times a power of $\sinh\chi$. `kiselev_check` holds each to Kiselev's stress tensor, $G^t{}_t = G^r{}_r = 3wh/r^2$ and $G^\theta{}_\theta = -3w(3w + 1)h/2r^2$, and so to the two pressures whose average is $w\rho$, the static chart to the published metrics of `schwarzschild` at $w = 0$, of `schwarzschild_de_sitter` at $w = -1$ and of `rn_metric` with $r_q^2 \to -r_q^2$ at $w = 1/3$, and each other chart to being the one before it pulled back; `kiselev.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `einstein_rosen_bridge` are written by it as well: the three of the neutral bridge are held to being a vacuum and to being the published metric of `schwarzschild` carried along $r = r_s + u^2$, $r$ and $(4r + r_s)^2/16r$, the charged chart in $r$ to being the published metric of `rn_metric` with $r_q^2 \to -r_q^2$ and to carrying the Maxwell stress tensor with its sign reversed, and the charged bridge in $u$ to being that chart at $r_s = 0$ pulled back; `einstein_rosen_bridge.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `near_horizon_extreme_kerr` are written by it as well, each after the first checked to be Bardeen and Horowitz's Poincaré chart pulled back, every one checked to be a vacuum and to have the rays of no angular momentum for its principal null rays, with a `pretty` that writes every value in $1 + \cos^2\theta$ and multiplies a factor by its mirror image in $\cos\theta$, and a `collect` that groups the near-NHEK chart's sums by powers of $r - k$ and $k$ and the global chart's by powers of $y$; `near_horizon_extreme_kerr.md` records each chart's source and map.
Senovilla's chart is written by it too, with `senovilla_pretty`, which finds the power of $\cosh^{1/3}(3a\rho)$ a value carries and writes the rest in the hyperbolic functions of $act$ and $3a\rho$, and the printer's `arguments`, which sets those two arguments tight, keeps a fractional power on the name, $\cosh^{2/3}(3a\rho)$, and writes $\sinh^k/\cosh^k$ as $\tanh^k$; `senovilla_fluid` holds the chart to the radiation fluid, the expansion, the acceleration and the circular light ray of Chinea, Fernández-Jambrina and Senovilla, its geodesic equations are their (7) and (8), and `senovilla.md` records the chart's source.
The cosmological chart of `reissner_nordstrom_de_sitter` passes `rnds_cosmological_pretty`, which writes twice the areal radius, $2H\tau\rho + r_s$, as one named factor and any other sum as a polynomial in it where that is shorter, and the chart is checked to be the static chart at $r_q = r_s/2$ pulled back before it is written.
The four charts of `simpson_visser` are written by it as well, each after the first pulled back onto Simpson and Visser's own, the areal chart on both sides of $r = 0$, with Simpson and Visser's Einstein tensor, Ricci scalar and Kretschmann scalar checked in each; the charts that keep $r$ name $\rho = \sqrt{r^2 + a^2}$ and pass `simpson_visser_radius`, which factors each value with $a^2$ written as $\rho^2 - r^2$ and then writes every factor in whichever of $r$ and $a$, $r$ and $\rho$, or $\rho$ and $a$ leaves it fewest terms, and `simpson_visser.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `fisher_jnw` are written by it as well, each held to $R_{\mu\nu} = 2\,\partial_\mu\varphi\,\partial_\nu\varphi$, to the wave equation and to vacuum without the field, and each after the first pulled back onto the first; three are printed around the powers of the ratio they name by `named_powers` and the harmonic chart in $\sinh(ku)$, $\cosh(ku)$ and $e^{mu}$ by `fjnw_hyperbolic`, and `fisher_jnw.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `roberts` are written by it as well, each held at six random points, in forty digits, to $R_{\mu\nu} = 2\,\partial_\mu\varphi\,\partial_\nu\varphi$, to the wave equation, to flat space at $p = 0$, and to being the double null chart pulled back.
The double null chart writes the factors of the areal radius as $(1 \pm p)v - u$, the areal chart passes `roberts_lambda`, which writes every value in Roberts's $\lambda$, and the scaling chart passes `roberts_scaling`, which pairs those factors as $4e^{2x}(\cosh^2x - p^2\sinh^2x)$; `roberts.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `bonnor_vaidya` are written by it as well: each Eddington-Finkelstein chart is held to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with a null current, to being the published metric of `rn_metric` pulled back where $m$ and $q$ are constant, and to being the published chart of `vaidya` at $q = 0$, and the homothetic chart to being the ingoing one pulled back at $m = \mu v$ and $q = Qm/M$; `bonnor_vaidya.md` records each chart's source.
A declared function whose name carries a subscript does not answer to `\partial`, which is why the mass and the charge are $m$ and $q$ and not $r_s$ and $r_q$.
The five charts of `kerr_de_sitter` declare $\Xi$, $\rho$, $\Delta_r$ and $\Delta_\theta$ as names and pass `KerrDeSitterForms`, which writes every value in them, and an `after` that writes each Riemann component as its Weyl component plus $(\Lambda/3)(g g - g g)$ over one denominator; each chart is held to $R_{\mu\nu} = \Lambda g_{\mu\nu}$ and each after the first to being the first pulled back, and `kerr_de_sitter.md` records the source of each chart.
The five charts of `kerr_taub_nut` declare $\Sigma$, $\Delta$ and $\chi$, or $Q$ and $P$ in Plebański and Demiański's chart, and pass `KerrTaubNutForms`, which writes every value in those names and in $l + a\cos\theta$, groups a sum by the powers of $\chi$ where that is shortest, and writes two derivatives of $g_{\phi\phi}$ by hand, each read back and compared like any other value; each chart is held to being a vacuum and each after the first to being the first pulled back, and `kerr_taub_nut.md` records the source of each chart.
The five charts of `brill_charged_taub_nut` declare $\Sigma$ and $\Delta$, or $\Sigma$ and $U$ in the chart of Brill's universe, and pass `BrillForms`, which writes every value in those names, takes the form of a sum with the fewest terms among those with $2mr$ or $r_q^2$ written out by $\Delta$ and $r^2$ by $\Sigma$, and writes $A\cos^2\theta + B$ as $(A + B)\cos^2\theta + B\sin^2\theta$ where that is shorter; `brill_charged_taub_nut_check` holds the first chart to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with the potential $r_q r\,(c\,dt + 2l\cos\theta\,d\phi)/\Sigma$, to the published metric of `taub_nut` at $r_q = 0$, of `rn_metric` at $l = 0$ and of `israel_wilson_perjes` at $r_q^2 = m^2 + l^2$, and each other chart to $R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu} = 4r_q^4/\Sigma^4$ and to being the first pulled back, and `brill_charged_taub_nut.md` records the source of each chart.
The four charts of `black_string` are written by the script too: the static charts are checked to be the published metrics of `schwarzschild` and of `tangherlini` in five dimensions with $dz^2$ added, and the ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein and Kerr-Schild charts to be the static chart pulled back; `black_string.md` records each chart's source.
The six charts of `topological_black_hole` are written by it as well: the static chart and both Eddington-Finkelstein charts with the curvature $k$ of the horizon a parameter, Lemos's black string, the brane in $z = L^2/r$, whose values keep $z^3 - z_h^3$ whole, and the hyperbolic chart, printed by `chart_printer.hyperbolic`; each is held to $R_{\mu\nu} = -(3/L^2)g_{\mu\nu}$ and each after the first to being the first pulled back, and `topological_black_hole.md` records the source of each chart.
The five charts of `ads_soliton` are written by it as well: Horowitz and Myers's chart in four, five and three dimensions and the Poincaré chart, whose values keep $r^n - r_0^n$ and $z^3 - z_0^3$ whole, and the polar chart, printed by `rooted_hyperbolic`, Senovilla's printer for any cube root of a hyperbolic cosine; `ads_soliton_check` holds each to $R_{\mu\nu} = -((d - 1)/L^2)g_{\mu\nu}$, the chart of four dimensions to the published brane of `topological_black_hole` with its time and one flat direction exchanged, the chart of three to constant curvature and to the published `btz` continued, and the others to Horowitz and Myers's pulled back; `ads_soliton.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `topological_star` are written by it as well: Bah and Heidmann's chart of the star, the chart about the bubble in their $\rho$, which names $r$ and passes `bubble_radius`, the black string's ingoing chart, the extremal string's isotropic chart, and the Einstein metric of four dimensions; `topological_star_check` holds each chart of five dimensions to Einstein's equations with Maxwell's field and each after the first to Bah and Heidmann's pulled back, theirs to the published `black_string` at $r_B = 0$, to the published `schwarzschild` with its time made the circle at $r_S = 0$ and to the exchange of $t$ with $y$, and the Einstein metric to the reduction and to the field equations of four dimensions; `topological_star.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `supertranslation_hair` are written by it as well: Compère and Long's static chart with the field $C(\theta)$ and the radius $B$ of a circle about the axis held, and their two charts of Bondi's form with the shear $\sigma(\theta)$ and $W = \sqrt{r^2 + \sigma^2}$ held; `supertranslation_hair_check` holds the static chart to their Cartesian coordinates, each Bondi chart to the static chart pulled back, and the retarded chart to Bondi's published metric. Its rates sit beside the declared function $C$ or $\sigma$, whose derivatives `Reader.by_rates` leaves as they stand.
The four charts of `randall_sundrum` are written by it too; the three that cross the wall pass `chart_printer.kink` wrapped so that each value is printed as anti-de Sitter's part plus the wall's delta, the delta's coefficient taken on the wall, and `randall_sundrum_check` holds each chart to $R_{\mu\nu} = -4k^2g_{\mu\nu}$ on each side and each after the first to being the first pulled back; `randall_sundrum.md` records the source of each chart.
The seven charts of `witten_black_hole` are written by it as well, each held with its own dilaton to $R_{\mu\nu} + 2\nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu\Phi = 0$ and to the dilaton's equation, to a vanishing Einstein tensor and to $K = R^2$, and each after the first to being Witten's chart carried along its map; Witten's chart is printed in $\sinh(\lambda r)$ and $\cosh(\lambda r)$ by `chart_printer.hyperbolic` and the charts written in one exponential by `witten_exponential`, which keeps $1 - m\,e^{-2\lambda x}$ and $e^{2\lambda\sigma} + m$ the factors their line elements have, and `witten_black_hole.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `jackiw_teitelboim_black_hole` are written by it as well, the static, proper distance, Kruskal, global and Poincaré charts: `jackiw_teitelboim_check` holds each to $R = -2/L^2$, to a vanishing Einstein tensor and to $K = R^2$, the dilaton its convention states to the dilaton's equation and to the one mass $\phi^2 - L^2(\nabla\phi)^2 = \phi_r^2r_h^2/L^4$, and each after the first to being the static chart carried along its map through the hyperboloid, metric and dilaton both; `_tools/test_jackiw_teitelboim_black_hole.py` holds the published files and drawings to what their texts state, and `jackiw_teitelboim_black_hole.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `eternal_traversable_wormhole` are written by it as well, the global, Poincaré and thermal charts of Maldacena and Qi's (2.1) and (2.2) times $L^2$: `eternal_traversable_wormhole_check` holds each to $R = -2/L^2$, to a vanishing Einstein tensor and to $K = R^2$, the global dilaton $\phi_h(1 + (\pi/2 - \sigma)\cot\sigma)$ of their (C.181) to the source $T_{\mu\nu} = -\phi_h\delta_{\mu\nu}$ exactly, every chart's source to a vanishing trace, and the Poincaré and thermal charts to being the global chart carried along their maps, metric, dilaton and stress tensor; `_tools/test_eternal_traversable_wormhole.py` holds the published files and drawings to what their texts state, and `eternal_traversable_wormhole.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `einstein_cluster` are written by it too: the areal chart holds the mass function as a name for $r^2\partial_r\Phi/(1 + 2r\partial_r\Phi)$ and passes a `reduce` that writes every value in $m$ and $\partial_r m$, the isotropic chart passes `einstein_powers`, which writes every value in whole powers of Einstein's $a$ and $b$, and the uniform chart `einstein_radicals`; `einstein_cluster_check` holds each to $G^r{}_r = 0$, to $G^t{}_t = -2m'/r^2$ and to a stress across the radius of half the energy density times $V^2$, and the isotropic and hyperspherical charts to being the constant speed and uniform charts pulled back, and `einstein_cluster.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `som_raychaudhuri` are written by it too, with a `pretty` that leaves $c^2 - \Omega^2r^2$ whole: the cylindrical chart is checked against its source, charged dust at rest in a uniform magnetic field with no Lorentz force on it, and the Cartesian chart to be the cylindrical one pulled back; `som_raychaudhuri.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `vuorio_warped_ads` are written by it as well: Rebouças and Tiomno's cylindrical chart, Bengtsson and Sandin's Poincaré disc, the fibred chart of Anninos, Li, Padi, Song and Strominger, and Rooman and Spindel's horospherical chart, each $-(c\,dt + A)^2 + h$ with $h$ a hyperbolic plane of curvature $-m^2$ and $dA = 2\Omega$ times its area. `vuorio_check` holds each to topologically massive gravity, $G_{\mu\nu} + \Lambda g_{\mu\nu} + C_{\mu\nu}/\mu = 0$ with $\mu = 3\Omega$ and $\Lambda = (\Omega^2 - m^2)/3$, its Cotton tensor built in the chart's own orientation, each after the first to the cylindrical chart's plane pulled back with a closed difference of twisting forms, and the cylindrical chart at $m = \Omega$ to Vuorio's (2.21) as Chow, Pope and Sezgin quote it; `vuorio_warped_ads.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `lqc_bounce` are written by it as well: the cosmic and comoving spherical charts with Cai and Wilson-Ewing's scale factor $a = (1 + t^2/t_b^2)^{1/6}$ and Ashtekar and Singh's harmonic chart with $a = \cosh^{1/3}(\tau/t_b)$, each line element naming $a$ and each chart's own line element writing it out in $x^0$, since a name defined in the time is read in the time the file prints. `lqc_scale_factor` writes every value as a power of $a$ times a function with no root, keeping $t_b^2 + t^2$ whole, and `lqc_bounce_check` holds each chart to the modified Friedmann equation of loop quantum cosmology and the harmonic chart to $\Box\tau = 0$ and to the cosmic chart pulled back; `lqc_bounce.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `bonnor_rotating_dust` are written by it too, with `one_exponential`, which gathers every exponential of a value into one power of the conformal factor, written as a rational multiple of the exponent the line element spells: the cylindrical chart, where $r = \sqrt{\rho^2 + z^2}$ is a name, is checked against van Stockum's three equations and its source, dust at rest in the chart with the Ricci scalar for its density, geodesic, rigid and of vorticity $\sqrt{2\pi G}$ times the root of the density, and the spherical chart to be the cylindrical one pulled back; `bonnor_rotating_dust.md` records each chart's source. An exponent over a sum times a number, as $a^4\rho^4/8r^8$ is once $r$ is written out, is read by `_exponent_terms` in `verify_metrics.py` with the number taken out of the sum, so that $e^{2\mu}$ and $e^{\mu}$ are powers of one generator; `_tools/test_roots.py` holds it.
The seven charts of `point_particle_2plus1` are written by the script too: the wedge, circumference radius, isotropic and moving charts are checked to be the conical chart pulled back, the two bodies' chart to be flat, to be the isotropic chart about either particle when the other has no mass and to carry the sum of the masses far away, and Gott and Alpert's planet to be dust at rest that meets the cone; the isotropic charts pass `conformal_cones`, which writes every value as a rational function times whole powers of $(\rho/\ell)^{2\alpha - 2}$, and `point_particle_2plus1.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `coleman_de_luccia` are written by it as well: the wall chart with $\rho(\xi)$ free is held to being a vacuum of each sign for Coleman and De Luccia's three $\rho$ and to giving their bubble radii through the junction condition, the open chart to being the wall chart continued through the light cone, the conformal time chart to being the open chart pulled back, and each static chart to pulling back onto the wall chart of each vacuum with the wall on $r = r_w(t)$; `coleman_de_luccia.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `gravastar` are written by it as well: the interior charts declare Mazur and Mottola's constant $C = (1 - r_s/R)/(1 - R^2/L^2)$ as a name and are printed by a `pretty` that writes $r_s$ back in $C$, after which the shell's radius cancels, the tortoise chart through `chart_printer.hyperbolic`; `gravastar_check` holds the interior charts to $R_{\mu\nu} = (3/L^2)g_{\mu\nu}$ and a vanishing Weyl tensor, the tortoise chart to being the interior pulled back, the exterior to Schwarzschild's published metric, and $g_{tt}$ to one value on the shell from both sides. The shell is in no chart, since $g_{rr}$ jumps across it, and `_tools/test_gravastar.py` holds its surface density and tension to the published metrics by the junction conditions; `gravastar.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `hiscock` are written by it as well, the two Vaidya charts printed with the rate of change of the mass standing apart: `hiscock_check` holds each to its one Einstein component, $G_{vv} = 2\,\partial_vm/r^2$ or $G_{uu} = -2\,\partial_um/r^2$, to Schwarzschild's published Eddington-Finkelstein chart where the mass is constant and to flat space without it, and the flat double null chart to being the ingoing chart without mass pulled back. The surface between the two Vaidya charts is in no chart, and `_tools/test_hiscock.py` holds its vanishing surface density and its tension to the published metrics by the junction conditions; `hiscock.md` records each chart's source.
The eight charts of `siklos` are written by it as well: Siklos's chart and the chart on the disc with their profiles free, the second printed in its functions $p$ and $q$ by `siklos_disc_pretty`, and Kaigorodov's spacetime in six; each is held to $R_{\mu\nu} = -(3/L^2)g_{\mu\nu}$, the free charts where their profile solves its equation, each after the first to being Siklos's pulled back, and $\partial_v$ to being a repeated principal null direction of the Weyl tensor, and `siklos.md` records the source of each chart.
The six charts of `schrodinger_spacetime` are written by it as well: the Poincaré chart with the deformation length $\beta$, the inverse radius, Blau, Hartong and Rollier's global chart, whose values are written over one denominator by powers of $\beta$, the chart that keeps the dynamical exponent $z$, which names $h = (\beta/r)^{2z - 2}$ and is printed as a polynomial in it as Kiselev's is, and the Poincaré chart in five and six dimensions; `schrodinger_check` holds each to Einstein's equations with $\Lambda = -(d + 1)(d + 2)/2L^2$ and Son's massive vector field, $m^2 = z(z + d)/L^2$, the Poincaré chart to Siklos's published chart at $H = -\beta^2/x^2$ and to anti-de Sitter space's at $\beta = 0$, each other chart to being it pulled back, and $\partial_\xi$ to being a null Killing vector and a repeated principal null direction, and `schrodinger_spacetime.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `kundt_waves` are written by it as well: Kundt's canonical form and Podolský and Beláň's chart with the profile $G$ free, the simplest wave $G = (x^2 - y^2)/\ell$ in their chart and on the flat space it crosses, and the family of Ozsváth, Robinson and Rózga with a cosmological constant, printed in $p$, $q$ and $\kappa$ by `kundt_family_pretty`; `kundt_check` holds each to its field equation, the rays to being null geodesics and a repeated principal null direction, each chart without a cosmological constant to the one before it pulled back, and the family to the published charts of `pp_wave` and `siklos` and to Podolský and Beláň's chart at their values of $\Lambda$, $\alpha$ and $\beta$. The Kerr-Schild chart holds Podolský and Beláň's $x$, $u$ and $v$ as functions, `HELD`, and `KundtFlatForms` writes every derivative of them by the three rational one-forms of `kundt_waves.md` Step 3; `kundt_waves.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `reissner_nordstrom_ads` are written by it as well, each held to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with $\Lambda = -3/L^2$ for the Coulomb field, the static chart to being Schwarzschild-anti-de Sitter's at $r_q = 0$ and each Eddington-Finkelstein chart to being the static one pulled back; `reissner_nordstrom_ads.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `tolman_vii` are written by the script too, with $\psi$, half a logarithm of radicals that stands only inside a cosine or a sine, held as a function of $r$; `TolmanForms` writes its derivatives in, which are algebraic, and every value in $\tan\psi$ or $\cot\psi$, $Z$, $\sqrt{Z}$ and $\sqrt{3\beta}$, and `tolman_vii_check` holds Lattimer and Prakash's chart to their density and pressure, to Schwarzschild's exterior at the surface and to being Tolman's chart at his constants, and Tolman's chart to his density and pressure; `tolman_vii.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `misner_zapolsky` are written by it as well: the sphere of radiation, Tolman's solutions V and VI at $n = 1/2$ in the radius $r_b$ of the star, and his solution V with $n$ free, printed through `named_powers`; the Tolman V star names $Z = g^{rr}$, which holds $r^{7/3}$ and is held as a function of $r$ with the declared slope $(7Z - 4)/(3r)$, and `misner_zapolsky_check` holds each chart to Tolman's density and pressure, the sphere of radiation to Oppenheimer and Volkoff's exact solution and to its homothety, and each star to Schwarzschild's published exterior at its surface; `misner_zapolsky.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `mass_inflation` are written by it as well: the ingoing charged Vaidya chart, printed with $\partial_v m$ standing apart, the double null chart and Brady and Smith's chart of advanced time and radius, each with its two functions free; `mass_inflation_check` holds the first to the Maxwell field of the charge plus one stream of null dust and to Reissner-Nordström's published metric at constant mass, the second to Poisson and Israel's equations for the mass function and its wave equation, and the third to Brady and Smith's two radial equations and to being the first at $g = -1$, $h = -f$. `_tools/test_mass_inflation.py` holds the published Einstein tensors to the same equations and Ori's shell to its laws, and `mass_inflation.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `boulware_deser` are written by it as well: each names $W = \sqrt{r^4 + 4\ell^2r_0^2}$ and is printed in $r$, $r_0$ and $W$ by `boulware_deser_pretty`, and `boulware_deser_check` holds each to the field equations of Einstein-Gauss-Bonnet gravity at three random points to forty digits, the static chart to Tangherlini's published metric as $\ell \to 0$, and each Eddington-Finkelstein chart to being the static one pulled back; `boulware_deser.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `israel_wilson_perjes` are written by it as well: the cylindrical chart, with $W = |U|$ and $\omega$ free, is held to the Einstein-Maxwell equations wherever $U$ is harmonic and $\omega$ solves its equation, the spheroidal chart, printed by `IwpForms` in its two names $\Sigma_0$ and $\Sigma$, to being the cylindrical one pulled back at $U = 1 + m/(r + ia\cos\theta)$ and the published metric of `kerr_newman` at $GM/c^2 = r_Q = m$ in the radius $r + m$, and the spherical chart to being it pulled back at $U = 1 + (m + il)/(r - m)$ and one hole of `majumdar_papapetrou` at $l = 0$; `israel_wilson_perjes.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `sultana_dyer` are written by it as well: the Kerr-Schild chart in the conformal time, the chart of Schwarzschild's time, which names $\eta = ct + r_s\ln(r/r_s - 1)$ and is printed in it by `SultanaDyerForms`, so that no logarithm and no $t$ stands in a value, and the ingoing Eddington-Finkelstein chart; `sultana_dyer_check` holds the first to $\eta^4/\eta_0^4$ times the published metric of `schwarzschild`, to Saida, Harada and Maeda's dust and null dust, to the published conformal chart of `frw` at $r_s = 0$ and to its two trapping horizons, and each other chart to being the first pulled back; `sultana_dyer.md` records each chart's source.
The six charts of `lifshitz_spacetime` are written by it as well, each naming the one ratio whose powers hold $z$, as $(r/L)^{2z}$ and $(L/zw)^{2/z}$, and printed by `named_powers`: `lifshitz_check` holds Kachru, Liu and Mulligan's chart to Einstein's equations with $\Lambda = -(z^2 + z + 4)/2L^2$ and Taylor's massive vector field, $A = \sqrt{2(z - 1)/z}\,(r/L)^z c\,dt$ with $m^2 = 2z/L^2$, and to that field's own equation, each other chart to being the first pulled back, and the inverse radius chart at $z = 1$ to the published Poincaré chart of `anti_de_sitter`; `_tools/test_lifshitz.py` holds the published files to the same physics, and `lifshitz_spacetime.md` records each chart's source.
The chart of `double_kerr` is written by it too, with $f$, $\omega$ and $\gamma$ free functions of $\rho$ and $z$ and no component assuming a field equation: `double_kerr_check` holds the three field equations its parameters state to making every Ricci component vanish, and Kramer and Neugebauer's Ernst potential and $e^{2\gamma}$, as those parameters write them, to solving the equations at random rod ends and phases in forty digits; `double_kerr.md` records why the functions are left free and which pair the drawings draw, and `_tools/test_double_kerr.py` holds that pair to Herdeiro and Rebelo's masses, spins, angular velocity, surface gravity and force.
The four charts of `penrose_impulsive_wave` are written by it as well, for the warp of a cosmic string, $h = Z^\beta$: the null chart, Podolský and Griffiths's continuous metric with $\epsilon = 0$ on $Z = \rho e^{i\phi}$, the retarded chart, the same with $\epsilon = 1$ at $V = \sqrt2\,r$, $U = -u/\sqrt2$ and $\rho = \cot(\theta/2)$, and the flat chart on either side of the front. Each continuous chart defines the step $\Theta$ as a name, $\tfrac{1}{2}(1 \pm \mathrm{sgn})$, so the checker reads it as it reads a kink, and passes `chart_printer.step`, which writes every value once for both sides of the front; `piw_check` holds each to a vanishing Ricci tensor, to a curvature that is a delta on the front alone, and to the flat metric on either side pulled back along Podolský and Griffiths's transformations, and the flat charts to the published charts of `minkowski` and `cosmic_string`; `penrose_impulsive_wave.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `neugebauer_meinel` are written by it as well: Weyl's chart as Neugebauer and Meinel write it, the same form in the frame that turns with the disc, Bardeen and Wagoner's lapse and dragging form in Weyl's coordinates and in the oblate spheroidal ones, each with its three functions free, and the limit $\mu \to \mu_0$ outside the disc, the extreme Kerr metric, exact. `neugebauer_meinel_check` holds the vacuum equations each free chart states to making every Ricci component vanish, the charts to one another, the limit to the published chart of `kerr` at $a = GM/c^2$, and the theta functions of `nm_disc.py` to Ernst's equation; the spheroidal chart passes `collect`, which groups each value by its monomial in the derivatives so that the polynomial in $\xi$ and $\eta$ in front is factored once, and `neugebauer_meinel.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `eguchi_hanson` are written by it as well, with no coordinate scaled by $c$, since the space is Riemannian and has no time: Eguchi and Hanson's chart is printed by `eguchi_hanson_pretty`, which keeps $r^4 - a^4$ whole, the Kähler chart names $W = \sqrt{\rho^4 + a^4}$ and is checked to be the first carried along $\rho^4 = r^4 - a^4$, and the two-centre chart names the distances $R_1$ and $R_2$ from its centres and is printed by `eguchi_hanson_distances`, which writes $z$ and every even power of $\rho$ in them so that no relation is left, and is checked at six random points in forty digits to be the first carried along Prasad's map; each is Ricci flat with the Kretschmann scalar $384a^8/r^{12}$ in its own letters, `_tools/test_eguchi_hanson.py` holds the curvature to being self dual and to the Euler number, and `eguchi_hanson.md` records each chart's source.
The eight charts of `gravitational_instantons` are written by it as well, each a Riemannian space of four dimensions with no coordinate scaled by $c$, and each a space of its own: the Euclidean Schwarzschild solution in the imaginary time and in a chart regular on the bolt, which names the areal radius $r$; Hawking's self-dual Taub-NUT solution and Page's with a bolt; Gibbons and Hawking's multi-centre metrics with their centres on one axis and $V$ and $\omega$ free; the Fubini-Study metric on $\mathbb{CP}^2$ in Gibbons and Pope's radius and in the distance from the nut; and Page's metric, which names $P$, $Q$ and $N$. `instanton_pretty` prints every value factored, each sum in whichever of sines and cosines leaves it shortest, with the named polynomials written as their letters. Each `check` holds its chart to its source: the first to the published metric of `schwarzschild` continued to $t = -i\tau/c$, the regular chart to the first carried along $x = 2r_s\sqrt{1 - r_s/r}$, the Taub-NUT charts to the published metric of `taub_nut` continued with $l = in$ at $m = n$ and $m = 5n/4$, the multi-centre chart to a vanishing Ricci tensor on every solution of $\nabla\times\boldsymbol\omega = \nabla V$, to the self-dual Taub-NUT chart at one centre and to the published two-centre chart of `eguchi_hanson`, and the last three to $R_{\mu\nu} = \Lambda g_{\mu\nu}$. Page's chart is written so that it is Einstein for every $\nu$, with $16\nu^2/N^2$ where Gibbons and Hawking write $1/(3 + \nu^2)^2$, the two equal exactly on Page's quartic, since a chart that is Einstein at one algebraic number only cannot be compared as rational functions; `_tools/test_gravitational_instantons.py` holds the regularity of the bolts to the quartic, the curvature to being self dual or not, and each Euler number to its nuts and bolts, and `gravitational_instantons.md` records each chart's source and why the multi-centre chart keeps its centres on one axis.
The five charts of `misner_brill_lindquist` are written by it as well, a Riemannian space of three dimensions with no coordinate scaled by $c$: Brill and Lindquist's Cartesian chart and the cylindrical chart with $\psi$ free, Misner's bispherical chart with $\Psi$ free, one hole in isotropic coordinates, and Brill and Lindquist's charged chart with $\chi$ and $\psi$ free. `misner_brill_lindquist_check` holds the Ricci scalar of each free chart to its constraint, $-8\nabla^2\psi/\psi^5$ or Misner's equation, the two-hole $\psi$, each term of Misner's sum and Brill and Lindquist's $\Psi$ to solving it, each chart after the first to being the first pulled back, the one hole to the space part of the published isotropic chart of `einstein_rosen_bridge`, and the charged chart to $R = 2E_iE^i$ for harmonic potentials, to the Cartesian chart at $\chi = \psi$ and to the space part of the published Cartesian chart of `majumdar_papapetrou` at $\psi = 1$; `misner_brill_lindquist.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `brill_waves` are written by it as well, one more Riemannian space of three dimensions: Brill's cylindrical chart and Eppley's spherical chart with $\psi$ and $q$ free, and Brill's chart with both functions depending on $\phi$ too, as Alcubierre and others' waves without axial symmetry do. `brill_waves_check` holds the Ricci scalar of the first two to Brill's equation, $-\psi^{-5}e^{-2q}(8\nabla^2\psi + 2\psi(\partial_\rho^2 q + \partial_z^2 q))$, the base metric to its scalar curvature, Holz, Miller, Wakano and Wheeler's $q$ to the potential `brill_wave.py` solves with, the spherical chart to being the cylindrical one pulled back, and the third chart to the first where nothing depends on $\phi$; `brill_waves.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `boson_star` are written by it as well, each with its two metric functions free, since no closed form is known for either: `boson_star_check` holds the polar areal chart to the Einstein-Klein-Gordon equations, the published $G^\theta{}_\theta$ equal to the field's stress across the radius wherever $G^t{}_t$, $G^r{}_r$ and the wave equation hold, and to the published metric of `schwarzschild` with no field, and the isotropic chart to being the first pulled back along $r = \psi^2R$; each Kretschmann scalar is written over the four independent components of the Riemann tensor in a static observer's frame, and `boson_star.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `kodama_kink` are written by it as well, each with its two metric functions free, since the kink is known only numerically: `kodama_kink_check` holds Kodama's areal chart and the proper radial chart of Kodama, de Oliveira and Santos to the Einstein equations of the ghost field, the published $G^\theta{}_\theta$ equal to its stress across the radius wherever $G^t{}_t$, the radial $G$ and the wave equation hold, the areal chart in a vacuum to the published metric of `schwarzschild`, and the proper chart to being the areal one pulled back along $r = r(R)$; each Kretschmann scalar is written over the frame components, and `kodama_kink.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `brane_wall_universe` are written by it as well: Kraus's static chart of one side of the wall, and the Gaussian normal chart of Binétruy, Deffayet, Ellwanger and Langlois in Mukohyama, Shiromizu and Maeda's closed form, whose $Q$, $A$ and $N$ are names the chart defines, held as functions of $t$ and $w$ because they hold the free scale factor $a(t)$. `brane_wall_universe_check` holds every component of that chart's Riemann tensor, its names written out, to the bulk's own form, the Weyl tensor of the black hole beside the curvature of anti-de Sitter space, and the chart's geometry then takes that tensor, so the Riemann, Ricci and Einstein tensors are printed in $A$ and $N$ as the bulk has them and the Weyl tensor and the Kretschmann scalar as the names have them; `_tools/derivations/brane_wall_universe.md` is the derivation, and `_tools/test_brane_wall_universe.py` reads the junction conditions off the published chart and holds them to the Friedmann equation of the history.
The four charts of `bartnik_mckinnon` are written by it as well, each with its functions free, since no soliton is known in closed form: the areal chart with $\sigma$, $m$ and the amplitude $w$ of the Yang-Mills field, its Kretschmann scalar written in the frame of a static observer as TOV's is, the isotropic chart with $f$ and $h$, the tortoise chart with $F$ and $r(\xi)$, and Breitenlohner, Forgács and Maison's with $A$, $N$ and $r(\tau)$; `bartnik_mckinnon_check` holds each, along its own radial coordinate, to the Einstein-Yang-Mills equations, $G^\mu{}_\nu = 2\ell^2T^\mu{}_\nu$ with a traceless stress, and each after the first to the areal chart's radius, mass function and lapse; `bartnik_mckinnon.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `morgan_morgan` are written by it too: Weyl's chart with $\psi$ and $\gamma$ free functions of $\rho$ and $z$ and no component assuming a field equation, and the oblate spheroidal chart of the first disc, whose $\psi$ and $\gamma$ are names the chart defines and `HELD` holds, each value a polynomial in the derivatives of $\psi$ after `morgan_morgan_reduce` has written $\gamma$'s derivatives by its quadrature and $\partial_\xi^2\psi$ by Laplace's equation, so that the Ricci tensor is exactly zero; `morgan_morgan_check` holds Weyl's chart to its stated field equations and the first disc to Laplace's equation, the quadrature, its surface density and being Weyl's chart pulled back, `_tools/test_morgan_morgan.py` holds the disc to the junction conditions of two streams of dust, and `morgan_morgan.md` records each chart's source. `null_rays.load` and `metric_tags.py` pass `HELD` to their readers, so a published value that writes a held name's derivative is read by every generator.
The two charts of `erez_rosen` are written by it as well, each with $\psi$ and $\gamma$ names the chart defines and `HELD` holds, as the first Morgan-Morgan disc's are: `erez_rosen_reduce` writes $\gamma$'s derivatives by Weyl's quadrature and the second derivative of $\psi$ along the radial coordinate by Laplace's equation, so the Ricci tensor is exactly zero, and each value is a polynomial in the derivatives of $\psi$. The spherical chart's values are factored in $r/m$ and $\cos\theta$, with each derivative of $\psi$ taken an odd number of times along $\theta$ counted as odd in $\sin\theta$, and written back in $r$, $m$ and $\sin\theta$. `erez_rosen_check` holds each chart to Weyl's equations, to Schwarzschild's functions at $q = 0$, the prolate spheroidal chart to the mass $m$ and the quadrupole moment $\tfrac{2}{15}qm^3$, and the spherical chart to being it pulled back and to the published metric of `schwarzschild` at $q = 0$; `RATES` declares the derivatives of $\psi$ and $\gamma$ to the checker, `_tools/test_erez_rosen.py` holds the published files to the same physics, and `erez_rosen.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `distorted_schwarzschild` are written by it as well: the prolate spheroidal and spherical charts with the distortion $U$ and $V$ names the chart defines and `HELD` holds, and Weyl's chart with his own $\psi$ and $\gamma$. `distorted_schwarzschild_reduce` writes $\partial_x^2U$ by Laplace's equation and $\partial_x^2V$ by the one field equation of second order and leaves the first derivatives of $V$ standing, so that no value divides by $x^2 - y^2$ as Weyl's quadrature does, and writes as zero a value that vanishes on the quadrature; the Weyl tensor is written as the Riemann tensor is, each component checked against it there, and the Kretschmann scalar as $16(a^2 + ab + b^2 + c^2)$ in three mixed components. `distorted_schwarzschild_check` holds each chart to Weyl's equations, to Doroshkevich, Zel'dovich, and Novikov's printed functions, to $V = 2U - 2q$ on the horizon, to being the chart before it pulled back and, at $q = 0$, to the published metric of `schwarzschild`; Weyl's chart declares its first derivatives in `RATES`, and `distorted_schwarzschild.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `quantum_oppenheimer_snyder` are written by it as well: Lewandowski, Ma, Yang and Zhang's static exterior, Kelly, Santacruz and Wilson-Ewing's Painlevé-Gullstrand exterior, printed by `quantum_os_fall` as a rational function plus one times the speed $\sqrt{2m/r - \alpha m^2/r^4}$ of the slices, the two Eddington-Finkelstein exteriors, and the flat Friedmann ball of dust with its scale factor free. `quantum_os_check` holds each exterior chart to the stress $G^t{}_t = -3\alpha m^2/r^6$ and $G^\theta{}_\theta = 6\alpha m^2/r^6$, the static chart to Schwarzschild's published metric at $\alpha = 0$, to $f(r_b) = 1$ and to the least mass, each other exterior chart to being the static one pulled back, and the ball to the Friedmann equation of loop quantum cosmology with $a^3 = 1 + 9c^2\tau^2/\alpha$ and to a surface that is the exterior's geodesic falling from rest far away. `quantum_os.py` beside it holds that surface in every exterior chart for the three generators, `_tools/test_quantum_oppenheimer_snyder.py` holds both to the published files, and `quantum_oppenheimer_snyder.md` records each chart's source and the junction.
The chart of `bonnor_magnetic_dipole` is written by it too, in Bonnor's four polynomials $P$, $Q$, $Y$ and $Z$, each written as its letter by `named_factors` and each numerator collected by its powers of $\cos\theta$: `bonnor_magnetic_dipole_check` holds it to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with the dipole's potential $A_\phi = 2mbr\sin^2\theta/P$ and to the published spherical chart of `zipoy_voorhees` at $b = 0$ and $q = 1$; `bonnor_magnetic_dipole.md` records the chart's source and why Weyl's chart and the prolate spheroidal chart are not published, and `_tools/test_bonnor_magnetic_dipole.py` holds the published components to the mass $2m$ and to Emparan's excess of angle on the axis between the holes.
The six charts of `lewis` are written by it as well: Lewis's chart and the canonical chart of the Weyl class, the Lewis class, and van Stockum's three exteriors, each naming the powers of $r/\ell$ or $r/R$ its exponents hold, and the phase $\psi = m\ln(r/\ell)$ where Lewis's exponent is imaginary, and printed by `LewisForms` as a rational function of those names. `lewis_check` holds each to being a vacuum with $fl + k^2 = r^2$, Lewis's chart at $b = q = 0$ and the canonical chart at $j = 0$ to the published metric of `levi_civita`, the canonical chart and the Lewis class to being Lewis's chart carried along its map or continued to $n = im$, and van Stockum's exteriors to being members of the family and to carrying the published metric of `stockum_dust` and its first derivative at $r = R$; `_tools/test_lewis.py` holds the published files to the radii the captions name, and `lewis.md` records each chart's source and why the light cylinder's radical is put on its branch before it is evaluated.
The four charts of `wahlquist` are written by it as well: Wahlquist's own, Mars's canonical chart and his ingoing one, with $h_1$ and $h_2$ or $U$ and $V$ held as functions, and Whittaker's static limit with $F$ held. `WahlquistForms` writes the connection with the first derivatives of those functions standing, the Weyl tensor as the two scalars $W_1$ and $W_2$ the charts name times the products of the frame, and the Riemann tensor as that plus the part its Ricci tensor fixes; `wahlquist_check` holds every chart to a perfect fluid with $\rho + 3p$ constant, Wahlquist's chart to being Mars's pulled back at three points in forty digits, the ingoing chart to being the canonical one pulled back, and Whittaker's to the limit of no rotation; `wahlquist.md` records each chart's source.
