The five charts of `hotta_tanaka` are written by it too: Hotta and Tanaka's conformally flat chart, Podolský and Griffiths's global chart, the Kruskal chart, the null cylindrical chart whose limit $a \to \infty$ is Aichelburg and Sexl's, and the Kundt chart. `hotta_tanaka_check` holds each to the hyperboloid, to Hotta and Tanaka's five-dimensional form pulled back, to $R_{\mu\nu} = (3/a^2)g_{\mu\nu}$ with the shock, and to the Weyl tensor Gauss's equation makes of the five-dimensional wave, at three random points in forty digits; `hotta_tanaka_pretty` factors the de Sitter part and the coefficient of each delta on their own and writes the profile's logarithm as the line element has it, and `hotta_tanaka.md` records each chart's source and why the Cartesian null chart is not printed. Four of the charts are in the checker's `IMPULSES`, which "Adding a spacetime, as far as the checker is concerned" below describes.
The eight charts of `plebanski_hacyan` are written by it too: the flat plane times a sphere in its inertial and Rindler charts, anti-de Sitter space of two dimensions times a flat plane in Plebański and Hacyan's chart, the null chart and the static chart, anti-Nariai in Dias and Lemos's two charts, and the exceptional chart with $f$ and $g$ free. `plebanski_hacyan_check` holds each to $G^\mu{}_\nu = -K_2$ on the Lorentzian surface and $-K_1$ on the other, the Einstein-Maxwell equations with $\Lambda = (K_1 + K_2)/2$ and a uniform field, and each chart with a map to another to being it pulled back; `_tools/test_plebanski_hacyan.py` holds the published files to the same curvatures and to the two black hole limits, and `plebanski_hacyan.md` records each chart's source. `REGIONS` in `metric_tags.py` counts the three products as three regions, so the spacetime carries only the tags all three have.
The five charts of `three_brane_throat` are written by it too: Duff and Lu's isotropic chart, Horowitz and Strominger's chart in the areal radius, Gibbons, Horowitz, and Townsend's chart in $w$, which holds the areal radius as a function with a declared slope and is written in $w$ and $r$ alone, Maldacena's throat, and Gibbons and Townsend's throat in the proper distance. `three_brane_check` holds each to $R_{MN} = F_{MPQRS}F_N{}^{PQRS}/96$ for a 5-form equal to its dual, to a vanishing Ricci scalar, the second and third to being the first pulled back, the first to Gibbons and Townsend's inversion, and the throat to being the limit of the first toward its horizon with no Weyl tensor; `three_brane_throat.md` records each chart's source, and `_tools/test_three_brane_throat.py` holds the published files to it.
The five charts of `freund_rubin` are written by it too: anti-de Sitter space of four dimensions times a 7-sphere of radius $2L$ in the global, conformal and Poincaré charts of Aharony, Gubser, Maldacena, Ooguri, and Oz, the static chart, and Gibbons and Townsend's chart of the proper distance. `freund_rubin_check` holds each to Freund and Rubin's field equations for a four-form on the anti-de Sitter factor, to the Riemann tensors of the two factors, and the static, conformal and proper distance charts to being the global or Poincaré chart pulled back; `freund_rubin.md` records each chart's source, and `_tools/test_freund_rubin.py` holds the published files to it.
The three charts of `maximally_supersymmetric_plane_wave` are written by it too: Blau, Figueroa-O'Farrill, Hull and Papadopoulos's Brinkmann chart, their Rosen chart of the Penrose limit, and Berenstein and Nastase's conformally flat chart, the last two with their time named, since their components hold it. `msw_check` holds the Brinkmann chart to type IIB supergravity with Metsaev's five-form, $R_{MN} = F_{MPQRS}F_N{}^{PQRS}/24$, to that form being equal to its dual up to sign, and to a vanishing Weyl tensor, and the other two to being the Brinkmann chart pulled back. Berenstein and Nastase's chart of the Einstein static universe printed to 17 MB, its conformal factor's $\cos(\mu ct - \beta)$ expanded through every component, so it is drawn as the conformal diagram instead, and `_tools/test_maximally_supersymmetric_plane_wave.py` holds their map with the factor $1/|e^{i\psi} - \cos\alpha\,e^{i\beta}|^2$, not the quarter of it their last line prints; `maximally_supersymmetric_plane_wave.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `bubbling_ads` are written by it too: Lin, Lunin, and Maldacena's metric on the polar coordinates of the plane of the droplets with $G$ and $V$ free, the black disc in the global chart of anti-de Sitter space times a 5-sphere, and the black half plane, the plane wave of Blau, Figueroa-O'Farrill, Hull, and Papadopoulos. `bubbling_ads_check` holds the global chart to the Riemann tensors of its two factors, the plane wave to $R_{tt} = 8$ alone, the disc in the polar chart and the half plane in their Cartesian metric to being those charts pulled back, and the disc, a black ring and the half plane to $R_{MN} = F_{MPQRS}F_N{}^{PQRS}/96$ with their 5-form, through `bubbling_ads.py`; the Cartesian chart with three free functions printed to 6.3 MB and is not published, and `bubbling_ads.md` records each chart's source and the two signs their text leaves to be fixed.
The chart of `cremmer_scherk` is written by it too: Minkowski space times a sphere of radius $a$ in six dimensions, Horváth, Palla, Cremmer, and Scherk's (2). `cremmer_scherk_check` holds it to the field equations of their paper, a monopole's field $F_{\theta\phi} = Ba^2\sin\theta$ with $8\pi GB^2 = 1/a^2$ and the cosmological constant $\Lambda = 1/2a^2$, to Maxwell's equations for that field, and to $G^\mu{}_\nu = -1/a^2$ on the four flat dimensions and nothing on the sphere; `cremmer_scherk.md` records the chart's source and how their (4) is read, and `_tools/test_cremmer_scherk.py` holds the published files to it.
The three charts of `israel_shell` are written by it as well: `israel_shell_check` holds the interior to a vanishing Riemann tensor and to the published spherical chart of `minkowski`, each exterior chart to a vacuum and to the published chart of `schwarzschild` it names, and the ingoing chart to being the exterior pulled back. The shell is in no chart, and `_tools/test_israel_shell.py` holds its motion to the published metrics by the junction conditions; `israel_shell.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `chandrasekhar_xanthopoulos` are written by it as well: the chart of $\eta$ and $\mu$ and the chart of $\psi$ and $\lambda$ take the angle $\alpha$ for their one free parameter and define $p = \cos\alpha$, $q = \sin\alpha$, $\rho$, $X$ and $Y$ as names, so that the constraint $p^2 + q^2 = 1$ needs no table, and `CxForms` prints every value in those names, shaping each once and keeping it, since a vacuum's Weyl tensor is its Riemann tensor; the Boyer-Lindquist and ingoing Kerr charts name $\Sigma$ and $\Delta$. `cx_check` holds each to a vanishing Ricci tensor, to Kerr's Kretschmann scalar, and each after the first to being the first carried along its map; `chandrasekhar_xanthopoulos.md` records each chart's source and why no double null chart is written. `--system <chart>` prints one chart of the spacetimes named, so that the charts of one spacetime can be printed in processes of their own, and `write` rewrites a spacetime's file under a lock for that reason; the two charts of the waves take three and a half minutes each.
The four charts of `ppn_metric` are written by it as well, each kept to post-Newtonian orders and printed order by order by `ppn_orders`: `ppn_metric_check` holds each to a Ricci tensor that vanishes at $\beta = \gamma = 1$ and nowhere else, the isotropic chart to Schwarzschild's metric in isotropic coordinates at those values, the Cartesian chart to being the isotropic one pulled back, the areal chart to being it carried along $r \to r - \gamma m$, and the rotating chart to the isotropic one at $a = 0$ and to the published Lense-Thirring chart of `hartle_thorne`; `_tools/test_ppn_metric.py` holds the published files to the bending of light, the advance of the perihelion and Shapiro's logarithm, and `ppn_metric.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `eih_many_bodies` are written by it as well, in the potentials $U$, $\psi$, $\chi$ and $V_1$, $V_2$, $V_3$ left free, each value printed with its terms in post-Newtonian order, lowest first, by the printer's `rank`: `eih_check` holds every Ricci component kept to vanishing wherever the potentials solve the field equations their parameters state, `eih_between_the_bodies`, and the standard gauge to being the harmonic chart at the time $x^0 + \partial_t\chi/2$; the inverse is written whole, its components between two directions of space among them, by `eih_whole_inverse`, and `_tools/derivations/eih_many_bodies.md` is the derivation.
The three charts of `bardeen_perturbations` are written by it as well, the longitudinal gauge in flat and in curved space and the synchronous gauge, each kept to first order in its potentials and printed by `bardeen_perturbations_grouped` as a sum over the products of the scale factor and its derivatives, Friedmann's term first: `bardeen_perturbations_check` holds each longitudinal chart to Mukhanov, Feldman and Brandenberger's Einstein tensor and the synchronous chart to the flat longitudinal one pulled back along their gauge transformation, the curved chart holds its conformal factor $\Omega$ as a name and writes every part of a value that depends on $x$, $y$ and $z$ through $kr^2$ alone by $4(\Omega - 1)$, and `_tools/derivations/bardeen_perturbations.md` is the derivation.
The four charts of `semiclosed_world` are written by it too: the dust in its proper time, the line element of the published interior of `oppenheimer_snyder`; the dust in its conformal time with the cycloid $a = \tfrac{1}{2}a_m(1 + \cos\eta)$ written out, held to being dust, $G^\eta{}_\eta = -3a_m/a^3$ and nothing else, and conformally flat; and Schwarzschild's and the isotropic chart of the exterior, each held to being the published metric of `schwarzschild` carried along its map. `semiclosed_world_matching` holds the junction for any $\chi_0$: the surface is a radial geodesic of the exterior with energy $\cos\chi_0$, and the areal radius changes along the unit normal at the rate $\cos\chi_0$ on both sides, negative past the equator. Its drawings are all at $\chi_0 = 3\pi/4$: the embedding is a movie of the dust's proper time until just before the throat closes at $c\tau = \pi r_s/2$, with the exterior read on both sheets from Tolman-Bondi's chart by `embedding.NovikovSheets`; the conformal diagram is `conformal.Bag`, the dust's rectangle with Kruskal's manifold fitted to its surface from behind the throat, one view for each chart; and the spacetime diagrams draw the dust from bang to crunch with `DustSolver`'s origin `rest`, which keeps the solution on both sides of a moment of time symmetry, and the isotropic plane with the time function $-t$ behind the throat, where a static time runs to the past. `_tools/test_semiclosed_world.py` holds the published files to the closed forms, and `semiclosed_world.md` records each chart's source.
The six charts of `white_hole` are written by it too: the dust in its proper time and in its conformal time, Schwarzschild's chart and the outgoing Eddington-Finkelstein chart outside it, Kruskal's null chart, whose areal radius is Lambert's function of $UV$, held as a function and printed by `white_hole_kruskal` in $r$ and $e^{-r/r_s}$ with no $U$ or $V$ below the line, and Novikov's comoving chart, printed by `white_hole_novikov` as a power of $R$ times a rational function of $F$, $b$ and $q = ct - b$. `white_hole_check` holds the conformal chart to dust and to the comoving chart along the cycloid, each exterior chart to the published metric of `schwarzschild` or to it pulled back, and Novikov's chart to Tolman's density, to Schwarzschild's vacuum where $F$ is constant and to the Einstein-de Sitter universe with no delay; `_tools/test_white_hole.py` holds the published files to the junction at the surface and the drawings to the numbers their captions state, and `white_hole.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `lindquist_wheeler_lattice` are written by it too: one cell of Lindquist and Wheeler's lattice in Schwarzschild's chart, in Clifton and Ferreira's cosmological time, whose metric is written as its line element has it, and in Lindquist and Wheeler's comoving chart with the areal radius $r(\tau, \rho)$ of the shells free, and the comparison hypersphere with its radius $a(\tau)$ free. `lindquist_wheeler_check` holds the first to the published metric of `schwarzschild`, the second to being it pulled back and to its comoving shells being unit normals of the slices, the third to being a vacuum with Schwarzschild's Kretschmann scalar wherever each shell obeys $(\partial_\tau r)^2 = r_s/r - r_s/\rho$, and the hypersphere to dust at rest where $\dot a^2 = r_s/(a\sin^3\psi) - 1$; `REGIONS` in `metric_tags.py` counts the two exact cell charts, `_tools/test_lindquist_wheeler_lattice.py` holds the published files and drawings to what their texts state, and `lindquist_wheeler_lattice.md` records each chart's source. Its drawings added three things to `null_rays.py`: a row's `cell`, the boundary of a cell with the cell inside it, `CellBoundary`; a row's `bang`, which looks for the curve where the metric stops being finite below the middle of the drawing as well as above; and the dust origin `rest`, which solves a scale factor on both sides of a moment of rest.
The four charts of `exponential_metric` are written by it as well: the isotropic chart of Papapetrou and Yilmaz's metric, its Cartesian form, the areal chart, whose isotropic radius $r = -m/\mathrm{W}(-m/R)$ is held as a function of $R$ and every value printed as a rational function of $r$, $R$ and $m$ by $e^{m/r} = R/r$, and Bronnikov's harmonic chart $u = 1/r$; `exponential_metric_check` holds each to the scalar field of negative energy, $R_{ab} = -2\,\partial_a\varphi\,\partial_b\varphi$ with $\varphi = m/r$, and each after the first to being the first pulled back, and `exponential_metric.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `kasner_scalar` are written by it as well: Belinskii and Khalatnikov's synchronous chart, the logarithmic time of Damour, Henneaux and Nicolai, and Kasner's vacuum of five dimensions that reduces to it. The synchronous chart and the chart of five dimensions pass `time_powers`, which writes each value as one power of the time with its whole exponent, $t^{2p_1 - 1}$, and the logarithmic chart `kasner_scalar_exponentials`, which gathers its exponentials into one; every value assumes the two conditions on the chart's exponents, so `kasner_scalar_check` hands the printer the Ricci tensor and the scalars as they stand on that surface through `OnSurface`, which builds the Einstein and Weyl tensors from them, after holding each to the tensor of free exponents on the checker's parametrisation, to the wave equation, to the stress of a stiff fluid, to the logarithmic chart being the synchronous one pulled back, and to the reduction of the vacuum of five dimensions landing on the surface of four; `kasner_scalar.md` records each chart's source.
The six charts of `moving_mirror` are written by it too: the inertial and null charts of the flat plane, with the mirror's world line $z(t)$ and its ray tracing function $p(u)$ declared and left out of the metric, the chart $U = p(u)$ that brings a mirror to rest, with the inverse $f$ free and printed with primes, that chart in $T$ and $X$ for Carlitz and Willey's mirror and for the mirror of Good, Anderson and Evans, each value written in $X - cT$, and Rindler's conformal chart; `moving_mirror_check` holds each flat, each after the first to being the inertial chart pulled back, and each mirror's flux, from its chart's conformal factor by Davies, Fulling and Unruh's formula, to its closed form, and `moving_mirror.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `btz_multi_holes_wormholes` are written by it as well: the sausage, stereographic and free fall charts of anti-de Sitter space of three dimensions and the chart outside one horizon; `btz_multi_check` holds each to $R_{\mu\nu} = -(2/\ell^2)g_{\mu\nu}$ and to being the surface $-U^2 - V^2 + X^2 + Y^2 = -\ell^2$ pulled back, the free fall chart passes `btz_multi_trig`, and `btz_multi_holes_wormholes.md` records each chart's source and the tent every drawing uses.
The three charts of `btz_shock_wave` are written by it as well: Shenker and Stanford's continuous Kruskal chart, with the step $\Theta = \tfrac{1}{2}(1 + \mathrm{sgn}(u))$ defined as a name and every value printed by `btz_shock_shifted` as its value ahead of the shock with $v + \alpha\Theta$ for $v$, which it checks behind the shock, plus its delta; their discontinuous chart, whose delta of $U$ is in `IMPULSES`; and the black hole's chart outside one horizon. `btz_shock_check` holds the Kruskal charts to the black hole's on either side of the shock and to $R_{uu} + (2/\ell^2)g_{uu} = 2\alpha\,\delta(u)$, their stress tensor of a shell of null particles, and the exterior chart to the Kruskal chart pulled back, and `btz_shock_wave.md` records each chart's source and the moment the embedding diagram draws.
The two charts of `kasner_magnetic` are written by it as well: Kastor and Traschen's chart in Kasner's time, which passes `magnetic_powers`, writing each value as one power of the time times sums in rising powers of $b^2t^{2p_3}$, and Rosen's chart of the axisymmetric universe, which passes `rosen_trigonometric`; `kasner_magnetic_check` holds both to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with $F_{xy}$ constant, the first on Kasner's circle through `OnSurface`, and the second to being the first pulled back, and `kasner_magnetic.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `belinski_zakharov` are written by it as well: the pole chart is Belinski and Zakharov's wave of two solitons on the isotropic Kasner universe, explicit, with $N$ a name the chart defines and every value printed by `factored_hyperbolic` as $1$ or $1/\sqrt{\sinh\tau\cosh\xi}$ times a rational function of the hyperbolic functions of $\tau$, $\xi$ and $\beta$, and the canonical chart is their block form with $f$, $P$ and $Q$ free and no component assuming a field equation. `belinski_zakharov_check` holds the first to a vanishing Ricci tensor, to their (5.10) as printed at three points in forty digits and to the diagonal metric at $\beta = 0$, and the second to the vacuum equations its parameters state, to the wave's own functions solving them, and to being the pole chart carried along $ct = w\sinh\tau\cosh\xi$, $z = w\cosh\tau\sinh\xi$; `belinski_zakharov.md` records each chart's source and why that wave is the one drawn. `factored_hyperbolic` factors each value in the exponentials the checker hands back, among which no relation holds, and writes each irreducible factor in the hyperbolic functions by Chebyshev's polynomials; `rooted_hyperbolic`, which reduces by $\sinh^2 = \cosh^2 - 1$ over every argument at once, did not finish a Christoffel symbol of the pole chart in two minutes, and the chart now prints in minutes.
Its canonical chart names its time and leaves functions of it free, so `Chart.named_time` writes $t$ as $ct$ only where it stands bare and leaves the arguments of a declared function and of its derivatives alone, which the reader reads the same way; and its Kretschmann scalar is printed by `belinski_zakharov_scalar` as one expanded sum over $4c^4t^4f^6$, since sympy's `factor` did not finish on it in twenty minutes. The pole chart printed in 532 seconds and the canonical chart in 423 on 2 October 2026, most of the second in its check.
The four charts of `nordstrom_scalar` are written by it as well: Einstein and Fokker's chart with the conformal factor $\Phi$ free, the point mass, the uniform field and the dust universe, the last printed around $L^2 - c^2t^2$; `nordstrom_scalar_check` holds the first to $R = -6\,\Box\Phi/\Phi^3$ and no Weyl tensor, each other to being it at its own $\Phi$, the point mass to Deruelle and Sasaki's $R_{\mu\nu}R^{\mu\nu}$ and to $\gamma = -1$, $\beta = 1/2$, and the dust to $R\Phi^3$ constant; `nordstrom_scalar.md` records each chart's source and `_tools/test_nordstrom_scalar.py` holds the published files to the perihelion, the fall and the drawings.
The five charts of `elliptic_de_sitter` are written by it as well: the global chart printed in $\cosh(ct/\ell)$ and $\tanh(ct/\ell)$ by `nariai_hyperbolic` and the printer's `arguments`, the conformal chart by `eds_conformal`, which writes every value in $\cos\eta$ and $\tan\eta$, the Kruskal chart with its factors $1 \pm UV$, the static chart and the planar chart. `elliptic_de_sitter_check` holds each to the hyperboloid pulled back, to $R_{\mu\nu} = (3/\ell^2)g_{\mu\nu}$ and to a vanishing Weyl tensor, the identification the global, conformal and Kruskal charts state to being $X \to -X$, `EDS_ANTIPODE`, and the static and planar charts to holding no antipodal pair; `_tools/test_elliptic_de_sitter.py` holds the published files to the same and the hyperboloid to what the texts say of it, and `elliptic_de_sitter.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `rp3_geon` are written by it as well: Kruskal's chart of $T$ and $X$, whose areal radius is Lambert's function of $X^2 - T^2$, held as a function and printed by `rp3_geon_kruskal` in $r$, $e^{-r/r_s}$, $T$ and $X$ with neither coordinate below the line, Schwarzschild's chart of the one exterior, and the isotropic chart. `rp3_geon_check` holds each to a vacuum and to the published chart of `schwarzschild` carried along its map, Louko and Marolf's (4.8) for Kruskal's chart, the fold `RP3_GEON_INVOLUTION`, $(T, X, \theta, \phi) \to (T, -X, \pi - \theta, \phi + \pi)$, to being an isometry of Kruskal's chart with no fixed point that keeps the directions of time and of space, and Misner and Wheeler's inversion $\rho \to r_s^2/16\rho$ to being an isometry of the isotropic chart and the fold on the moment $t = 0$; `_tools/test_rp3_geon.py` holds the published files to the same and the drawings to what their captions say, and `rp3_geon.md` records each chart's source.
The three charts of `born_infeld_charge` are written by it as well, each naming the radical $W = \sqrt{r^4 + r_0^4}$ and the mass function $m$, an incomplete elliptic integral of the first kind that the chart holds as a function with the slope $r_q^2/(r^2 + W)$, and printed in $r$, $W$, $m$ and $r_q$ by `born_infeld_pretty`; `born_infeld_check` holds each to Einstein's equations with the field of a point charge of Born and Infeld's Lagrangian, the static chart to the published metric of `schwarzschild` at $r_q = 0$ and of `rn_metric` as $r_0 \to 0$, to the mass $r_s/2 - 1.2360\,r_q^2/r_0$ at the centre and to the cone of Hoffmann's particle, and each Eddington-Finkelstein chart to being the static one pulled back; `_tools/test_born_infeld_charge.py` holds the published files to the same physics, and `born_infeld_charge.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `charged_shell` are written by it as well: `charged_shell_check` holds the interior to the published spherical chart of `minkowski`, the static chart to the published metric of `rn_metric`, every exterior chart to the Einstein tensor of a point charge's field and to being the static chart pulled back, and the isotropic chart, which takes Arnowitt, Deser and Misner's lengths $a = r_s/4 + r_q/2$ and $b = r_s/4 - r_q/2$ for its parameters, to the published single hole of `majumdar_papapetrou` at $b = 0$. The shell is in no chart, and `_tools/test_charged_shell.py` holds its motion to the published metrics by the junction conditions; `charged_shell.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `bondi_sachs` are written by it as well, each with $V$, $\beta$, $U$ and $\gamma$ free and no component assuming a field equation: Bondi, van der Burg and Metzner's chart and the chart of $\ell = 1/r$. `bondi_sachs_check` holds the first to the published charts of `schwarzschild`, `vaidya` and `photon_rocket` at their own functions, to the published axisymmetric chart of `robinson_trautman` carried along $r \to f\,r$, to flat space at $V = r$ and to $R_{rr} = 4\,\partial_r\beta/r - 2(\partial_r\gamma)^2$, and the second to being the first pulled back; the Kretschmann scalar is printed by `bondi_sachs_scalar` as one expanded sum, as Belinski and Zakharov's is. Its drawings take `bondi_burst` in `null_rays.py`, Bondi's expansion in $1/r$ for a weak quadrupole burst, as closed forms handed to each generator as `functions`; `_tools/test_bondi_sachs.py` holds the published Ricci tensor on it to 1.4% of the curvature at the world tube, falling as $1/r$, and the mass radiated to Bondi's formula, and `bondi_sachs.md` records each chart's source, the expansion, why Sachs's six functions are not printed and why the embedding diagram is one sphere.
The two charts of `petrov_homogeneous` are written by it too: Petrov's own chart, in the letters of Gibbons and Gielen with the length $\ell$ for his $1/k$, and the chart outside Bonnor's cylinder of dust, the heavy cylinder of `lewis` at $m = \sqrt{3}$, each printed in the cosine and the sine of the phase $\psi$ it names. `petrov_homogeneous_check` holds each to a vacuum whose Kretschmann and Chern-Pontryagin scalars vanish while the cubic scalar is $-48/\ell^6$, Petrov's chart to Gibbons and Gielen's four Killing vectors, their algebra and their left-invariant frame, and the cylinder's to the published heavy cylinder, to Petrov's chart carried along their map with the time reversed, and to the published dust of `stockum_dust` and its first derivative on $r = R$; `_tools/test_petrov_homogeneous.py` holds the published files and drawings to what their texts state, and `petrov_homogeneous.md` records each chart's source. Its figure, `projections.petrov_loop`, draws the cones turning along $r$ and a closed timelike curve round them, every step checked timelike and future directed against the published metric.
The three charts of `kopczynski_trautman` are written by it as well: the two comoving charts name the scale factor $a = (1 + c^2t^2/\ell^2)^{1/3}$ and are printed in $a$, $ct$ and $\ell$, each side of a value factored in $a^3$, and the conformal chart leaves $a(\eta)$ free; `kopczynski_trautman_check` holds each to a vanishing Weyl tensor and to the Einstein tensor of dust plus the repulsion of its spins, $G^t{}_t = -(4/3\ell^2)(a^{-3} - a^{-6})$ and $G^x{}_x = -(4/3\ell^2)a^{-6}$, the explicit scale factor to Trautman's modified Friedmann equation, and the spherical chart to being the Cartesian one pulled back. Its conformal chart is drawn with $a(\eta)$ solved by `null_rays.DustSolver` from the published $G^r{}_r$ set to a declared source, the row's `sources`, where the matter is not dust; `_tools/test_kopczynski_trautman.py` holds the published files to the same physics and to the numbers the history states, and `kopczynski_trautman.md` records each chart's source.
The twelve charts of `ab_metrics` are written by it as well: Ehlers and Kundt's A II in its static region, beyond its horizon, in Kruskal's chart, whose radius is held as the white hole's is, and in the inertial coordinates inside the cone; B I in its static chart, on the de Sitter slicing, through the neck and in the inertial coordinates outside the cone; A III; B II in its static chart and through its axis; and B III. The inertial charts name $\sigma = \sqrt{T^2 - X^2 - Y^2}$ and $r = \sqrt{X^2 + Y^2 - T^2}$ and are printed by `ab_inertial` in the name, $X$ and $Y$ with at most one $T$ in front. `ab_metrics_check` holds every chart to a vacuum with the Kretschmann scalar $12b^2/r^6$ in its own radius and each chart of A II, B I and B II after the first to being the first carried along its map; `_tools/test_ab_metrics.py` holds the published files to Schwarzschild's metric with the time and the angle exchanged, to the topological black holes without the cosmological constant, to Kasner's universe and to Levi-Civita's cylinder, and `ab_metrics.md` records each chart's source.
The five charts of `datt_ruban_t_models` are written by it as well: the comoving chart with $a(t, r)$ and $b(t)$ free; Ruban's chart in the cycloid's parameter, whose $a$ is a name the chart defines and `DattRubanCycloid` prints, the curvature in $a$ itself and the connection in $\epsilon - \mu\eta$; the areal chart and Ruban's T-model on de Sitter space, where the derivative of $a$ along the time is rational in $a$ and `datt_ruban_rates` writes every value by it; and Kruskal's chart outside a T-sphere, the white hole's. `datt_ruban_check` holds the comoving chart to the published comoving chart of `kantowski_sachs`, each explicit chart to dust of density $2\mu/(a\,b^2)$, Ruban's chart at $\mu = 1/2$ to that spacetime's published dust chart, the areal chart to Ruban's carried along its map, to the published inside of Schwarzschild's horizon without dust and to the junction of a T-sphere, and the surface $V = U$ of Kruskal's chart to the dust's; `_tools/test_datt_ruban_t_models.py` holds the published files to the same and to the numbers the texts state, and `datt_ruban_t_models.md` records each chart's source and the factor of 2 in which Ruban's printed solution is corrected.
The four charts of `small_universes` are written by it as well, each Friedmann's line element with the scale factor free and the identifications in its domains: `small_universes_check` holds each to a vanishing Weyl tensor, the two torus charts to the published charts of `frw` at $k = 0$ pulled back and to translations being isometries, the hyperbolic chart to the published comoving chart of `frw` at $k = -1/\ell^2$ carried along $r = \ell\sinh\chi$, and the horn to the Einstein tensor of the open universe and to Sokolov and Starobinskii's map from the spherical form of hyperbolic space, `small_universes_horn_map`; `small_universes_pretty` keeps the factor $a'^2 - 1$ whole. `_tools/test_small_universes.py` holds the published files and the drawings to their captions, and `small_universes.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `kerr_bertotti_robinson` are written by it as well: Podolský and Ovcharenko's chart in the squared lapse $N$, $F$, $W$ and the dragging rate $\omega$ of their observers of no angular momentum, held as functions with their $Q$ and $P$, and the chart of the hole with no spin with $\Omega$, $f$ and $P$ held, its Ricci and Kretschmann scalars stated as functions of the coordinates by `kerr_bertotti_robinson_scalars`. The conicity $C$ is a constant of each chart, so no value spells it out, and `kerr_bertotti_robinson_pretty` writes a cosine beside a dividing sine as a cotangent. `kerr_bertotti_robinson_check` holds each chart to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with their potential at two points in 30 digits, to $CP = 1$ on the axis, the spinning chart to their line element, to Kerr's at $B = 0$ and to the static chart at $a = 0$, and the static chart to Schwarzschild's at $B = 0$, to its horizon and surface gravity, and to a vanishing Weyl tensor at $m = 0$; `kerr_bertotti_robinson.md` records each chart's source.
The four charts of `einstein_1912_static` are written by it as well: the static field with the lapse $N$ free, the uniform field, and the field outside a body by Einstein's equation of February 1912 and by that of March; `einstein_1912_check` holds the first to $R = -2\nabla^2N/N$ and no $G_{tt}$, each other to being it at its own $N$, the uniform field to being flat, and the bodies to their equations and to $\gamma = 0$ with $\beta = 1/2$ and $3/4$; `einstein_1912_static.md` records each chart's source and `_tools/test_einstein_1912_static.py` holds the published files to the bending of light, the perihelion, the declared star and the drawings.
The three charts of `brans_dicke_sphere` are written by it as well: Brans and Dicke's isotropic chart in their $B$, $C$ and $\lambda$, printed around the powers of $h = (\rho - B)/(\rho + B)$ by `named_powers` with an exponent over $\lambda$ written on one line, Campanelli and Lousto's chart in their $r_0$, $m$ and $n$, and Bronnikov's harmonic chart, printed in $\sinh(ku)$, $\cosh(ku)$ and one exponential by `brans_hyperbolic`. `brans_dicke_check` holds each to the vacuum equations of the theory, $R_{\mu\nu} = \omega\,\partial_\mu\phi\,\partial_\nu\phi/\phi^2 + \nabla_\mu\nabla_\nu\phi/\phi$ and $\nabla^\mu\nabla_\mu\phi = 0$, with $\omega$ read off the chart's own parameters, to a vacuum without the field, the isotropic and harmonic charts to being the spherical one pulled back, and the spherical chart times $\phi/\phi_0$ to the published form of `fisher_jnw`; `_tools/test_brans_dicke_sphere.py` holds the published files and drawings to what their texts state, and `brans_dicke_sphere.md` records each chart's source and why the diagrams are drawn at $C = -1/4$.
The chart of `maitra_dust` is written by it too: Maitra's own chart, in the letters of Chan and Santos, with the root $s$ a name the chart defines and his two functions $k$ and $\gamma$ held as functions with their slopes declared in `RATES`, so that every value is a rational function of $r$, $a$, $s$, $k$ and $e^{\gamma}$. `maitra_pretty` writes every even power of $r$ by $r^2 = a^2(s^2 - 1)/4$, which leaves no relation among what is printed, and `maitra_dust_check` holds the chart to dust in free fall with the Ricci scalar for its density, to $g^{tt} = -(1 - k^2/r^2)$ with $k < r$, to a regular axis, and to the published dust of `stockum_dust` at $R = 2a$ through the second order in $r$; `_tools/test_maitra_dust.py` holds the published files and drawings to what their texts state, and `maitra_dust.md` records the chart's sources. Its figure, `projections.maitra_dust`, draws the cones about the axis with no null circle, and `projections.Slice` now writes out a name the checker holds, as `null_rays.Chart` does.
The four charts of `tilted_universes` are written by it too: the homogeneous chart with $X(u)$ and $Y(u)$ free, Farnsworth's dust in the parameter $\eta$, whose values `TiltedParametric` writes in $X$, $X \pm C$ and the hyperbolic functions of $\eta/2$, the flat model, and its inertial chart. `tilted_universes_check` holds the first to the motions of Bianchi's type V and to $\tanh\beta = C/X$, the second to dust of density $3/(WX\sinh^4(\eta/2))$, the third to a vanishing Riemann tensor and to Minkowski space pulled back, and `tilted_universes.md` records each chart's source.
The chart of `bowers_liang` is written by it as well, with $N$, whose power $1/Q$ is $-g_{tt}$, and $P = Z^Q/N$ held as functions of $r$ with the slopes `RATES` declares, so that every value is a rational function of $r$, $R$, $r_s$, $Q$ and $P$ times at most one factor $N^{1/Q}$; its `pretty` writes $r_sr^2$ as $R^3(1 - Z)$ and its `collect` groups a sum by powers of $Z$ and of $1 - Z$, which sets the terms the anisotropy adds apart, and `bowers_liang_check` holds the chart to Bowers and Liang's density, pressure and anisotropy, to Schwarzschild's published exterior at the surface, to Schwarzschild's published interior at $Q = 1/2$ and to Florides's published cluster as $Q \to 0$; `bowers_liang.md` records the source and a slip of the paper's.
The two charts of `kerr_melvin` are written by it as well: Ernst and Wild's chart in Boyer and Lindquist's coordinates of the Kerr seed and the same with an azimuth turning at a constant rate, each written in the squared lapse $N = H\Delta\Sigma/A$, $F = H\Sigma$, $P = A\sin^2\theta/(H\Sigma)$ and the dragging rate $\omega$, which with Kerr's $\Delta$ are held as functions, `HELD`, so that a value is a rational function of the five and their derivatives, with $a$, $m$ and $B$ entering only through $k = 1 + a^2m^2B^4$, which `kerr_melvin_pretty` writes as $k$. `kerr_melvin_check` writes the names out and holds each chart to the Einstein-Maxwell equations with Ernst and Wild's potential at two points of rational coordinates, exactly, $H$ to $k$ at both poles, $\omega$ on the horizon to $a/(2mr_+) + amB^4(r_+ + m)/2$ at every $\theta$, the first chart to the published Boyer-Lindquist chart of `kerr` at $B = 0$ and to the published chart of Ernst's hole in `melvin` at $a = 0$, the second to the first pulled back, and, by `kerr_melvin_traceless`, the Ricci scalar to vanishing identically; `_tools/test_kerr_melvin.py` holds the published files to the same physics, to Wald's charge $2amB$ and to what the drawings' captions state, and `kerr_melvin.md` records each chart's source.
The two charts of `swirling_universe_black_hole` are written by it as well: Astorino, Martelli and Viganò's black hole in Schwarzschild's coordinates with their frame constant $\omega_0$ set to zero, and the swirling universe in the cylindrical chart, each value printed around $1 + j^2r^4\sin^4\theta$ or $1 + j^2\rho^4$ and $g_{tt}$ and $g^{\phi\phi}$ grouped as Gibbons, Mujtaba and Pope group the universe's $g_{tt}$; `swirling_check` holds both to the vacuum equations, the spherical chart at $j = 0$ to the published metric of `schwarzschild` and its Kretschmann scalar to the source's closed form, and the cylindrical chart to the spherical one at $m = 0$ and its Kretschmann scalar to Moreira, Herdeiro and Crispino's, and `swirling_universe_black_hole.md` records each chart's source.
The other charts were written by hand and are not touched by the script.

## A system kept to an order

Hartle and Thorne's exterior of a slowly rotating star solves the vacuum equations through second order in the spin and no further, and Lense and Thirring's field to first order in the mass, so neither can be checked as an exact metric: its exact Ricci tensor is not zero.
`ORDERS` in `verify_metrics.py` names, per system, the order each small parameter counts as and the highest order kept, as `({"a": 1, "q": 2}, 2)` for Hartle and Thorne's chart.
`Reader.truncated` cuts an expression to its Taylor polynomial of that order, `Reader.inverse` builds the inverse metric as a series about the zeroth order, and `Geometry` cuts every tensor after each product, so the whole of Hartle and Thorne's chart takes about a minute where its exact inverse did not finish in ten.
Each comparison is made between the two sides' polynomials, and a published connection or curvature component that carries a term beyond the order is a disagreement, so nothing of higher order is printed as if it were known.
A metric component or an inverse component may stand as the line element writes it, uncut, so that the drawings read a metric and its exact inverse.
A system with no entry in `ORDERS` is checked exactly, as before.
`Orders` in `_tools/test_print_charts.py` holds the mechanism, and `_tools/derivations/hartle_thorne.md` is the worked example.

A post-Newtonian metric is known to a different order in each component, $g_{tt}$ through $m^2$ and the metric of space through $m$, so one order cuts none of its tensors rightly.
Its line of `ORDERS` counts in powers of $v/c$ and names the time coordinate as a third item, as `({"m": 2, "a": 1}, 2, "t")` for the rotating chart of `ppn_metric`, and `Reader.kept_order` then keeps a component of the connection, the Riemann tensor or the Ricci tensor with $n$ time indices $n$ orders further than the order named, an Einstein or Weyl component only where $n$ is odd, the Ricci scalar at the order named and the Kretschmann scalar at twice it.
`Geometry` builds every tensor two orders further, whole, and `PostNewtonian` cuts each component as it hands the tensor over; `geometry_of` gives the checker, `metric_tags.py` and the printer the same geometry.
`quotient_checks` in `null_rays.py` checks the lifted rays of such a chart with $v/c$ a hundredth of what is drawn, where the terms its Christoffel symbols leave out are below the tolerance.
`Orders` in `_tools/test_ppn_metric.py` holds the rule to keeping nothing that a term of the next order in the metric would move, and `_tools/derivations/ppn_metric.md` Step 2 is the worked example.
The small quantities of a post-Newtonian system may be declared functions of the coordinates, as the potentials of `eih_many_bodies` are, `({"U": 2, "chi": 2, "V_1": 3, "V_2": 3, "V_3": 3, "psi": 4}, 2, "t")`: `Reader._scaled` gives each function and each of its derivatives its order, a derivative along the named time one order more each time it is taken, since the bodies move slowly; `Orders` in `_tools/test_eih_many_bodies.py` holds it, and `_tools/derivations/eih_many_bodies.md` Step 2 is the worked example.
A line whose third item is `None` counts declared functions as small with no time slow, every derivative of one at the function's own order, since a perturbation of a Friedmann universe changes as fast in time as in space: the perturbed Friedmann universe keeps its potentials to first order, `({"Phi": 1, "Psi": 1}, 1, None)`, `Orders` in `_tools/test_bardeen_perturbations.py` holds it, and `_tools/derivations/bardeen_perturbations.md` Step 3 is the worked example.
A line of two items counts constants alone and refuses a function, as `Orders` in `_tools/test_eih_many_bodies.py` holds, so a post-Newtonian line that forgot its time is caught.

## The three conventions the checker encodes

All three are things the files do consistently, and only the newest of them say so in their own `convention` field, so they are recorded here and in the script's header as well.

The chart is $x^0 = cT$.
A coordinate carrying dimensions of time is not itself the chart coordinate; $c$ times it is, and every published component is a component in that chart even though the index is printed with the bare name.
Schwarzschild shows it plainly, publishing $g_{tt} = -(1-r_s/r)$ against a line element whose time term is $-(1-r_s/r)c^2dt^2$.
Because the rescaling is linear, a component in the chart is the one taken with the bare coordinate multiplied by $c$ once per upper time index and divided by $c$ once per lower one.

The Ricci tensor is the standard contraction $R_{\mu\nu} = R^\alpha{}_{\mu\alpha\nu}$, on the first lower index, and it carries through to the Einstein tensor and the Ricci scalar.
That is the contraction the signature $(-,+,+,+)$ asks for, settled by the captain on 18 September 2026: the Ricci tensor should be whatever the metric sign convention has.
It is what makes ordinary matter come out with a positive energy density, so FRW publishes $G_{tt} = 3(\dot a^2+k)/a^2$ and the interior Schwarzschild solution $G^t{}_t = -3r_s/R^3 = -8\pi G\rho$.
The collection contracted on the last index before that, which is minus this in every slot, and every entry with a nonzero Ricci tensor was moved over in one pass.
The Weyl tensor is built from the same contraction and always was, because it is defined by removing the traces of Riemann and those traces are Riemann's own.

The dots in a geodesic equation are velocities of that same chart, so a dot on a time coordinate means $d(cT)/d\lambda$ and not $dT/d\lambda$, even though it is printed on the bare letter.
It has to be that one, because the equation is $\ddot x^\mu + \Gamma^\mu{}_{\nu\rho}\dot x^\nu\dot x^\rho = 0$ with the same printed $\Gamma$ the entry lists, and those are chart symbols.
The reading is checkable without sympy: on it every term of every equation carries the dimensions of its left hand side, and on the other reading a term mixing a time velocity with a space velocity comes out wrong by one factor of $c$.
That is what the dimensional pass does, over the geodesics and over every published component alike, and `_tools/derivations/kasner.md` Step 17 works the check through term by term for one entry.
A coordinate's name may carry a subscript, as the Kaluza-Klein monopole's fifth coordinate $x_5$ does, and its dot is written `\dot{x_5}`; a superscript is read as a power, so $x^5$ cannot name a coordinate.

## Adding a spacetime, as far as the checker is concerned

Telling a time coordinate from a length needs the dimensions of the parameters, which live in prose, so the script cannot read it off the file.
`DIMENSIONS` in `verify_metrics.py` declares the dimension of every coordinate and every parameter, per system.
A coordinate declared as a time is exactly a coordinate the chart multiplies by $c$, so that one table answers both which chart a component is in and what each term of it has to carry; there is no second table to keep in step with it.
A new coordinate system that is not listed there is reported `UNCHECKED` rather than guessed at, so adding a spacetime means adding its line, and forgetting to is visible rather than silent.
So is declaring a dimension for a parameter the system does not have, or leaving one out.

A dimension is written in $L$, $T$ and $M$.
Most entries never name a mass, because they fold it into a length such as $r_s = 2GM/c^2$ and quote that; Vaidya keeps $G$ and $m(u)$ explicit, so it declares $[G] = L^3M^{-1}T^{-2}$ and $[m] = M$ and its components balance through the length $Gm/c^2$.
Kerr does the same with a constant $M$, and adds the one declaration a reader cannot guess: its spin parameter $a = J/(Mc)$ is a length, not a dimensionless number, which is what makes $r^2 + a^2\cos^2\theta$ an area.

Some of what the table has to decide is a choice the entry leaves open.
FRW can be read with a dimensionless comoving $r$ and a scale factor carrying the length, or with $r$ a length, $a$ dimensionless and $k$ a curvature; the line element balances either way, and only the published Riemann tensor picks the second.
Where that happens, the table carries a comment saying which reading the entry's own values obey.

A parameter may be a function rather than a constant.
It may also be a name for an expression in the coordinates and the parameters declared before it, as Weyl's chart of the Curzon-Chazy particle declares `R = \sqrt{\rho^2 + z^2}`: every published value that writes the name is read as the expression, its dimension is declared like any other and checked against the definition's, and `_tools/derivations/curzon_chazy.md` is the worked example.
A defined name whose definition holds a declared function of the coordinates, as Szekeres's $E$ holds $S(r)$, $P(r)$ and $Q(r)$, is held as a function of the coordinates it varies with while the tensors are built, so a published value writes its derivatives as $\partial_r E$ and $\partial_p E$, and `Reader.surface` writes the definition out on both sides of each comparison; built with $E$ written out, Szekeres's geometry did not finish in ten minutes, and held it takes seconds.
`a = a(t)` declares a function of one coordinate, which answers to a dot and to a prime, a prime up to the third derivative, as the travelling wave on a string's $A'''$, and `H = H(u,x,y)` declares a function of several, which answers to `\partial`: a published `\partial_x H`, `\partial_x^2 H` or `\partial_x\partial_y H` is read as that partial derivative, and so is one of any higher order.
Every such derivative is taken with respect to the chart coordinate, exactly as the dot and the prime are, so one along a time carries a factor of $1/c$ and one along a length does not.
`_tools/derivations/pp_wave.md` is the worked example, and its Step 15 is where that factor earns its keep.

The reader fixes no highest order.
A curvature is two derivatives of the metric, so it reaches the second derivative of a function only while the line element carries that function undifferentiated, and Tolman-Bondi is the entry where it does not: its $g_{rr}$ is built from $\partial_r R$, so its curvature reaches $\partial_r\partial_t^2R$.
`Reader._declare_partials` therefore declares a partial derivative when a published value names one, at whatever order it is written and in whatever order its factors are spelled, since mixed partials commute.
It still refuses a derivative of anything the system does not declare as a function, or along a coordinate the function is not declared to depend on, so a typo is an error naming the function and the coordinate rather than a new symbol.
`_tools/derivations/tolman_bondi.md` Step 18 is the worked example, and records that the entry's seventy third derivative expressions came back `UNCHECKED` until 22 September 2026.

A Dirac delta is written $\delta(u)$, with its argument, and each prime on it, $\delta'(ct - z)$, is a derivative with respect to that argument; the reader reads it as sympy's `DiracDelta`, so the chain rule through an argument such as $ct - z$ is exact, and the dimensional pass gives it the inverse dimension of its argument and one more for each prime.
A system that declares a parameter $\delta$ of its own, as the cosmic string declares its deficit angle, keeps it, and no delta is read there.
`norm` puts the argument of every logarithm in one factored form, since `expand` spreads $(x^2 + y^2)/\rho_0^2$ into two fractions, whose logarithms would otherwise be two generators.
It factors the argument of every arctangent the same way, and sympy then takes out its sign, so that the flare of Interstellar's wormhole reads $\arctan(2(\ell - a)/\pi M)$ and $\arctan(2a/\pi M - 2\ell/\pi M)$ as one generator and its negative; `_tools/derivations/interstellar_wormhole.md` Step 3 is where that was found.
`_tools/derivations/aichelburg_sexl.md` is the worked example.

A delta on a curved background, where what multiplies it varies across it, is read as the limit of a pulse.
`IMPULSES` in `verify_metrics.py` lists such systems: the reader reads their delta as `Pulse`, a smooth function, so that every tensor is built with no product of it reduced, and `Reader.surface` takes each side of a comparison to the limit with `on_the_shock`.
A term with one factor is reduced exactly, $f(x)\,\delta^{(k)}(x) = \sum_j (-1)^j\binom{k}{j}f^{(j)}(0)\,\delta^{(k-j)}(x)$; a product of deltas goes only when its coefficient vanishes on the shock fast enough that the term has a limit for any pulse even about the shock, as $u\,\delta(u)^2$ has; any other product is left standing, and the comparison fails.
The argument may be a coordinate or a sum of two with a unit coefficient on one, as $\eta - \chi$.
The limit is taken once a value stands whole, since the limit of a product is not the product of the limits, so `chart_printer.Reduced` hands the unreduced tensor back in where the printer raises an index.
The shared convention of such a spacetime says the delta is the limit of a pulse even about the shock.
`_tools/test_hotta_tanaka.py` holds the rules, and `_tools/derivations/hotta_tanaka.md` Step 3 is the worked example.

A metric with a kink, as the domain wall's $(1 - k|z|)^2$, is written with $|z|$ and $\mathrm{sgn}(z)$, which the reader reads as sympy's `Abs` and `sign`.
`norm` writes $|z|$ as $z\,\mathrm{sgn}(z)$, so that `diff` gives $\mathrm{sgn}(z)$ and then $2\delta(z)$, takes $\mathrm{sgn}(z)^2 = 1$ among its relations, and reads $\delta(z)$ times a function continuous at $z = 0$ as that function's value there times $\delta(z)$, stopping on a delta times a function that jumps.
So the curvature of a thin wall is the delta the Israel junction condition asks for.
Its Kretschmann scalar holds the square of that delta, which is no distribution, so it is written $K = 0 \;\text{for}\; z \neq 0$, and `scalar_parts` and `off_support` read it and compare it where $z$ is not zero.
`_tools/derivations/domain_wall.md` is the worked example.
The thin shell wormhole's chart through its throat, with $r = a + |\ell|$, has a curvature that is Schwarzschild's plus such a delta, and `_tools/derivations/thin_shell_wormhole.md` works it through.

A defined name may be held as a function of the coordinates while the tensors are built, by listing it for its system in `HELD` in `verify_metrics.py`, as Tolman's phase $\psi$ is.
The reader then treats it as it treats a defined name whose definition carries a declared function: the geometry is built with $\psi(r)$ and its derivatives, and `Reader.surface` writes the definition out on both sides of each comparison, so the file's own definition is what is checked.
It is for a name that is a logarithm of radicals standing inside a sine or a cosine: `norm` factors a logarithm's argument before it splits the radicals, so a second pass spells the logarithm differently from the first, and a value built from normalised pieces would not cancel against one read from the file.
`_tools/derivations/tolman_vii.md` Steps 4 and 5 are the worked example, and Step 4 records why that chart takes $R$ and $r_s$ for its parameters: a factor under a root has to lead with a positive coefficient, which $1 - 2\beta$ does not.
The reader reads `\arctan`, with its argument in brackets, as it reads `\ln`.
It reads `\mathrm{W}` as Lambert's function on its principal branch, and `norm` writes $e^{k\mathrm{W}(x)}$ as $(x/\mathrm{W}(x))^k$, so that a value which holds it is a rational function of $\mathrm{W}(x)$; Kruskal's chart of the white hole defines its areal radius by it, and `_tools/derivations/white_hole.md` Step 2 is the worked example.
It reads `\mathrm{F}\left(\varphi \mid m\right)` as the incomplete elliptic integral of the first kind with the parameter $m$, `EllipticF` in `verify_metrics.py`: sympy's `elliptic_f` under a name of its own, which differentiates along its amplitude by its integrand, evaluates through mpmath to any number of digits, and carries its own numbers for `lambdify`, scipy's for floats and mpmath's for mpmath's, so that every generator evaluates a published value that holds one. The mass function of `born_infeld_charge` is the one use, held in `HELD` with its slope declared in `RATES`; `_tools/derivations/born_infeld_charge.md` Step 6 is the worked example.
It reads `\mathrm{K}\left(\kappa\right)` and `\mathrm{E}\left(\kappa\right)` as the complete elliptic integrals of the first and second kinds with the parameter $\kappa$, the square of the modulus, `EllipticK` and `EllipticE` beside `EllipticF`: each differentiates into the two again, $d\mathrm{K}/d\kappa = (\mathrm{E} - (1 - \kappa)\mathrm{K})/(2\kappa(1 - \kappa))$ and $d\mathrm{E}/d\kappa = (\mathrm{E} - \mathrm{K})/(2\kappa)$, so a value that holds them is a rational function of the two. Weyl's functions of `bach_weyl_ring` are the one use, held in `HELD` with their first derivatives declared in `RATES` in each of its three charts; `_tools/derivations/bach_weyl_ring.md` is the worked example.
It reads `\arcsin` and `\mathrm{arsinh}` the same way.
A `\partial` binds to the name that follows it together with that name's numeral subscript where the two are a declared function, as `\partial_\xi h_1` is the derivative of Wahlquist's $h_1$; a subscript that belongs to no declared function is left where it stands.
The canonical form writes the root of a radicand that leads with a negative term on its other branch, $\sqrt{1 - k^2\xi^2}$ as $i\sqrt{k\xi - 1}\sqrt{k\xi + 1}$, which is sound in every comparison and the wrong number if evaluated; `_tools/derivations/wahlquist.md` Step 5 says how Wahlquist's chart is printed and checked around it.

A held name may have its first derivatives declared, in `RATES` in `verify_metrics.py`, each written in the held names themselves.
The reader checks each declared derivative against the name's definition, and from then on never writes the name out: every derivative of it, to any order, is written by the declared ones after each product, and each comparison is made between rational functions of the names.
That is sound where the names are functions of the coordinates with no relation among them, as the coordinates of another chart are, and a relation the names did have would show as a disagreement and never as a false agreement.
It is for names that hold a root: Podolský and Beláň's $x = \sqrt{X^2 + Z^2 - c^2T^2}$, $u$ and $v$ on the Kerr-Schild chart of Kundt's waves, which took four minutes with the names written out at the end and takes four seconds; `_tools/derivations/kundt_waves.md` Step 3 is the worked example.

A system that holds names and declares no rates, order, pulse or relation has each comparison made first between the two values as functions of the held names, `agree_held` in `verify_metrics.py`: two values equal in the names are equal for every definition of them, so only a value that differs there is written out.
A definition may hold a name defined before it, as the squared lapse of `kerr_melvin` holds $H$, which holds Kerr's $A$, which holds $\Delta$, so the reader writes each held definition out in full as it reads it, and one substitution of the held names leaves none, in the checker and in every drawing; `Reader.written` writes a value out name by name, each in canonical form, which `metric_tags.py` uses for such a chart, after asking whether the value vanishes at one point of rational coordinates, since one that does not is not zero.
A value stated only for the definitions, as a Ricci scalar written $R = 0$ would be for that chart, is still compared written out and has to come within the budget; `_tools/derivations/kerr_melvin.md` Step 3 is the worked example, where it did not, and the scalar is published in the names.

A parameter with a subscript may be written against the letters before it, as the $b_0$ of $2ab_0^2$: the parser splits a token it does not know letter by letter, which would leave a stray `_0` and multiply the term by zero without a word, so `Reader._part_subscripted` parts the declared name from what stands before it and refuses any other unknown token that holds an underscore.
A defined name may carry a command in its subscript, as Kerr-de Sitter's `\Delta_\theta`, which `Reader` reads whole as it reads `\chi_0`.
`_tools/derivations/teo_wormhole.md` Step 6 is where that was found.

A power of $e$ is read as the exponential, unless the system declares `e` as a name of its own, as the foliation of Ori's time machine declares his parameter $e$: there `4e^2` is four times its square, and a chart of that spacetime with no such parameter writes an exponential as `\exp`.
`_tools/test_reader_names.py` holds both readings, and `_tools/derivations/ori_time_machine.md` Step 4 is where it was found.

A trigonometric or hyperbolic function may carry a negative or a fractional power on its name before a bracketed argument, as `\cosh^{-2/3}(3a\rho)`, which `powered_trig_calls` reads as the power of the call.

The prime works only on a function whose name is not a LaTeX command.
The reader turns a prime into a name suffix before it turns `\Phi` into a name, so `b'` reads as the derivative of `b` while `\Phi'` leaves a stray backslash and is rejected as unhandled LaTeX.
`\partial` works on either kind of name, so an entry with a Greek named function writes every derivative that way; `_tools/derivations/morris_thorne.md` Step 17 says so of its own `\Phi` and `b`.

An entry whose parameters are not free needs a second line, in `PARAMETER_RELATIONS`.
Kasner prints three exponents bound by $\sum_i p_i = \sum_i p_i^2 = 1$ and claims its values only on the surface those two equations cut out; its Ricci tensor is zero there and nowhere else.
Checked against free symbols it would report seventy four disagreements that are not disagreements.
The table carries a rational parametrisation of the surface instead, and every constrained parameter is replaced by its parametrised value on both sides of each comparison, so the identity checked is the one the entry asserts.
That is exact rather than a sample, because a rational parametrisation covers a dense subset of an irreducible variety.
`_tools/derivations/kasner.md` derives the parametrisation the Kasner entry uses and why the constraints are the field equations.
Levi-Civita's Kasner form lies on the same circle, parametrised by its mass parameter, as `_tools/derivations/levi_civita.md` Step 2 derives.
